# Provider watch — 2026-11-23

- Three shipped defaults are dead out of the box, and one wrapper now hands the LLM a destructive tool. `nvidia/stt` is today's: NVIDIA retired the hosted `canary-1b-asr` function, so an unconfigured `NvidiaSegmentedSTTService` answers NOT_FOUND — #6971 moves it to `parakeet-1.1b-rnnt-multilingual`. `sambanova/llm` lost `Meta-Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct` on 2026-11-17: it is absent from `/v1/models` and answers 404, so anyone constructing `SambaNovaLLMService` without a model gets a dead model; the fix is one literal, but `SAMBANOVA_API_KEY` has been rejected for three runs, so the move to `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` cannot be verified. `heygen/video`'s default `service_type=INTERACTIVE_AVATAR` still 404s on `/v1/streaming.new`, and the legacy surface shrank again — `GET /v2/avatars` answered 200 two weeks ago and now returns 410. Separately, `keenable/tool` passes no `tools_filter`, so Keenable's 2026-11-18 Monitors launch puts four off-mission tools, the irreversible `delete_monitor` among them, in front of every keyed user's LLM.

- Three dates land before the next cycle. 2026-11-28: the Pro checkpoint promoted for en/es/hi — `smallest/tts`'s own default configuration — stops emitting word timestamps for `meher` (9 per-word frames against 1 whole-text frame, reproduced through probe.py). 2026-11-30: Smallest retires `lightning_v3.1`, after which `SmallestTTSModel.LIGHTNING_V3_1` builds a request Pro rejects, because `sophia` and four of the five other voices the `word_timestamps` docstring names exist only in the retiring catalogue. 2026-12-01: `gemini-2.5-flash` and `gemini-2.5-pro` start being served by Gemini 3 models while their `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` entries still send a `thinking_budget` those models may ignore or reject (`google/llm`). Close behind: Mist v1/v2 retire on 2026-12-03, which leaves only the Mist v3 users whose de/es/fr requests `rime/tts` breaks with `phonemizeBetweenBrackets`; Tavus drops Phoenix-3 on 2026-12-14 while the release-eval replica is still a Phoenix-3 face (`tavus/video`); and Inception stops serving on 2026-12-28 though `pipecat init` still offers it to new projects (`inception/llm`).

- 24 PRs stand across the date — 22 open, #6640 and #5551 merged — and no branches were left unreviewed. Three fix something broken now and should go first: #6971 (the dead NVIDIA segmented default), #6979 (`together/stt` declares `pcm_s16le_16000` whatever the pipeline's input rate is, a one-property fix now that the STT base class resamples) and #6961 (Burmese reaches Gladia as a code it rejects). Four more are one decision in five places and read best together — #6962, #6965, #6967 and #6973 send the newest model families their lowest thinking or reasoning level across `google/llm`, `google/interactions-llm`, `google/realtime`, `google/vertex-llm` and `openai/responses-llm`.

- The reasoning-default rule that CONTRIBUTING.md sets for every LLM service is the date's widest hole, and this run put numbers on it. `aws/llm` keeps no `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` table at all, so a Bedrock reasoning model runs at the provider's default: `us.openai.gpt-6.2-sol` answers in TTFAT 1111 ms untouched against 737 ms at `effort=low`, with `us.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1` at 3048/1238 ms and `us.xai.grok-4.7` at 9007/5351 ms. `sarvam/llm` (12 weeks) and `together/llm` (11 weeks) ship no table either — out of the box `zai-org/GLM-5.2` spends 2370 ms thinking on a scheduling question against 186 ms with reasoning off. The shared Google table has a hole the open PRs do not close: `gemini-4-carbon` produced no token inside the 20 s stream idle timeout and failed with "LLM completion timeout" where `thinking_level` low answers in 2029 ms, and `gemini-4-hydrogen` and `-neon` have no entry either (`google/interactions-vertex-llm`).

- 16 of 118 units could not probe, and whole providers stayed dark for the second cycle running: all three ElevenLabs units on a key the API rejects with 401, all three Zoom AI units and both Gradium units with no key at all, plus `bland/tts`, `camb/tts` (401), `crusoe/llm` (401), `meta/stt` and `azure/voice_live-realtime`. `fal/stt` is new to the list — every request answered 403 "User is locked. Reason: Exhausted balance", so its auto-detect ErrorFrame could only be re-read in the source. Two block a decision rather than just a probe: `sambanova/llm`, where the 401 is what stops the replacement default being verified, and `azure/realtime`, where `AZURE_REALTIME_BASE_URL` names a host that does not resolve and still carries the retired preview path.

**118 units researched** — 24 PRs, 0 branches, 592 changes to consider, 16 errors, 2 with nothing new.

## Changes to consider

**High**

- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Pipecat offers streaming model IDs AssemblyAI has dropped, and universal-3-5-pro streaming retires 2027-02-01 — the spec's `speech_model` enum is now exactly assemblyai-stt-3-7-realtime-agent / assemblyai-stt-3-7-realtime / universal-3-6-pro; `U3_PRO_MODEL_PREFIXES` still carries `u3-rt-pro` and `universal-3-5-pro`, and the deprecated `AssemblyAIConnectionParams.speech_model` Literal still offers those plus `u3-rt-pro-beta-1`, `universal-streaming-english` and `universal-streaming-multilingual`. The models page gives Universal-3.5 Pro Realtime a hard retirement date of 2027-02-01; the others are undated — *needs a call: dropping values from a public Literal breaks users pinned to a model that still works today, so decide between a deprecation warning now and a removal timed to 2027-02-01*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — No reasoning-defaults table, so a Bedrock reasoning model runs at the provider's default (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — three models now show it: on a reasoning-triggering prompt `us.openai.gpt-6.2-sol` (new this run) answers in TTFAT 1111 ms untouched against 737 ms with `effort=low`, and last run `us.moonshotai.kimi-k3.1` measured 3048/1238 ms and `us.xai.grok-4.7` 9007/5351 ms. The service has the `effort` setting to fix this with but keeps no `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` table, which CONTRIBUTING.md makes the rule for every LLM service. — *needs a call: what a Bedrock table keys on — model ids carry region and vendor prefixes — and what to do for models whose reasoning control is vendor-specific (Claude's `thinking` in `additionalModelRequestFields`) rather than Converse's `effort`*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-23.md) — `invoke_agent_runtime` is called without `runtimeSessionId`, so turns are not pinned to one AgentCore session (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The session guide's instruction is unchanged — generate a unique ID of at least 33 characters per user or conversation, pass the same ID for all related invocations, and "Clients must capture the session ID returned in the response and include it in all subsequent requests"; without one "each request may be routed to a new microVM, which may result in additional latency due to cold starts". The request header accepts 33-256 characters and the response returns the ID, so an echo-back scheme is possible. Two V2 features added this month hang off the same ID: session pinning binds a session to the creating user's identity, and an `s3BucketMount` prefix containing `{runtimeSessionId}` gives each session its own folder. The processor sends neither the ID nor reads the one returned, and the default payload transformer sends only the latest user message, so the agent gets continuity from neither side. — *needs a call: Where the ID comes from — a constructor argument, one generated per conversation, or the ID echoed from the first response — and whether teardown should call StopRuntimeSession.*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-23.md) — Service calls the retired `images/generations:submit` endpoint with api-version 2023-06-01-preview (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `run_image_gen` posts to `{endpoint}openai/images/generations:submit?api-version=2023-06-01-preview` and polls `operation-location`. That api-version retired 2024-07-01 and the submit/poll flow was DALL-E 2's; dall-e-2 retired 2025-01-27 and dall-e-3 on 2026-03-04 with existing deployments non-functional. Every image model Azure documents today (gpt-image-2.5-sunburst / -flare, gpt-image-2, gpt-image-1.5 and gpt-image-1-mini GA; gpt-image-1 retired 2026-10-28 per the schedule) is served synchronously by `POST {endpoint}openai/v1/images/generations` (or the deployment-scoped `images/generations` at api-version 2025-04-01-preview); the how-to page states the call is synchronous and documents no `:submit` or operation-location flow. The request also never sends `model`, so `AzureImageGenSettings.model` is silently ignored, and the response is read as `result.data[0].url` while the whole gpt-image-* family returns `b64_json` only. — *needs a call: Porting to the v1 images endpoint changes the request, the response decoding (base64 to `URLImageRawFrame` or a plain image frame) and the polling loop at once - a behavioral rewrite that needs a maintainer's call on scope and a live Azure image deployment to verify; no AZURE_DALLE_API_KEY is configured here.*
- [azure/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is Deprecated on Azure's schedule (new subscriptions cannot deploy it; retires 2027-04-14) (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Schedule refreshed (ms.date 2026-11-18): gpt-4.1 (2025-04-14) is still Deprecated, retirement 2027-04-14, Replacement column still empty, while gpt-4o points at gpt-5.1 and the o-series at gpt-5.6-sol/gpt-5.6-terra (those retired 2026-11-19). The table now also carries GA rows for gpt-5.5, the gpt-5.6 family and gpt-6/gpt-6.1, none of them named as a replacement for anything. aiewf-eval is unchanged since last run - gpt-4.1 95.9% / 743 ms, gpt-5.6-sol (none) 96.9% / 765 ms, gpt-5.5 (none) 98.2% / 780 ms - so the two candidates still clear the current default on quality inside the 10% latency allowance. gpt-6.2-astra/gpt-6.2-sol went GA in Foundry on 2026-11-17 and are unbenchmarked. The blocker is still the probe: the test resource deploys gpt-4.1 only among these, and gpt-5.6-sol, gpt-5.5 and gpt-6-sol all return 404 DeploymentNotFound, so no Azure-side latency comparison exists. On Azure the model string is a deployment name, so the default only matters for users who name their deployment after the model. — *needs a call: whether to adopt gpt-5.6-sol (or gpt-5.5) as the default on OpenAI-direct benchmark evidence alone, given Azure lists no replacement for gpt-4.1 and the test resource has no candidate deployment to measure latency against*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Example bot and CLI scaffold never set Settings.model, so the URL carries OpenAI's default gpt-realtime-2.1 instead of the Azure deployment (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `examples/realtime/realtime-azure.py:101` and the `azure_realtime` entries in `src/pipecat/cli/registry/_configs.py:680` / `service_metadata.py:1296` build the service with no `model`, so the base class appends `?model=gpt-realtime-2.1` (`openai/realtime/llm.py:341`). On Azure that query parameter is the deployment name, not a model name, so a fresh bot only connects if the user happened to name their deployment `gpt-realtime-2.1`. — *needs a call: which env var should name the deployment (the scaffold's env template emits only `AZURE_REALTIME_API_KEY` and `AZURE_REALTIME_BASE_URL`), and whether the service should refuse to connect when no deployment is named*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-4o-mini is on Foundry's Deprecated lifecycle (new customers blocked), retiring 2027-04-14 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Unchanged this run. The retirement schedule (refreshed 2026-11-18) still marks gpt-4o-mini 2024-07-18 Deprecated, retirement 2027-04-14, Replacement column empty. Voice Live still lists gpt-4o-mini in its supported-models table (Standard tier). Standard-tier successors: gpt-5.6-luna (GA, retires 2028-01-11), gpt-5-mini (GA, 2027-07-13), native-audio gpt-realtime-2.1-mini (GA, 2027-07-31, moved out from 2027-06-25). gpt-6.1-sol is GA to 2028-03-11 but Pro-tier; gpt-6-luna GA to 2028-03-11 but Lite-tier. — *needs a call: Microsoft names no replacement, and the natural successors split two ways: a cascaded text model (gpt-5.6-luna / gpt-5-mini, same Azure-TTS voice behaviour) or a native-audio one (gpt-realtime-2.1-mini, which changes voice handling). Which line the default should follow is a maintainer's call, and no credential was available to probe either.*
- [cartesia/turns-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/turns-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Setting `language_hints` on the default `ink-2` makes the service unusable — Cartesia gates hints to `ink-preview` ("ink-2 doesn't take hints. It returns an `error` event if you pass them"); the service sends `language_hint` query parameters for whatever model is configured, so on `ink-2` the server rejects the handshake with "Model does not support language hints", the receive loop declines to reconnect, and the worker tears the processor down (probe below). The Settings docstring and our docs page only say "Not every model accepts hints", naming no model. — *needs a call: whether the service should reject hints for a model that can't take them at construction, drop them with a warning, or only name `ink-preview` in the docstring*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Deepgram deprecated `diarize` in favour of `diarize_model`, and DeepgramSTTService sends `diarize` unconditionally, so `diarize_model` is rejected (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — `DeepgramSTTService` hardcodes `diarize=False` into its default settings, so every connection carries `diarize=false`. Deepgram rejects a request that sets both parameters: `extra={"diarize_model": "latest"}` fails the handshake with 400 and permanently retires the service (re-probed today on deepgram-sdk 7.17.0). `Settings(diarize=None, extra={"diarize_model": "latest"})` connects, but `None` is outside the field's declared `bool | NotGiven` type. `DeepgramSegmentedSTTService` solved this in 3545e4aea: `diarize` stays the on/off switch, `diarize_model` (default `"latest"`) picks the model, and Deepgram's `diarize` parameter is never sent. The streaming class has no `diarize_model` field and has not followed. Both the streaming and pre-recorded `/v1/listen` references still mark `diarize` "Deprecated. Use `diarize_model` instead.", with no removal date. — *needs a call: whether DeepgramSTTService should adopt the segmented class's mapping, which moves existing `diarize=True` users from Deepgram's oldest diarizer to `latest`*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-23.md) — An `extra`/`addons` key the deepgram-sdk `connect()` signature does not declare retires DeepgramSTTService instead of reaching Deepgram (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — `_build_connect_kwargs` spreads `extra`/`addons` into `client.listen.v1.connect(**kwargs)`, which takes exactly the AsyncAPI query-param list; an undeclared key raises TypeError inside `_connection_handler`, is retried 3x and then permanently retires the service. Probed today: `extra={"redact_usage": true}` does this on the installed 7.17.0 and works on 7.19.0, which declares it. 7.19.0's signature now covers every parameter the streaming reference documents, so the escape hatch is closed only in the window between Deepgram shipping a parameter and the SDK regenerating — and for anyone on an older SDK, which our `>=6.1.1,<8` pin allows. The SDK spreads `request_options["additional_query_parameters"]` into the WebSocket query string, so routing undeclared keys there would restore the hatch. `DeepgramSegmentedSTTService` and `DeepgramSageMakerSTTService` build their own query strings and already accept any key. — *needs a call: a call on whether undeclared keys should be forwarded to Deepgram unvalidated or rejected at construction time instead of failing mid-run*
- [fal/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fal/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Language auto-detect (`settings=Settings(language=None)`) produces no transcript (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — fal documents passing `language: null` as auto-detect, but `run_stt` omits the field entirely when the setting is None, so Wizper falls back to its `en` default; the frame is then built with `Language(self._settings.language)`, which raises `None is not a valid Language` and turns the utterance into an ErrorFrame. Code unchanged since the 2026-11-09 probe reproduced it; not re-probed today (account locked). — *needs a call: a call on what `TranscriptionFrame.language` should carry when fal auto-detects — the response has a `languages` list, not a single code*
- [fireworks/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fireworks/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default `nemotron-3-ultra-nvfp4` is on Fireworks' Serverless retirement list (announced 2026-10-19, effective 2026-11-07) (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the evidence hardened this run - Fireworks' model page for `nemotron-3-ultra-nvfp4` now states "Serverless - Not Supported", the serverless filter of the model library does not list it, and the recommended-models guide no longer names Nemotron for low-latency work. The endpoint nonetheless answers every probe (5/5, median TTFAT 363 ms) and is still returned by `/v1/models`. Fireworks' named replacement remains a dedicated deployment of Nemotron Super VL, which has no serverless model ID. Serverless stand-ins all work - `deepseek-v4p1-flash-1017` (median TTFAT 811 ms), `glm-5p3` (1202 ms), `ember-1` (1116 ms), `muse-spark-open` (2902 ms, 2.5 s of it reasoning by default) — *needs a call: which serverless model becomes the default - Fireworks designates none, `deepseek-v4p1-flash-1017` and `ember-1` have no aiewf-eval row, and glm-5.3's 97.7% row ran at `low` reasoning rather than the Fireworks defaults this service sends*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Fish's WebSocket `error` event is ignored, so a rejected request is silent — probed against the plain `/v1/tts/live` the service uses — a start request with an unknown `reference_id` answers `{event: error, error: "Reference not found"}`; `_receive_messages` handles only `audio` and `finish`, so the reason is dropped, nothing is pushed upstream, and when the socket closes `_receive_task_handler` reconnects with the same settings until retries run out. The same event also reports a concurrency refusal, with `max_concurrency`. Related: `finish` with `reason: "error"` now carries a `message` the service replaces with a fixed string — *needs a call: whether to surface the event with `push_error` and how — fatal or not, and whether a reconnect should still be attempted after a request the server has already refused*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `language_config.languages` is sent verbatim, so a regional `Language` (e.g. `Language.EN_US`) fails session init with a 400 (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `_prepare_settings` calls `language_config.model_dump()` without routing entries through `language_to_gladia_language`, so `Language.EN_US` reaches Gladia as `en-US`. Ad-hoc init POST again this run: `languages: ["en-US"]` → 400, `["en"]` → 201. `_setup_gladia` raises on a non-ok response, so the service cannot start. Gladia's OpenAPI now exposes the accepted set as `TranscriptionLanguageCodeEnum` (99 iso639-1 codes), so the valid values are machine-readable. Our examples happen to pass `Language.EN`, which is already `en`. — *needs a call: whether `language_config.languages` should be converted like other language inputs (and typed to accept `Language`), or validated with a clear error instead*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-23.md) — `mode="SMART"` returns the cumulative session transcript, which the service pushes as a per-turn final — probed two utterances in one session — VERBATIM gave deltas ("The quick brown fox...", then "...makes voice agents easy to build."), SMART gave the first sentence and then both sentences. `_handle_input_transcription` pushes every `input_transcription` as `TranscriptionFrame(finalized=True)`, so an aggregator re-appends everything already said on each turn. Google documents this ("replace your buffer rather than append") and says voice editing is on by default in SMART mode. — *needs a call: whether the service should diff SMART finals against what it already pushed, push a replacing frame, or document `mode="SMART"` as unsuitable for conversational pipelines*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Live sessions end after 10 minutes; the service treats the close as a failure and has no session resumption (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — docs still cap live transcription at 10 minutes per session; `_receive_handler` catches the close, calls `push_error`, and reopens, so every long call sees an ErrorFrame plus a gap in transcripts. `go_away` and `session_resumption_update` are ignored, though `GeminiLiveLLMService` already handles both. — *needs a call: a call on whether STT should reuse the LLM service's session-resumption handling, or just stop reporting an expected expiry as an error*
- [google/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/image/2026-11-23.md) — Default gemini-3.1-flash-image was deprecated on 2026-10-06 in favour of gemini-nano-banana-2.1 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — re-probed today and the gap widened again: 7.6 s vs 12.2 s median wall clock over 3 interleaved rounds (~60%, far past the 10% bar for an automatic bump); both models work unchanged through the class and no shutdown date is announced, so nothing breaks today — *needs a call: whether a ~60% slower generation is an acceptable price for moving off a deprecated default, or whether the faster gemini-3.1-flash-lite-image is the better target*
- [google/interactions-vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — gemini-4-carbon, gemini-4-hydrogen and gemini-4-neon have no _THINKING_DEFAULTS entry, so they run at Google's HIGH/MEDIUM default — All three went GA on Vertex this month and answer through this class, but with no entry the service sends no thinking_level. gemini-4-carbon (default MEDIUM) produced no token inside the 20 s stream idle timeout and failed with "LLM completion timeout"; with thinking_level low it answered in TTFAT 2029 ms. gemini-4-hydrogen (default HIGH) took TTFAT 3381 ms (median of 3) on a river-crossing prompt against 2008 ms with thinking_level low, and 1342 ms for the gemini-3.8-flash default, which has an entry. Google documents LOW as the floor for every Gemini 4 model: MINIMAL and thinking_budget both return 400. _THINKING_DEFAULTS lives in src/pipecat/services/google/utils.py and is shared with GoogleLLMService, GoogleVertexLLMService and GoogleInteractionsLLMService, so one change covers all four units; gemini-4-argon already has its low entry. — *needs a call: which Gemini unit's PR carries the shared table change, and do hydrogen/neon/carbon each get thinking_level low, their documented floor?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — gemini-2.5-flash and gemini-2.5-pro route to Gemini 3 models on 2026-12-01, but their _THINKING_DEFAULTS entries still send a thinking_budget (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Google deprecated both on 2026-11-02 — from 2026-12-01 gemini-2.5-flash is served by gemini-3.5-flash-lite and gemini-2.5-pro by gemini-3.8-flash. The table is keyed by the requested name, so those requests keep carrying thinking_budget 0 / 128 to a model family that takes thinking_level, which the service's own warning says a Gemini 3 model may ignore or reject. — *needs a call: drop the two entries on 2026-12-01, or map the retired names onto the successor's thinking_level so a user who never changes the model keeps thinking off?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The gemini-3.8-flash default is 2-3x slower on Vertex than gemini-3.6-flash (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — Fourth consecutive run showing it. Interleaved medians of 5 on a trivial prompt: 3.8 Flash TTFAT 1523 ms (spread 845-2101 ms) against 598 ms (526-673 ms) for 3.6 Flash. On a reasoning-style prompt: 1797 ms (795-3644 ms) against 702 ms (638-773 ms). thinking_ms is 0 throughout, so this is not reasoning time. The mid-stream stall that tripped stream_idle_timeout_secs once in five last run did not recur: 20/20 probe runs completed this time. The quality case is unchanged and still favours the default - aiewf-eval medium-context has gemini-3.8-flash (low) at 99.9% / 1411 ms P50 against gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1% / 808 ms - and the retirement cited when commit 714573d00 made the bump still does not exist: the deprecations page lists gemini-3.6-flash as `No shutdown date announced`. GoogleLLMService (google/llm) still defaults to gemini-3.6-flash, so the two units disagree. — *needs a call: keep gemini-3.8-flash for its benchmark score and accept first tokens several times slower, go back to gemini-3.6-flash until Google announces a shutdown date, or wait for a gemini-4-hydrogen leaderboard row - and should google/llm move in step?*
- [google/vertex-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `pipecat init` scaffold for Gemini Vertex Live emits a snippet with a stray `),` that fails to parse, and passes a credentials *path* to `credentials=` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — unchanged at 218a7f836 and still the only one of the now 110 entries in `pipecat.cli.registry._configs.SERVICE_CONFIGS` that fails `compile()` (duplicated in `service_metadata.MANUAL_SERVICE_CONFIGS`); it also wires `credentials=os.getenv("GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS")` while the generated `.env` documents that variable as a file path, so the fixed snippet would still raise from `json.loads` — *needs a call: whether to fix only the parens plus `credentials_path=`, or also align the CLI's `GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID`/`GOOGLE_LOCATION`/`GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` names with the `GOOGLE_CLOUD_*` names the examples and docs use and the registry's `GOOGLE_VERTEX` prefix*
- [groq/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Service buffers the whole /audio/speech response instead of consuming Groq's chunked stream (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — still true: `run_tts` calls `create()` and the probe pipeline receives exactly one `TTSAudioRawFrame` at TTFB 996 ms, while the 2026-11-09 run measured the same endpoint delivering `transfer-encoding: chunked` with first bytes at 0.231 s. `with_streaming_response` plus the base class's `_stream_audio_frames_from_iterator(strip_wav_header=True)` would stream and drop the per-chunk `wave.open` decode — *needs a call: rewriting `run_tts` is a behavioral change beyond a value swap, so it needs a maintainer to own the switch to the streaming client and the base-class audio helper*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-23.md) — Default service_type is INTERACTIVE_AVATAR, whose `/v1/streaming.*` endpoints are retired and return 404 (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — re-probed today: `POST /v1/streaming.new` and `/v1/streaming.create_token` still return 404, and HeyGen's legacy surface has shrunk further — `GET /v2/avatars`, which answered 200 two weeks ago, now returns 410 with `{"code": "endpoint_retired", ... "Use GET /v3/avatars instead"}`. A user who omits `service_type` gets `Failed to setup HeyGenClient` and a pipeline with no avatar; the class docstring still advertises the retired `Shawn_Therapist_public` / `v2` default. — *needs a call: whether to flip the default to LIVE_AVATAR now, or deprecate the Interactive Avatar path (api_interactive_avatar.py, ServiceType.INTERACTIVE_AVATAR) on the usual cycle*
- [inception/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inception/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `pipecat init` still offers Inception as an LLM choice for new projects — `ServiceDefinition(value="inception_llm", ...)` in `src/pipecat/cli/registry/service_metadata.py` scaffolds a new bot onto a service deprecated in 1.17.0 whose API stops serving after 2026-12-28; `ServiceDefinition` has no deprecated flag, so the entry cannot be kept with a warning next to it — *needs a call: whether the registry entry comes out now, ahead of the service itself, or waits for the service's removal*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Default `openai/gpt-4.1-mini` is shut down by OpenAI on 2027-01-14 and no successor is both benchmarked and fast (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — OpenAI's deprecation page schedules `gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14` for 2027-01-14 with `gpt-6-luna` as the replacement, and Inworld's router drops a model once upstream retires it. Re-measured this run through the realtime API; median TTFAT is 692 ms for `openai/gpt-4.1-mini` (5 turns), 868 ms for `openai/gpt-6-luna` (3 turns, +25%), 604 ms for `openai/gpt-6-luna:fast` (5 turns, 13% faster than the default). The `:fast` figure contradicts last run's +19% and is now the better of the two on latency, but aiewf-eval has no row for it. aiewf-eval favours plain `gpt-6-luna (low)` on quality, 91.8% against `gpt-4.1-mini` 81.8%, and the branch on this run makes `low` effort selectable. `examples/realtime/realtime-inworld-locally-driven-turns.py` pins the same model string — *needs a call: whether to take `openai/gpt-6-luna` and accept ~25% more first-token latency for the benchmarked quality gain, or take `openai/gpt-6-luna:fast` on measured latency without a benchmark row*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — A text-only session pushes no text — `response.output_text.delta` has no event model, so `output_modalities=["text"]` produces silence (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — re-confirmed on the wire this run — a text-only session sent `response.output_text.delta` ×7 and `.done`, and zero `response.output_audio_transcript.delta`. `_server_event_types` in `events.py` registers neither type, so `parse_server_event` returns `None` and the receive loop logs "ignoring unhandled server event"; `_push_output_transcript_text_frames` is only reached from the audio-transcript branch, so no `LLMTextFrame` or `TTSTextFrame` is pushed at all. `output_modalities` is a user-settable `SessionProperties` field that `_create_response` forwards to `response.create` — *needs a call: whether a text-only session should push `TTSTextFrame` as well as `LLMTextFrame` (the audio path pushes both, but output aggregation in a session with no audio may not want them)*
- [keenable/tool](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/keenable/tool/2026-11-23.md) — Keyed users now get Keenable's four Monitors tools advertised to the LLM, `delete_monitor` among them — Keenable added `create_monitor`, `list_monitors`, `get_monitor_results` and `delete_monitor` on 2026-11-18, listed only for a request carrying `X-API-Key` or an OAuth sign-in; `KeenableWebSearch` passes no `tools_filter`, so `tools()` hands all six schemas to the context, and `examples/features/features-keenable-web-search.py` passes `KEENABLE_API_KEY` when set. `delete_monitor` deletes a monitor and its results with no undo. `MCPClient` already takes `tools_filter`, so the fix is naming the two web tools again; it reverses e412d5481, whose premise was that anything Keenable added would still serve web search — *needs a call: whether the wrapper should go back to a fixed two-tool allowlist now that the server hosts non-search tools, or expose the Monitors tools deliberately*

It transcribes correctly through MistralSTTService unchanged. A bump also needs MISTRAL_TTFS_P99 (1.89 s) re-measured. — *needs a call: whether to wait for realtime 3 to reach GA and get faster, or bump early and accept the latency, given the 2027-04-30 retirement*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Default voxtral-mini-tts-2603 is deprecated (2026-10-31, retires 2027-04-30); Mistral names voxtral-mini-tts-3-0 as its alternative (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Voxtral TTS 3 runs through MistralTTSService unchanged. On the saved cloned voice both models accept, this run measured TTS 3 at 570 ms vs 2603's 657 ms (round 1) and 515 ms vs 503 ms (round 2) — parity or better, where 2026-11-09 measured it 14-26% slower on the same voice. The shipped default also performs no expression tags: the Voxtral 3 expression adapter only engages for `voxtral-mini-tts-3*` models. — *needs a call: whether to bump now, given this run's medians put TTS 3 at parity but the previous run's put it well behind on the same voice*
- [novita/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/novita/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default moonshotai/kimi-k2.5 is retired upstream (Moonshot 2026-08-31) and still served only by hosts like Novita (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Moonshot still heads its model list with kimi-k3.1-pro and marks kimi-k2.5 flat Deprecated with no date; Novita serves kimi-k2.5 with no sunset notice. Our probe now has kimi-k2.5 at TTFAT 900 ms (median of 4/5 runs; one run failed outright, and a separate repeat saw a 9730 ms outlier) against 1899 ms for kimi-k3.1-pro with thinking on and 1498 ms with thinking off. kimi-k3.1-pro is roughly a third faster than last run (2997 ms) but still slower than the default. aiewf-eval still has no row for kimi-k2.5, kimi-k3 or kimi-k3.1-pro, so the required pass-rate comparison cannot be made — *needs a call: a call on which successor to adopt while aiewf-eval has benchmarked none of them, and whether the deprecated default's new failure/outlier behavior now outweighs the candidate's worse median latency*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is scheduled for shutdown on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — deprecations table unchanged — GPT-4.1 (gpt-4.1-2025-04-14) → gpt-6-luna. The candidate set narrowed rather than grew: GPT-6.2 Astra and Sol arrived 2026-11-17 as the new flagship tier but reject function tools on /v1/chat/completions, so they are not usable defaults for this class at all. gpt-6-luna is still below the default on aiewf-eval medium-context (91.8% / 917 ms at low vs gpt-4.1 95.9% / 743 ms, `none` unranked at 0 of 30), and the leaderboard still has no gpt-6.1-terra, gpt-6.1-sol, gpt-6-sol, gpt-6-astra or gpt-6.2 row. Latency re-measured here: gpt-4.1 459 ms, gpt-6.2-sol 1053 ms, gpt-6.2-astra 1481 ms median TTFAT. The same decision settles the gpt-4.1, gpt-4.1-mini, gpt-4o and gpt-4o-mini rows in evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml and the gpt-4.1 pins in the flows examples, which go on the same date. — *needs a call: which model replaces gpt-4.1 before 2027-01-14 — OpenAI's named gpt-6-luna at a lower pass rate, the unbenchmarked gpt-6.1-terra, or gpt-5.6-terra from the generation OpenAI is retiring tier by tier*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `temperature` and `max_tokens` Settings make every current OpenAI model reject the request with a 400 (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — re-probed on gpt-6.2-sol: temperature=0.7 returns `unsupported_value` (only the default 1 is supported) and max_tokens=200 returns `unsupported_parameter` (use max_completion_tokens). Both are inherited LLMSettings fields that build_chat_completion_params sends verbatim, both 400s are classified permanent, so the worker marks the service unusable for the session. #6565 exempts only content_filter 400s. — *needs a call: a stance on per-model parameter handling — drop temperature and map max_tokens to max_completion_tokens for reasoning models, or fail with a clearer message*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — A session configured with an MCP tool hangs — conversation.item.added carries an item type ConversationItem rejects (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `SessionProperties.tools` accepts provider-native dicts, so OpenAI's `{"type": "mcp", ...}` reaches the server, the server answers with a `conversation.item.added` whose item is `mcp_list_tools`, and `ConversationItem.type` is still `Literal["message", "function_call", "function_call_output"]`. GA's `ConversationItem` union has nine members — the three message shapes, `function_call`, `function_call_output`, `mcp_list_tools`, `mcp_call`, `mcp_approval_request`, `mcp_approval_response`. Unmodeled event *types* are skipped (`parse_server_event` returns None), so the `mcp_list_tools.*` / `response.mcp_call*` events no longer stop the receive loop; a modeled event failing validation still raises out of `_receive_task_handler`, which has no try/except, so the session goes quiet with no ErrorFrame — *needs a call: whether to model the MCP item types (and surface approval requests) or to make the receive loop log and skip any event that fails validation, not just unmodeled types*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-4.1 is deprecated and leaves the API on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — OpenAI's 2026-10-14 deprecation still names gpt-6-luna as the replacement, and gpt-6-luna still fails the quality gate — aiewf-eval medium-context ranks it 91.8% against gpt-4.1's 95.9%, and at effort="none" it completed 0 of 30 conversations. The leaderboard is unchanged again (last touched 2026-10-19): among benchmarked models only gpt-5.6-sol at effort="none" clears gpt-4.1 (96.9% vs 95.9%, TTFAT P50 765 ms vs 743 ms), and OpenAI positions it as the successor to gpt-5 and o3, not to gpt-4.1. The two GPT-6.2 models released on 2026-11-17 have no leaderboard row either, and neither do gpt-6.1-terra and gpt-6.1-sol. Five examples under examples/flows/yaml pin gpt-4.1 explicitly and examples/flows/python/utils.py names it twice as a default_model — *needs a call: which model replaces gpt-4.1 before 2027-01-14 — OpenAI's choice (gpt-6-luna) or the one that benchmarks at parity (gpt-5.6-sol); the same gpt-4.1 default is shared with openai/llm*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — OpenAIRealtimeSTTService treats input_audio_buffer_commit_empty as fatal, so a commit with under 100 ms of audio tears down and reconnects the session (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — re-confirmed unchanged (stt.py untouched since 2026-10-21). An ad-hoc client on a fully configured session (session.updated received, turn_detection null, no VAD events) appended 60 ms and committed, and the server answered input_audio_buffer_commit_empty / invalid_request_error; _handle_error re-raises, so the receive loop drops the socket and reconnects. Any short VAD segment reaches this. The same rejection is reached a second way at session start: probe.py bursts the whole clip, part of it lands before session.updated (87 ms after session.created this run, 75-94 ms last run), gets the session's default turn detection and a server auto-commit, and our commit then finds an empty buffer. The visible result is lost audio, and it is flaky rather than absolute: this run gpt-realtime-whisper returned a full transcript on 1 of 4 probe runs, a truncated tail on 2, and no final transcript on 1, against 0/5 with no transcript on 2026-11-09 and 4/5 truncated on 2026-10-28 — *needs a call: whether an empty-buffer commit should be an error frame and a reconnect at all, rather than a logged no-op, and whether run_stt should also hold audio until _session_ready*
- [openai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Every /v1/audio/speech model is deprecated with a 2027-01-06 shutdown and the named replacement is a Realtime model (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — the deprecation table still lists `tts-1`, `tts-1-hd`, `gpt-4o-mini-tts` and its two snapshots, all shutting down 2027-01-06, all replaced by `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`, which the TTS guide says is not supported on /v1/audio/speech. The alias still serves (TTFB 991 ms median of 3) and the endpoint itself carries no deprecation — *needs a call: whether OpenAITTSService gets deprecated in favour of the Realtime service, or we keep it serving until 2027-01-06 and wait for OpenAI to offer a speech-API successor*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default openai/gpt-4.1 is scheduled for OpenAI shutdown on 2027-01-14 (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — OpenAI's deprecations page still carries the 2026-10-14 entry removing gpt-4.1 from the API on 2027-01-14 with gpt-6-luna as the replacement. OpenRouter serves the slug from OpenAI and Azure (two endpoints this run, down from three, both at 100% uptime, no sunset notice), so the slug itself is unlikely to go dark on that date. gpt-6-luna remains a regression on both bump criteria -- aiewf-eval 91.8% against gpt-4.1's 95.9%, and 867 ms against 752 ms median TTFAT in this run's probe. Models scoring above gpt-4.1 (gpt-5.6-terra medium 99.9%, gpt-5.5 none 98.2%, gpt-5.4 low 97.7%, gpt-5.6-sol none 96.9%) are not positioned as gpt-4.1's successor — *needs a call: which successor to take, given the designated one regresses on aiewf-eval and probes slower, and whether this wrapper should track OpenAILLMService's default rather than move on its own*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-23.md) — evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml calls qwen-turbo, which Alibaba decommissioned on 2026-10-10 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — the id no longer resolves at all — the probe now returns HTTP 404 "Model not exist", where the previous run saw HTTP 429 and the run before that a normal completion. Alibaba's qwen-turbo doc page no longer mentions the model, and the model overview's recommended text lineup is qwen4-max / qwen3.7-plus / qwen3.8-flash. Alibaba names no single successor; qwen-flash is the surviving alias at the same tier — *needs a call: whether the turn-completion row should be dropped or repointed, and to which model — swapping a benchmark row changes what the corpus measures*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Resemble no longer sells voice generation; the TTS docs are an archive and new customers are not accepted (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — unchanged this run - the CEO's 2026-08-13 post still stands, docs.resemble.ai's index lists only Detect products plus a single "Voice AI Research Archive" entry, and every voice page is reachable only through it ("retained for historical reference", "does not imply continued availability of commercial voice services"); the hosted WebSocket still synthesizes for an existing key (probe below) and no shutdown date is published — *needs a call: a stance on a service whose provider has left the market - deprecate it, note the closed signup in our docs and the two examples, or leave it running for existing customers*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Mist v3 rejects `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` for de/es/fr with HTTP 400, and the services send it regardless of language (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Probed live: `lang=de` plus `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` returns 400 `invalid_input`, `lang=en` returns audio. `RimeTTSService.PRONOUNCE()` sets the field implicitly, so a German or French Mist v3 bot loses the turn with no hint from the service. Mist v1/v2 retire 2026-12-03, so the remaining Mist users are Mist v3 users. — *needs a call: a call on whether the services should drop or warn about model- and language-specific fields rather than pass the user's setting through*
- [sambanova/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sambanova/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default Meta-Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct was retired on 11/17/2026 and no longer exists on SambaCloud — `GET /v1/models/Meta-Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct` answers 404 "Model not found" and the model is absent from `/v1/models`; SambaNova's deprecations page gives removal date 11/17/2026 with recommended replacement MiniMax-M3.1-Flash. Any user constructing `SambaNovaLLMService` without a model gets a dead model. The fix is one literal in `llm.py` plus its docstring. — *needs a call: the bump cannot be verified — SAMBANOVA_API_KEY has been rejected with 401 for three runs, so no live turn against MiniMax-M3.1-Flash was possible, and aiewf-eval has no row for it or for the outgoing default. Separately, SambaNova now labels its entire catalogue Preview, so picking any default means overriding the rule that preview models are never defaults.*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Reasoning cannot be turned off on the default sarvam-105b through Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The service ships no reasoning-defaults table, so every model runs at Sarvam's own default. Measured today on sarvam-105b: 1456 ms median TTFAT on a trivial prompt (352 ms thinking) and 3196 ms on a reasoning prompt (2439 ms thinking), against 681/687 ms for sarvam-105b-conversations, which never reasons. The /v2 reference documents the lowest value per model (`none` on the DeepSeek models and `qwen38-27b`, `low` elsewhere), which CONTRIBUTING's "Reasoning Defaults for LLM Services" asks the service to send. For sarvam-105b both references give the off-switch as an explicit null `reasoning_effort`, and `None` means do-not-send in our code, so that null cannot be expressed. — *needs a call: whether to give Sarvam a `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` table keyed by model (the base `LLMService._apply_thinking_default` already exists) and how to express the documented explicit null that `reasoning_effort=None` currently swallows*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — LANGUAGE_MAP does not match Pulse's streaming language set (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — 15 mapped codes (bg, cs, da, et, fi, hu, lt, lv, mt, pa, pl, ro, sk, sv, uk) are pre-recorded-only or undocumented on streaming; `language=pl` fails the handshake and surfaces as an opaque reconnection error. The four streaming East Asian codes (zh, yue, ja, ko) and `hi-dev` are absent, so they only reach the API through resolve_language's unverified fallback. The streaming table now has 26 entries and four aggregators - `north_indic`, `multi-asian`, `multi-south-indic` and the new `indic` (all ten Indian languages in one stream, India region only), which are the only way to get Pulse's streaming language detection and are reachable only by passing a raw string rather than a Language. East Asian codes and multi-asian are served only from `wss://api.us.smallest.ai`; hi-dev, ta, te, kn, ml, multi-south-indic and indic are India-region only and return LANGUAGE_NOT_ENABLED_IN_REGION elsewhere. — *needs a call: trimming the map is a breaking change for anyone passing those codes today; decide between removing them, raising a clear error, or documenting the streaming/batch and region split*
- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-23.md) — lightning_v3.1 retires 2026-11-30 while SmallestTTSModel.LIGHTNING_V3_1 and its sophia default voice remain (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Seven days out. Smallest retires `lightning_v3.1` on 2026-11-30 18:30 UTC; after that the model value is served by `lightning_v3.1_pro`, whose voice catalogue is disjoint. `sophia`, the enum member's default voice, is in the v3.1 catalogue only (re-confirmed against the public get_voices endpoints: 275 Pro voices, 240 v3.1), as are four of the five other voices the `word_timestamps` docstring names (`devansh`, `kartik`, `maithili`, `liam`, `avery`); only `meher` is a Pro voice. So from 2026-11-30 the enum member builds a request Pro rejects. The shipped default model is unaffected. — *needs a call: whether to deprecate the enum member now (sarvam's `warn_deprecated` pattern needs a since-version) or drop it after retirement, and what the docstring should say about word-timestamp voices once only `meher` survives*
- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-23.md) — the Pro checkpoint promoted on 2026-11-28 emits no word timestamps for meher (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Five days out, and reproduced through probe.py this run rather than an ad-hoc socket: `lightning_v3.1_pro` returned 9 per-word TTSTextFrames, `lightning_v3.1_pro_10_26` returned 1 whole-text frame, same sentence and same `meher` voice. On 2026-11-28 that checkpoint becomes the stable `lightning_v3.1_pro` for en/es/hi and for requests omitting language — exactly this service's default configuration — while the Pro model card still states word events emit on the base-queue English and Hindi voices. Nothing breaks: `_apply_force_complete` already covers providers that drop word events. — *needs a call: whether to raise the contradiction with Smallest before 2026-11-28 or document the loss of word-aligned text for English, Spanish and Hindi*
- [soniox/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/tts/2026-11-23.md) — An eagerly opened stream is terminated with 408 `request_timeout` when the turn's first sentence takes more than ~5 s — `on_turn_context_created` sends the per-stream config on `LLMFullResponseStartFrame`, but Soniox terminates a configured stream that receives no `text` within ~5 s; measured at 5.1 s and 5.2 s on a bare WebSocket, with and without keepalives, which the docs confirm do not hold a stream open. A pipeline probe that delayed the first text by 8 s got `Soniox TTS error 408 request_timeout`, pushed upstream as a non-fatal ErrorFrame (category UNKNOWN) and logged as a worker-level failure; the turn then spoke normally, because the audio context is created only when text arrives and the stream re-opens under the same stream_id. — *needs a call: whether a `request_timeout` on a stream that never received text should be swallowed (it is the expected outcome of pre-opening) or the stream should stop being pre-opened before text exists*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Setting `punctuation_overrides` makes the Agent STT session fail permanently (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — `_build_config` still passes `punctuation_overrides` on the Agent STT `TranscriptionConfig`, and the endpoint still rejects the session — re-probed this run, now worded `transcription_config: additional properties 'punctuation_overrides' not allowed`. The service classes `protocol_error` as permanent, so the processor is marked unusable and no transcript ever arrives. The field is absent from the Agent STT WebSocket schema (it reaches our config only because the SDK shares `TranscriptionConfig` with Realtime) and from Speechmatics' own Pipecat page; only docs.pipecat.ai still presents it as a working setting. Unset by default, so only users who set it are hit. — *needs a call: dropping the field contradicts the deliberate vendor commit that kept it on Agent STT, so someone has to decide between deprecating it and waiting for the proxy to accept it*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-23.md) — A plain string for `turn_detection_mode` crashes the service at StartFrame (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — `_handle_turn_detection_mode` still does `mode.value` (stt.py:192), so `Settings(turn_detection_mode="vad")` raises `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'value'` while processing StartFrame and the session never opens; re-probed this run, unchanged. `TurnDetectionMode` is a `StrEnum` and `Settings` is a plain dataclass that coerces nothing, so the string form is easy to reach — the vendor's own Pipecat page passes `turn_detection_mode` through `Settings`. `AgentTurnDetectionMode(mode)` accepts both the member and the string. — *needs a call: whether enum-typed Settings fields should coerce strings across the codebase, or whether the annotation is the contract and the right fix is a clear error instead*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-23.md) — Phoenix-3 faces stop working on 2026-12-14 and nothing in Pipecat flags the face generation a replica needs — Tavus's 11-19 changelog says "Phoenix-3 will be fully deprecated on December 14, 2026". Nothing in the repo hard-codes a replica, so no source change makes a Phoenix-3 face work: `replica_id` comes from `TAVUS_REPLICA_ID` in `env.example`, the `video-avatar/video-avatar-tavus-video-service.py` example and the release-eval manifest entry. `GET /v2/replicas/{TAVUS_REPLICA_ID}` in this environment returns `model_name: phoenix-3`, so that eval and any user on a legacy stock replica break on the date; conversations with it still return 200 today. — *needs a call: whether to move the Tavus eval/CI replica to a Phoenix-4.5 face and say in the service docstring and example which face generations Tavus still serves*
- [together/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The shipped default reasons before every answer, costing seconds before the first spoken token (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — out of the box `zai-org/GLM-5.2` spent 2370 ms thinking on a scheduling question (TTFAT 2626 ms, median of 3, spread 2039–6832 ms) against 186 ms with `reasoning={"enabled": false}`; `LLMService._apply_thinking_default` is the framework hook the Anthropic, Google, OpenAI Responses and Perplexity services use for exactly this, and `DeepSeekLLMService` ships `thinking` disabled outright; both Settings fields a Together table would need (`reasoning`, `reasoning_effort`) already ship — *needs a call: whether TogetherLLMService should send GLM its reasoning-off default, as DeepSeekLLMService does for its V4 models and as #5446 did for Sonnet 5 and later*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — A user-supplied `AudioOutput` without a `format` is never backfilled, so `_get_output_sample_rate()` raises on every assistant audio delta (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — `_ensure_audio_config` replaces a wholly missing `audio.input` / `audio.output` but never fills a missing `format` inside one, so `AudioOutput(speed=1.2)` — the documented way to slow the bot down — leaves `format=None` and the service raises RuntimeError at llm.py:394 for every `response.output_audio.delta`; the same shape on input makes `_get_configured_sample_rate("input")` return None, which also skips the audio-config rescue in `_update_settings` — *needs a call: a call on whether the missing `format` should be backfilled from the transport's sample rate (the fix changes what a today-broken config does) or the service should reject the incomplete config up front*
- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Audio pushed before xAI's `transcript.created` is discarded, not buffered (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `run_stt` drops every frame while `_session_ready` is clear (~250ms after connect, and again after each automatic reconnect). probe.py run fails again this run — all 354 audio frames discarded, no TranscriptionFrame. A raw socket bursting the same clip after `transcript.created` transcribes it correctly, so the burst rate is not the problem. Silent: no warning, no error. — *needs a call: a call on the fix — buffer the pre-ready audio and flush it on `transcript.created`, or hold the service's start until the session is ready; both change startup behavior*

**Medium**

- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default claude-sonnet-4-6 may be superseded by claude-sonnet-5-5 (GA 2026-09-28) (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — Sonnet 5.5 is Anthropic's newest Sonnet and the replacement it names for the Sonnet 4.5 that retires on 2026-11-30. Latency through our class was measured on 2026-10-28 and is carried forward: TTFAT 462 ms against 777 ms trivial, 466 ms against 921 ms on a scheduling prompt, medians of 5. The leaderboard is unchanged for the third run: `claude-sonnet-5-5 (low)` 99.9% / 838 ms, and `claude-sonnet-4-6` is still only in the archived rows at 100.0% / 850 ms. — *needs a call: whether 99.9% against an archived, differently-judged 100.0% counts as parity, and whether to pair the bump with an effort level, since neither leaderboard row matches what Pipecat sends (between_tools at the default high effort)*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Replayed thinking blocks fail Anthropic's preserved-thinking prefix check (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — On Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 the check binds every account; on Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 it binds accounts created on or after 2026-08-31. The adapter round-trips thinking blocks with their signatures, and Pipecat is not append-only: `LLMContextSummarizer._apply_summary` calls `set_messages` to replace history, and `_should_inject_trailing_user_message` injects a "." user turn that never enters the context. The escape hatch, `thinking.block_binding.prefix_mismatch_behavior: "drop_block"` behind the `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01` beta, is out of reach in the streaming path, which overwrites any `betas` set through `extra` (see the `betas` item below). — *needs a call: whether Pipecat should opt into `drop_block` for Anthropic by default, expose the field, or make the summarizer and the trailing-user injection thinking-aware*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The anthropic extra pins <2, excluding the SDK major that Anthropic now ships (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `pyproject.toml` pins `anthropic>=0.49.0,<2`; the latest release is 2.13.0 (2026-11-23). SDK 2.0.0 sends API version `2026-10-21`, which removes `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` for every model — a 400 even at their default values — and drops them from the SDK types. `_apply_sampling_settings` sends them through `extra_body`, so they would reach the wire and fail on every model rather than only on Opus 4.7 and later. The 2.x minors since add error codes, Admin API surface and Bedrock fixes, nothing the service needs. — *needs a call: whether to raise the ceiling to admit 2.x and make the sampling settings version-aware, or hold the pin until the sampling question below is settled*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Pipecat does not pick a low-latency effort level on Sonnet 4.6 (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` sends `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` on Sonnet 4.6, but effort is a separate control and the request omits it, so it runs at Anthropic's `high`. The effort page is unchanged: users are told to set effort explicitly on Sonnet 4.6 "to avoid unexpected latency", `medium` is the recommended default and `low` the latency-sensitive one. — *needs a call: whether the service should choose an effort level the way `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` already chooses a thinking mode, which changes output quality as well as latency*
- [anthropic/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/anthropic/llm/2026-11-23.md) — A `betas` list set through `extra` is discarded on the streaming path — In the streaming path `params.update({"betas": ["interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14"]})` (`llm.py:626`) runs after `params.update(self._settings.extra)` (`llm.py:615`), so a user's `betas` is overwritten and no other beta header can reach a conversation turn. `run_inference` sets `betas` in the request dict before merging `extra` (`llm.py:514`), so there the user wins — the two paths disagree. This is what puts fast mode on Opus 5 and Opus 4.8, and the preserved-thinking `block_binding` controls, out of reach. — *needs a call: whether `extra` should win over the service's own betas or merge with them, since the interleaved-thinking beta is load-bearing for thinking between tool calls*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — mode="manual", AssemblyAI's mechanism for clients that own turn detection, is unreachable and unused — the turn-detection page recommends `mode=manual` + `ForceEndpoint` for exactly what `vad_force_turn_endpoint=True` does, with `max_turn_silence` left as a safety net (10000 ms default in manual); the `mode` Settings Literal lists only min_latency/balanced/max_accuracy, and `_configure_pipecat_turn_mode` forces `min_turn_silence = max_turn_silence = 100`, which would end turns before ForceEndpoint anyway. Manual is agent-model-only and falls back to the speaker-label defaults when `speaker_labels` is on — *needs a call: using manual properly means Pipecat mode stops pinning max_turn_silence to 100 ms, a turn-taking behavior change on the default path — confirm the team wants it before widening the Literal*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The new default is slower to a final transcript than universal-3-6-pro in Pipecat's forced-endpoint mode — probe medians of 5 interleaved runs, speech end to final transcript: assemblyai-stt-3-7-realtime-agent 811 ms (784-930) against universal-3-6-pro 555 ms (288-678). AssemblyAI publishes the reverse for the pair (final transcript 86 ms after speech ends vs 172 ms; Cekura time-to-final 47 ms vs 91 ms), so the gap looks like a configuration interaction with min_turn_silence = max_turn_silence = 100 rather than the model — *needs a call: this contradicts AssemblyAI's own benchmark, so someone has to decide whether to re-tune Pipecat mode (see the manual-mode item) or take it up with AssemblyAI before the default is questioned*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — assemblyai-stt-3-7-realtime is gated out of features AssemblyAI documents it as having — `is_u3_pro_model()` returns False for it, so `prompt` raises ValueError at construction and language_codes / agent_context / previous_context_n_turns / continuous_partials are dropped, though the models page gives it key terms, a 400-character prompt and context carryover; `vad_force_turn_endpoint=False` also raises for it, and `_configure_pipecat_turn_mode` applies the legacy universal-streaming values (end_of_turn_confidence_threshold=1.0, min_turn_silence=160, below the model's 320 ms floor). It is the model AssemblyAI recommends for diarization, which our speaker-diarization example drives with vad_force_turn_endpoint=False — *needs a call: voice_focus and interruption_delay genuinely do not apply to this model, and its exclusion from U3_PRO_MODEL_PREFIXES was deliberate, so this needs a finer per-feature gate rather than adding the prefix*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Server Error messages and other unparsed message types become "Unknown message type" ErrorFrames (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — AssemblyAI documents an `Error` frame (`type`, `error_code`, `error`) sent immediately before close, with the close reason always "See Error message for details" — so the close code carries nothing; `_parse_message` knows Begin/Turn/SpeechStarted/Termination/SpeakerRevision and raises on everything else, so Error, Heartbeat and LLMGatewayResponse all land in push_error as "Unknown error occurred" — *needs a call: the fix is a new message model plus a handler, so decide what an Error becomes downstream — in particular whether 1008 (authorization or account) should be a fatal error rather than a recoverable one*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — A model ID or parameter the server does not recognize is silently ignored, and nothing checks what actually ran (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — the spec still tells clients "Always check that `model` matches the `speech_model` you requested — unrecognized or misspelled query parameters are ignored rather than rejected", and `Begin.configuration` now echoes model, mode, api_version, speaker_labels, redact_pii, filter_profanity, domain and voice_focus; `BeginMessage` parses only `id` and `expires_at`, so the echo is discarded — *needs a call: warning when the applied configuration differs from the requested one is new behavior on the connect path — decide whether to log it and at what level*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Streaming PII redaction (redact_pii, redact_pii_policies, redact_pii_sub) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — three connect params plus `include_partial_turns`, which the server forces to false when redaction is on — *needs a call: redaction suppresses partial turns, so a stance is needed on what the service does with interim transcriptions when a user enables it*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Only agent_context and language_codes are applied live; every other setting change tears down the websocket (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — UpdateConfiguration accepts prompt, keyterms_prompt, min_turn_silence, max_turn_silence, vad_threshold, interruption_delay, mode, language_codes, session_heartbeat and agent_context; HOT_UPDATABLE_SETTINGS lists two of the ten — *needs a call: widening the set changes reconnect behavior mid-call, and Pipecat turn mode rewrites min/max_turn_silence — needs a call on which fields are safe to hot-update*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Connect params inactivity_timeout, include_partial_turns, session_heartbeat and speaker_labels_revision_interval_ms have no Settings fields (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — each would be a one-line pass-through in `_build_ws_url`, but all four change session or frame behavior; inactivity_timeout also brings in the client-side KeepAlive message the service never sends, and speaker_labels_revision_interval_ms is documented only for universal-3-6-pro (the 3.7 models have no revision pass) — *needs a call: session_heartbeat is only useful once Heartbeat is parsed and include_partial_turns=false would starve the pipeline of interim frames — confirm which of the four the team wants exposed*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — AssemblyAISyncSTTService drops a turn on the responses AssemblyAI documents as retryable (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — the Sync error-handling page calls 429 and 503 transient (retry after `Retry-After`, or a short backoff when absent) and 500/504 safe to retry once; `_transcribe` raises on any non-200 and `run_stt` turns that into an ErrorFrame, so the utterance is lost — *needs a call: a retry inside run_stt delays a live turn, so the retry budget and whether to honor Retry-After verbatim need a call*
- [assemblyai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — AssemblyAISyncSTTService posts the whole utterance after speech ends rather than streaming it to /v1/transcribe/live (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — the Sync API also exposes POST /v1/transcribe/live, a chunked multipart upload (config part first) that transcribes while the speaker is still talking; the service uses /v1/transcribe and /v1/warm only — *needs a call: streaming the body during the segment is a restructure of SegmentedSTTService's buffer-then-post flow, not a Settings field — needs a design call on whether the latency win is worth it*
- [assemblyai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Connect params inactivity_timeout and extend_biometric_consent have no Settings fields, and the cloned-voice consent expiry Begin reports is only logged (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — both are documented optional connect params; `Begin.configuration` carries `voice_expires_at` and `resolved_voice.extend_due_at`, which the service logs inside the whole configuration dump and never surfaces, so a cloned voice stops working with a 3012 `voice_expired` and no warning — *needs a call: extend_biometric_consent renews a biometric consent on the account rather than shaping a request, and both are connect-time only, so a Settings field would also have to join the voice/language/model reconnect set — confirm whether they belong in Settings at all, or as constructor arguments*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Server error frames are dropped by the websocket receive loop (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Async's error frames carry `error_code`, `message`, `extra` and `request_id` but no `context_id`, so `_receive_messages` keys off `msg.get("context_id")`, finds `None` unavailable, and discards them down its "unavailable context" branch. Reproduced in the 2026-11-09 run through the ordinary mistake of constructing the service with no voice: three `BAD_REQUEST` frames discarded, and the application sees only a generic `TTS context <id> completed with no audio` from `TTSService` ~3 s later, naming neither Async nor the reason. The same branch swallows the undocumented connect ack (`event: init_ack` with a server-assigned `context_id`), which is why every healthy connect logs `Ignoring message from unavailable context: <uuid>`. Code unchanged since that run. — *needs a call: whether an `error_code` frame should push an ErrorFrame carrying Async's message and close the context, or only be logged now that the base class reports the silence*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — AsyncAIHttpTTSService plays Async's in-band `--ERROR:QUOTA_EXCEEDED--` marker as audio — The streaming endpoint reference for `POST /text_to_speech/streaming` — the endpoint `run_tts` calls — documents that when quota runs out mid-stream the server keeps the 200 and writes the literal bytes `--ERROR:QUOTA_EXCEEDED--` into the `application/octet-stream` body. `run_tts` only branches on `response.status != 200`, then appends every chunk to one buffer and yields it as a `TTSAudioRawFrame`, so those 24 bytes are emitted as PCM and the turn reports success. Not probed: triggering it means exhausting the account's quota. — *needs a call: whether to scan streamed chunks for the sentinel (and whether it can arrive split across chunk boundaries) or to leave in-band errors to the caller*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The documented `latency` setting is rejected by the default model and by current Claude models (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `latency="optimized"` sends `performanceConfig`, which Bedrock still previews for Nova Pro, Llama 3.1 70B/405B and the retired Claude 3.5 Haiku only; the Settings docstring says only 'standard or optimized' — *needs a call: whether to name the supported models in the docstring, validate before sending, or drop the field as Bedrock narrows the preview*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default us.amazon.nova-lite-v1:0 is a generation behind Nova 2 Lite (GA 2025-12-02) (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — this run's TTFAT medians of 5 are 627 ms for the default against 747 ms for Nova 2 Lite, a 19% gap that widened from last run's 12%; nova-lite-v1:0 is still ACTIVE with no EOL date — *needs a call: aiewf-eval still has no Amazon Nova row of any generation, so the benchmark condition for a default bump cannot be met yet*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Structured output is unreachable even though Bedrock made it GA for Claude on Converse (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Converse takes `outputConfig.textFormat` as `OutputFormat {type: json_schema, structure.jsonSchema}` and AWS announced structured outputs for Claude on 2026-10-18, documenting per-model support on the model cards; the service leaves `supports_response_schema` False, so `run_inference(response_schema=...)` logs "not supported and is ignored", and its docstring still asserts Bedrock's Converse API has no reply schema — *needs a call: whether to claim response-schema support and gate it per model through `model_supports_response_schema`, given that Nova rejects `outputConfig` outright and some cross-Region profiles do not support it either*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Bedrock's strict tool use (`toolSpec.strict`) is never requested (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — Bedrock's structured-outputs page documents `strict: true` on a tool definition as the way to get schema-valid tool names and arguments, and botocore's `ToolSpecification` carries the member; the Bedrock adapter emits `toolSpec` without it, so every function call is best-effort — *needs a call: whether strict tool use should be on for everyone, a Settings opt-in, or per tool — and what a model that rejects `strict` should do*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Bedrock Guardrails (`guardrailConfig`) are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — ConverseStream takes guardrailIdentifier/guardrailVersion/trace; the service never sends the block — *needs a call: no guardrail exists in the probe account to verify a pass-through against, and guardrail interventions arriving mid-stream need a stance on what the pipeline should see*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-23.md) — Agent runtimes using JWT/OAuth inbound auth cannot be invoked at all (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — The API reference still states plainly: "If you're integrating your agent with OAuth, you can't use the AWS SDK to call InvokeAgentRuntime. Instead, make a HTTPS request." A runtime created with `customJWTAuthorizer` accepts only a bearer token against `https://bedrock-agentcore.<region>.amazonaws.com/runtimes/<escaped-arn>/invocations?qualifier=...`. The processor invokes exclusively through aiobotocore (SigV4), so those runtimes are unreachable; a runtime supports either SigV4 or JWT, never both. — *needs a call: Whether a bearer-token HTTP invocation path belongs in this processor at all — it means a second transport (aiohttp) plus a token-refresh story, well past a pass-through setting.*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-23.md) — InvokeAgentRuntime's qualifier, runtimeUserId, contentType/accept and trace headers are not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The call passes only `agentRuntimeArn` and `payload`, the two required members of the 16 the request has. `qualifier` selects a versioned endpoint instead of the default; `runtimeUserId` scopes a user for AgentCore Identity/Memory and is now also what makes a V2 session pinned (a SigV4 caller that omits the header creates an unpinned session); `traceId`/`traceParent`/`traceState`/`baggage` carry distributed tracing into AgentCore observability; `accountId` addresses a runtime by agent ID instead of full ARN. The four `mcp*` members apply only to MCP-protocol runtimes. — *needs a call: The processor has no Settings class, so exposing these means either a new params object or several constructor arguments — a shape decision; runtimeUserId also needs the separate InvokeAgentRuntimeForUser IAM action, so it is not a free pass-through.*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-23.md) — RetryableConflictException (409) is not retried, unlike the other transient errors (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The client is created with no `Config`, so botocore's default legacy retry mode applies: its `__default__` policies cover socket errors, 500/502/503/504 and the throttling family (429), up to 5 attempts. RetryableConflictException is 409 and its shape carries the modeled `retryable: {throttling: false}` trait that only standard/adaptive honour. The session guide pins the window down — while a session is being provisioned or torn down, a second operation on it returns 409 "Session operation in progress, please retry", to be retried with short exponential backoff. Untouched, the exception escapes the invocation and the turn is lost. — *needs a call: Whether to switch the client to retries mode standard — that changes default behaviour (added backoff inside a voice turn's latency budget), so it is a call, not a pass-through.*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The first-generation code path still ships for a model Bedrock has removed (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `_is_first_generation_sonic_model()` gates the `ready.wav` assistant-response-trigger hack, `AWAIT_TRIGGER_ASSISTANT_RESPONSE_INSTRUCTION`, `trigger_assistant_response()` and the `endpointing_sensitivity` downgrade warning. The only model that selects it, `amazon.nova-sonic-v1:0`, is still rejected at connect with "The provided model identifier is invalid", so the path is unreachable in practice. — *needs a call: whether the first-generation path (and the bundled `ready.wav`) is deprecated for removal in 2.0.0 or deleted outright*
