- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Authentication is limited to static IAM access keys; Bedrock API keys and the default credential chain are unreachable (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `access_key_id`/`secret_access_key`/`region` are required constructor arguments and `create_client()` hardcodes `StaticCredentialsResolver()` with a SigV4-only `auth_schemes` map, so an instance/task role, a named profile or a Bedrock API key cannot be used. The installed SDK 0.9.0 already lists `smithy.api#httpBearerAuth` on the bidirectional-stream operation, and the Nova 2.5 Sonic model card still presents the API key (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`) as the only getting-started credential. — *needs a call: a call on which auth modes to support (bearer API key, default credential chain) and how to keep the existing required arguments working*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Remaining StartStreamTranscription request parameters are unreachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Out of reach: `vocabulary-name` and `vocabulary-filter-name` (already parameters of `get_presigned_url`, never passed by the service), their language-identification counterparts `vocabulary-names` and `vocabulary-filter-names` (now relevant, since language identification landed), `vocabulary-filter-method`, `content-redaction-type` / `content-identification-type` / `pii-entity-types`, `language-model-name`, and `enable-channel-identification` with `number-of-channels` (private attributes fixed at False and 1). — *needs a call: Which subset to expose first. None can be probed end-to-end without account-side resources (a custom vocabulary, a custom language model), so they cannot ride an automatic PR.*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-23.md) — A dropped connection starts a new transcription session rather than resuming (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The presigned URL carries no `session-id` or `session-resume-window`, so the reconnect that `WebsocketSTTService` performs opens an unrelated stream and any in-flight segment is lost. AWS allows resuming within a window of 1-300 minutes. — *needs a call: Whether resume is worth the state it adds, given a voice turn is short and a lost partial is usually re-spoken.*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — run_tts reads the whole AudioStream before yielding, so TTFB is full synthesis time (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — measured 2026-08-31 on generative: first chunk 182 ms vs full read 1193 ms; generative probes on 2026-10-28 landed at TTFB 723-834 ms against 226 ms for the standard default (502 ms this run). `AudioStream.iter_chunks()` is available on aiobotocore's stream — *needs a call: yielding partial audio changes when TTSAudioRawFrame first appears and how resampling is chunked; needs a call on reworking run_tts*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Polly's bidirectional streaming API (StartSpeechSynthesisStream, GA 2026-03-19) is not used (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — HTTP/2 stream that takes text incrementally and returns audio as synthesized, generative engine only; botocore ships the model but deletes the operation from the client (botocore/handlers.py remove_polly_start_speech_synthesis_stream), so it needs the smithy client aws_sdk_polly. Available in nine regions, most recently us-gov-west-1 (2026-10-22) — *needs a call: a streaming Polly class means a new class plus a new SDK dependency, the same shape as aws-nova-sonic's aws_sdk_bedrock_runtime extra — needs a product call*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The service leaves Engine unset, so Polly synthesizes with the standard engine (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — standard is Polly's oldest concatenative engine and its docs page still carries no retirement notice (re-read this run, 60 voices in 29 locales); neural measured at the same latency (TTFB 242 ms vs 239 ms, medians of 5, 2026-09-28) but neural costs $16 vs $4 per 1M characters. Custom (cloned) voices are generative-only, so they need the engine set too — *needs a call: a default engine change raises every user's bill 4x and breaks voices that only exist as standard; needs a maintainer's call*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-23.md) — Settings expose only `image_size`; the v1 images request documents quality, background, output_format and more (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Azure's v1 preview OpenAPI schema and the how-to page for `/images/generations` list `quality`, `background` (auto|transparent), `output_format` (png|jpeg; webp unsupported), `output_compression` (0-100, JPEG only), `moderation`, `n` (1-10), `style`, `response_format`, `user` and `stream`/`partial_images`. `AzureImageGenSettings` adds only `image_size` on top of the inherited `model`; `n` is hard-coded to 1. The parameter domains vary by model - gpt-image-2/2.5 take custom WIDTHxHEIGHT (edges multiples of 16, long edge <= 3840px) while gpt-image-1/-1.5/-mini take three fixed sizes, and quality is low|medium|high|xhigh|max|auto on 2.5 against low|medium|high elsewhere. — *needs a call: Which fields to expose depends on the endpoint rewrite above - the parameter set differs between the retired submit flow and the v1 endpoint, so adding fields now would encode the dead schema.*
- [azure/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/image/2026-11-23.md) — A non-2xx submit response raises `KeyError` instead of yielding an `ErrorFrame` (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `operation_location = submission.headers["operation-location"]` runs unguarded; the header is absent on any error response, so every call against the retired endpoint dies with an unhandled `KeyError` rather than an `ErrorFrame`. The code carries a comment acknowledging this. — *needs a call: Whether to patch the error path on its own or only as part of the endpoint rewrite - a standalone fix touches code that is due to be replaced.*
- [azure/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Azure's recommended Responses API has no Azure service class (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Azure's v1 API guidance still says "For Azure OpenAI models we recommend using the Responses API" (page unchanged, ms.date 2026-05-13), and OpenAIResponsesHttpLLMService already reaches it when base_url is the resource's /openai/v1/ endpoint (probed live on 2026-08-31, TTFAT 1157 ms vs 1860 ms for AzureLLMService chat completions on the same gpt-4.1 deployment). What is missing is an Azure entry point - no Entra ID token-provider path, and the WebSocket Responses class defaults ws_url to OpenAI's host with no Azure equivalent documented. — *needs a call: whether Azure gets a Responses subclass plus docs, or users are simply pointed at OpenAIResponsesHttpLLMService with base_url*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — CLI scaffold passes `input_audio_transcription=` to SessionProperties, a field the event model no longer has (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `SessionProperties(input_audio_transcription=InputAudioTranscription(model="whisper-1"))` dumps to `{'type': 'realtime'}` — the field is not in `SessionProperties.model_fields` and pydantic drops the kwarg — so scaffolded Azure bots run with no user transcription. The shape the model accepts is `audio=AudioConfiguration(input=AudioInput(transcription=...))`, as `examples/realtime/realtime-azure.py` uses. On Azure the transcription `model` must also name a transcription deployment, not the literal `whisper-1`. — *needs a call: the scaffold needs a transcription deployment name from somewhere, so fixing the shape alone still leaves a literal that only works by coincidence*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Reasoning settings are stripped for Azure deployments of gpt-realtime-2/2.1 because the check reads the deployment name (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `OpenAIRealtimeLLMService._strip_unsupported_reasoning` keeps `reasoning` only when `settings.model` contains one of `_REASONING_CAPABLE_MODEL_SUBSTRINGS` (`gpt-realtime-2`, `gpt-realtime-2.1`, `openai/realtime/llm.py:863`). On Azure that string is the deployment name, so a reasoning-capable deployment called anything else silently loses `Reasoning(effort=...)` (with a warning). `gpt-realtime-2.1` is GA on Azure, so reasoning-capable deployments are production, not preview. — *needs a call: how Azure should decide reasoning capability when the model name is opaque — trust the user (no stripping on Azure) or add an explicit opt-in*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Azure's GPT-Live surface (gpt-live-1, GA) is not reachable from Pipecat — Azure serves OpenAI's Live protocol at `wss://<resource>.openai.azure.com/openai/v1/live/sessions` with `gpt-live-1` GA (retirement 2027-09-10), same `session.start` / delegation / append events Pipecat's `OpenAILiveLLMService` already speaks. That class takes a `base_url`, but `_connect` hard-codes `additional_headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {self.api_key}"}` (`openai/live/llm.py:682`) with no auth hook, so only an Entra access token passed as `api_key` would work and key-based auth cannot; there is no Azure subclass and no example. — *needs a call: whether Azure GPT-Live belongs as a new `AzureLiveLLMService` (mirroring this class) or as an `_auth_headers` hook on `OpenAILiveLLMService` plus docs — and it is arguably the openai/live unit's call, not this one's*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Azure's newest STT models are not reachable; the recognizer always runs the base streaming model (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Speech SDK 1.52 added streaming model selection - Python sets `speech_config.model = "mai-transcribe-2-streaming"` before constructing the recognizer. The probe environment runs SDK 1.53.0, where `SpeechConfig.model` exists, but the model overview (ms.date 2026-09-30, updated 2026-10-07) is unchanged - still public preview, still served only from swedencentral/centralus/eastus2/southeastasia, and our probe credential's region is not one of them, so the path stays unverified. `AzureSTTSettings` already inherits `model` from `STTSettings` and this class ignores it, so a user who sets it today gets silence — *needs a call: `SpeechConfig.model` does not exist below SDK 1.52 while the `azure` extra still allows 1.47, so wiring the inherited `model` field means raising the floor or guarding on the attribute - plus a stance on shipping a preview model path we cannot probe from this account's region.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Explicit audio-boundary commit (`PushAudioInputStream.commit()`) is not used (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — preview feature Azure documents for "turn-based voice agents" - `set_service_property("setfeature", "forcecommit", ServicePropertyChannel.UriQueryParameter)` on the config, `pushStream.commit()` after the last audio of a turn, and `result.commit_token` on the matching `recognized` event. It would finalize a turn on the user-stopped-speaking signal instead of waiting out `Speech_SegmentationSilenceTimeoutMs`. Python has had `commit()` since 1.52 (present on the installed 1.53.0); the format limits (PCM/A-law/mu-law/G.711, no Microsoft Audio Stack) are all satisfied here — *needs a call: not a pass-through field - it needs the service to observe turn-stop and call `commit()`, plus the same SDK floor question; decide whether a preview latency path belongs in the service.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Semantic segmentation (`Speech_SegmentationStrategy="Semantic"`) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — one more `set_property` pass-through alongside the merged `segmentation_silence_timeout_ms` and `post_processing_option`; segments on sentence-ending punctuation instead of silence. Needs SDK 1.41+, which our floor already satisfies, and the documented loss of confidence scores and NBest lists costs this class nothing - it reads only `result.text`. The related `Speech_SegmentationMaximumTimeMs` (20000-70000 ms, caps phrase length under the "Time" strategy) is a separate knob and not a clean pass-through - it shortens the silence timeout as the cap approaches, so it interacts with a setting we already expose — *needs a call: the how-to page (updated 2026-11-01) still scopes Semantic to continuous recognition for dictation and captioning, says it is not intended for interactive scenarios, and does not offer it in every locale - exposing it needs a stance plus a latency measurement on agent-length turns.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Automatic language detection is not supported; the recognizer is always pinned to one locale (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Azure needs `AutoDetectSourceLanguageConfig` passed to the `SpeechRecognizer` constructor (open-range form covers the multilingual set); `_on_handle_recognized` already reads `result.language`, so per-utterance language would flow through. It is also the gate on multilingual post-stream refinement, GA since October, which needs the open-range config in addition to `PostRefinement` — *needs a call: a second recognizer construction path plus a stance on what `Settings.language` means when detection is on - bigger than a pass-through field.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Phrase lists (keyword/entity biasing) are not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `PhraseListGrammar.from_recognizer(...)` boosts names and domain terms and is supported on real-time transcription, including both post-stream refinement modes; the cap is 2,000 phrases generally and 200 phrases / 3,200 characters on the multilingual refinement path, and `set_weight` takes 0.0-2.0 — *needs a call: it attaches to the recognizer, not the config, so it has to be re-applied on every reconnect and cleared on update - decide whether that lives in `_connect` or a dedicated setting handler.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — MAI voices emit no word boundaries, so AzureTTSService produces audio but almost no TTSTextFrame (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `AzureTTSService` sets `push_text_frames=False` and derives text frames from Azure's word boundary events. Re-probed today with Pipecat's exact SSML: `en-US-Harper:MAI-Voice-2.1-Flash` returned 0 word boundaries where `en-US-SaraNeural`, `en-US-Ava:DragonHDLatestNeural` and `en-US-Ava:DragonHDV3Neural` each returned 13. Through the class the MAI voice yields 1 `TTSTextFrame` against 9 for the default voice. Only the MAI family is affected, and it is the one Microsoft points at real-time voice agents. — *needs a call: a fallback (push the aggregated sentence when a synthesis completes with no word boundary) changes timing semantics for every voice — someone has to decide whether that fallback is unconditional or keyed to the voice family.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Custom voice deployments are unreachable; the TTS classes have no endpoint_id (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `AzureSTTService` already takes `endpoint_id` and sets `SpeechConfig.endpoint_id`; the TTS classes only take `private_endpoint`, which is Private Link/custom domain, not a fine-tuned voice deployment. — *needs a call: it is a constructor argument rather than a runtime setting, so it needs a maintainer's call on the shape (and on whether it pairs with `voice` validation).*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `azure` extra floor (`azure-cognitiveservices-speech>=1.47.0`) predates TTS fixes in 1.50 through 1.53 (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — 1.50 fixed a TTS connection race condition, 1.51 fixed TTS token credential errors on the REST backend, 1.51.2 fixed a SIGABRT crash during speech synthesis plus a Python AudioDataStream memory leak, and 1.52/1.53 fixed TTS crashes with compressed audio; 1.48.2 carries the CRL fix Microsoft required for Linux/Android by 2026-07-01. Fresh resolves get 1.53.1, so only pinned-old installs are exposed. Shared with azure/stt, which raised the same item. — *needs a call: whether provider-watch may move dependency floors, or whether that is a maintainer-owned change.*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `reasoning_effort` and `parallel_tool_calls` session properties are unreachable (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Both are documented top-level fields of RealtimeRequestSession. `SessionProperties` is a Pydantic model with the default `extra="ignore"`, so `SessionProperties(reasoning_effort="low", parallel_tool_calls=False)` validates and then serialises to nothing (re-confirmed offline: the dump is `{}`). ReasoningEffort values are none/minimal/low/medium/high/xhigh, which matter for the GPT-5 and GPT-6 models Voice Live offers. — *needs a call: a probe against a live Voice Live session to confirm the fields are accepted, which needs a Foundry resource credential*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `InputAudioNoiseReduction.type` rejects the documented headset and personal suppression modes (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — api-version 2026-07-15 documents four noise-reduction models: RealtimeOpenAINoiseReduction (near_field/far_field), RealtimeAzureDeepNoiseSuppression, RealtimeAzureDeepNoiseSuppressionHeadset (azure_deep_noise_suppression_headset) and RealtimeAzurePersonalNoiseSuppression (azure_personal_noise_suppression). Our Literal allows only the first three values, so the two modes raise a ValidationError before a request is built (re-confirmed offline). Widening the Literal is a one-line change. — *needs a call: the docs say both modes require deployment support, and the change needs a live session to confirm the service accepts them; no Voice Live credential here.*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Interim responses (`interim-response`) cannot be configured (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Voice Live can speak filler while a tool runs or latency exceeds a threshold, via a static (`static-interim-response`: triggers/latency_threshold_ms/texts) or LLM-generated (`llm-interim-response`: adds model/instructions/max_completion_tokens) config on the session, and per-response via `interim_response`. Pipecat has no equivalent for a realtime service, and the session key is hyphenated so it needs a field alias. — *needs a call: whether Pipecat should surface a provider-side filler mechanism at all, given pipelines usually handle this themselves*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `EndOfUtteranceDetection.threshold` does not match the documented `threshold_level` (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — RealtimeEOUDetection documents `model` (semantic_detection_v1, _v1_en, _v1_multilingual), `threshold_level` (low/medium/high/default, default equals medium) and `timeout_ms` (default 1000). The events model exposes `threshold: float` instead, so the documented knob is unreachable and the float is sent under a name the schema does not list. — *needs a call: whether `threshold` is still accepted by the service (a rename would be a breaking change for anyone setting it), which needs a live session to check*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Foundry Agent Service sessions are not supported (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — An agent-backed session connects with `agent_id` and `project_id` query parameters instead of `model`; `_connect` always sends `model`. Agents also reject `instructions`, which the service always syncs into the session. — *needs a call: new constructor arguments and a branch in the session payload, so larger than a settings pass-through; worth deciding whether agent-backed Voice Live is in scope*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Transport input sample rates other than 16000/24000 are forwarded unchanged (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `_ensure_audio_config` forces `input_audio_format` to pcm16 and sets `input_audio_sampling_rate` to the transport's rate; the how-to documents 16000 and 24000 as the supported PCM values (the reference notes 8000 only as the default for g711, which this service never sends). An 8 kHz telephony transport therefore configures an undocumented rate, where the output path already falls back to 24 kHz with a warning. — *needs a call: confirmation that Voice Live rejects (rather than resamples) 8000 Hz PCM input before adding a resample-or-warn path*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The language map offers locales mars-flash and mars-pro reject with HTTP 422 — `language_to_camb_language` maps 64 Pipecat languages to locale tags, but Camb's Language Support page lists only 33 locales for `mars-flash` (the default) and `mars-pro`. `en-gb`, `en-au`, `zh-tw`, `th-th`, `vi-vn`, `he-il`, `sw-ke`, `sv-se`, `da-dk`, `cs-cz`, `uk-ua`, `ms-my`, `gu-in` and others are in our map and outside that set; the endpoint answers 422 with the allowed locales listed. The map warns only when a Language is absent from it, so a mapped-but-unsupported locale looks supported until the request fails. The module docstring's blanket "140+ languages supported" matches mars-instruct's 142, not the default's 33. — *needs a call: a call on whether to gate the map per model (a table like MODEL_SAMPLE_RATES) or just warn, plus a live probe of a rejected locale that the credential blocks*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — MARS 8.1 beta models are missing from MODEL_SAMPLE_RATES, so their 48 kHz audio is labelled 22050 Hz (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `mars-8.1-flash-beta` and `mars-8.1-pro-beta` are in the SDK's speech_model literal and are the endpoint's documented default; `MODEL_SAMPLE_RATES.get(model, 22050)` falls back to 22050 for both, and the docs list both at 48 kHz. Camb also lists mars-flash as 22.05 kHz/48 kHz, which the single table entry cannot express. — *needs a call: a live probe to confirm the beta models' actual stream rate, which CAMB_API_KEY currently blocks; also a call on whether beta models belong in the table at all*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Camb's `voice_settings` (speaking_rate, maintain_source_accent, enhance_reference_audio_quality) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `StreamTtsVoiceSettings` is a documented parameter of the streaming TTS endpoint the service calls; `speaking_rate` (default 1) is documented as the only way to adjust pacing, since the endpoint has no duration parameter. `run_tts` never builds the object. — *needs a call: a probe with the field set, which the rejected credential blocks; and a call on whether to expose speaking_rate alone or the whole nested object*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The constructor's `sample_rate` is never sent to Camb, so a user-chosen rate mislabels the audio (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `output_configuration` carries a documented `sample_rate` field, but the service only sets `format="pcm_s16le"`. A caller passing `sample_rate=` gets a warning and frames tagged at their rate while the stream stays at the model's native rate. The same object's `apply_enhancement` is also unreachable. — *needs a call: a call on whether to request the rate from Camb or keep the warning-only behaviour, plus a live probe of a non-native rate*
- [cartesia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Default ink-whisper is Cartesia's older STT model; ink-2 is the stable recommended one (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — latency is unchanged for a sixth run - TTFS 759 ms for ink-2 against 218 ms for ink-whisper, in line with the 523/239 median-of-5 comparison from 2026-10-12, so the default-bump latency test still fails. ink-2 stays five languages (en/es/fr/hi/ja) while ink-whisper covers 100, and ink-2 rejects both `language_hint` and more than 100 keyterms. ink-whisper is still Stable on the older-models page with no sunset date. — *needs a call: whether the manual-endpoint service should follow ink-2 and accept a 2-3x TTFS regression, or stay on ink-whisper and leave ink-2 to CartesiaTurnsSTTService*
- [cartesia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `language_hint` is not reachable from Settings, so ink-preview's 44 languages cannot be asked for through this class — new this run - the `/stt/websocket` reference documents `language_hint` (repeatable, up to 6 base codes, alias `language_hints`) for ink-preview, which the models page re-dated 2026-11-17 with 44 languages. An ad-hoc connection confirms ink-preview accepts it both with and without `language`, and ink-2 closes with 1008 "Language hints not supported". CartesiaTurnsSTTService already has the field and a `_prepare_language_hints` helper living in its own module. — *needs a call: whether to lift `_prepare_language_hints` out of the turns service into the shared module, and whether this class should stop sending its always-set `language=en` on ink-preview, which the reference scopes to ink-whisper*
- [cartesia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Keyterms are capped at ink-2's 100 terms / 1200 characters for every model, truncating ink-preview's documented 500 / 5000 — new this run - the keyterms page now gives per-model limits, and an ad-hoc connection with 300 keyterms succeeds on ink-preview (URL 6090 bytes) while ink-2 closes with 1008 "Too many keyterms". `_prepare_keyterms` in `services/cartesia/stt.py` applies 100/1200 unconditionally and warns about what it drops, so ink-preview users silently lose terms Cartesia would accept. The helper is imported by CartesiaTurnsSTTService. — *needs a call: whether per-model caps are worth changing a helper shared with the turns service, given Cartesia's ~8 KB URL limit - 500 terms of 5000 characters can exceed it once URL-encoded and then fail the handshake with HTTP 414*
- [cartesia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/tts/2026-11-23.md) — A dict-valued `generation_config` crashes synthesis (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `CartesiaTTSSettings.from_mapping` leaves the nested value a plain dict and both services call `.model_dump()` on it, so the turn errors out with no audio; reproduced again this run on `CartesiaTTSService` from `probe.py --setting generation_config={"emotion":"calm"}`; fish, inworld, google and anthropic override `from_mapping` for exactly this — *needs a call: whether the dict path should coerce nested values into `GenerationConfig` (and what to do with unknown keys) or reject them outright*
- [cartesia/turns-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/turns-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Keyterm limits are hard-coded to `ink-2`'s, so `ink-preview` keyterms are silently truncated — Cartesia documents 100 keyterms / 1,200 characters for `ink-2` but 500 / 5,000 for `ink-preview`; `_prepare_keyterms` in `cartesia/stt.py` applies `_MAX_KEYTERMS = 100` and `_MAX_KEYTERM_CHARS = 1200` to every model, so an `ink-preview` session loses keyterms the server would have accepted, with only a warning — *needs a call: the constants are shared with `CartesiaSTTService`, so a per-model table has to be agreed across both Cartesia STT units*
- [cerebras/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cerebras/llm/2026-11-23.md) — qwen-3.8-27b is the only alternative on the public tier and is unbenchmarked for voice (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — Unchanged this run. aiewf-eval still has no row for `gpt-oss-120b` (the Groq row remains archived; last published 86.3% pass / 98 ms P50 with an unresolved cohort mapping), and the qwen rows still read 96.0% / 649 ms (thinking off, FP8, Baseten) and 95.1% / 101 ms (thinking off, NVFP4, local). Cerebras still positions qwen-3.8-27b as the Shared Inference replacement for the retired `gemma-4-31b`, not for `gpt-oss-120b`, which is not deprecated and is still the named replacement for most earlier retirements. It works as-is through the thin wrapper (no allowlist); this run's single-turn probe put it at TTFAT 593 ms with 464 ms of thinking against the default's 232 ms, again showing reasoning on by default rather than a stable latency ranking. — *needs a call: A call on whether a candidate may be bumped over a default whose benchmark row has been withdrawn, given that Cerebras names qwen-3.8-27b as gemma-4-31b's replacement rather than gpt-oss-120b's - and on whether a bump would have to pin `reasoning_effort="none"`, since Cerebras runs qwen-3.8-27b at reasoning `high` by default while every benchmarked row is thinking-off.*
- [crusoe/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/crusoe/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default openai/gpt-oss-120b is below the Crusoe-hosted field on aiewf-eval aiwf_medium_context (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — Unchanged from the last run. `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash`, Crusoe's migration target for the models it retired on 2026-11-02, still has no aiewf-eval row. Crusoe-hosted rows on the board: glm-5.3 (low reasoning) 97.7% / 2376 ms, gemma-4-31b-it (thinking off) 96.4% / 497 ms, qwen3.8-27b (thinking off, FP8) 96.0% / 649 ms, nemotron-3.5-lightning (thinking on, NVFP4) 83.9% / 1470 ms. The default has no row under the current Opus 5.5 medium judge — the groq gpt-oss-120b row stays archived at a pre-rejudge 86.3%. `openai/gpt-oss-120b` is still listed and carries no deprecation marker, so nothing forces a move. — *needs a call: whether to wait for an aiewf-eval row on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash or pick from the three benchmarked Crusoe-hosted models despite the default having only an archived pre-rejudge score — and a working CRUSOE_API_KEY either way, since no candidate can be probed*
- [crusoe/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/crusoe/llm/2026-11-23.md) — CRUSOE_API_KEY is rejected with HTTP 401, so the unit has gone unprobed for six runs (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both /v1/models and a chat completion answer 401; the base_url and request shape match Crusoe's documented endpoint, so this is a credential problem on our side, not a service defect — *needs a call: someone with Crusoe Intelligence Foundry access to reissue the key in the provider-watch secret store, or a decision to mark the unit docs-only until one exists*
- [deepgram/flux-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-stt/2026-11-23.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10`, four minors behind 0.13.1 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — PyPI moved 0.12.0 (2026-10-08) to 0.13.1 (2026-11-18) this cycle; the pin still excludes 0.10 through 0.13, so an app on a current AWS preview SDK cannot resolve `pipecat-ai[deepgram,sagemaker]`. AWS publishes no release notes for the package, and `pipecat.services.aws.sagemaker.bidi_client` still imports the deprecated `SageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client` alias. — *needs a call: the pin is shared by every SageMaker service, not just Flux, and the SDK is a 0.x preview with no changelog — widening it needs someone to run the AWS BiDi services against 0.13.1*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-23.md) — Flux's inline IPA pronunciation is not emitted; `format_pronunciation` returns None (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Still Early Access. Flux streaming accepts the exact markup `format_deepgram_pronunciation` already builds for Aura-2, so support is the Aura-2 `format_pronunciation` override plus a `supports_pronunciations` gate on `speed`. The control matrix still limits Flux controls to English, so a gate would have to account for the 41 non-English languages. — *needs a call: whether to emit an Early-Access control whose misuse is fatal — a pronunciation on a connection with `speed` set closes the session with `DATA-0002`, and a `Configure` speed over a queued pronunciation turn is `CONTROL_COMBINATION_INVALID`*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-23.md) — `Interrupt` is sent without `playback_offset`, so Flux never reports `text_spoken` / `text_remaining` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Deepgram's documented barge-in pattern is Interrupt + `playback_offset: {type: time_ms, value: <session ms played>}`; the server then returns the exact text the user heard so the LLM context can be corrected. `on_audio_context_interrupted` sends a bare `{"type": "Interrupt"}`, so both fields are omitted and `audio_played_ms` falls back to the server's generated-audio total — which the code comment already notes. — *needs a call: whether Pipecat can hand the service a session-cumulative played-milliseconds counter, and whether Flux's `text_spoken` should override the assistant aggregator's own account of what was spoken*
- [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10` and the BiDi client imports a deprecated alias (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — PyPI is now at 0.13.1 (2026-11-18), four minors past the `<0.10` ceiling, which resolves 0.9.0. On 0.9.0 the import in `src/pipecat/services/aws/sagemaker/bidi_client.py` warns that `SageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client` is a deprecated alias for `AsyncSageMakerRuntimeHTTP2Client`, and the Flux SageMaker tests emit that warning on every run. The SDK is pre-1.0 and documents breaking changes before then. — *needs a call: someone with a deployed SageMaker endpoint to verify the BiDi client against 0.13 before widening the pin and switching to the async client name*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Keyterm changes reconnect the WebSocket instead of using Deepgram's `/v1/listen` `Configure` message (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — deepgram-sdk has had `send_configure(ListenV1Configure(keyterms=..., features=...))` on the Listen v1 socket client since 7.13.0 with typed `ConfigureSuccess`/`ConfigureFailure` replies since 7.14.0, and Deepgram runs mid-stream keyterm updates on the global, EU, AU and IN endpoints. `DeepgramSTTService._update_settings` still reconnects on any non-local change, so updating `keyterm` (or `numerals`, which `features` also toggles) drops and re-establishes the connection and loses the audio buffered across it. — *needs a call: whether `_update_settings` should route keyterm and numerals changes through `Configure` and reconnect only for the rest, and what to do with a `ConfigureFailure`*
- [deepgram/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Deepgram's `UtteranceEnd`, `SpeechStarted` and `Configure` reply messages are dropped, so the `utterance_end_ms` Settings field has no observable effect (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_on_message` handles only `ListenV1Results`; the SDK also exports `ListenV1UtteranceEnd`, `ListenV1SpeechStarted`, `ListenV1Metadata`, `ListenV1Error` and the two Configure replies, and since 7.18.0 keeps anything else as a raw dict rather than dropping it. Setting `utterance_end_ms` therefore changes the request but nothing downstream — Pipecat ends turns from VAD plus `Finalize` instead — and the Configure item above depends on reading a reply. — *needs a call: whether Deepgram's endpointing signals should drive turn-taking at all now that VAD plus `Finalize` does, or whether the dead knob should just be documented as inert*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `sagemaker` extra pins `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2<0.10`, five releases behind the published 0.13.1 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — pyproject still pins `>=0.6.0,<0.10`; PyPI is now at 0.13.1 (2026-11-18), with 0.10.0, 0.11.0, 0.12.0 and 0.13.0 in between. The package calls itself experimental with breaking changes possible before 1.0.0, so the ceiling reads as deliberate. `DeepgramSageMakerTTSService` imports only `ResponseStreamEventPayloadPart`, but the shared `SageMakerBidiClient` uses more of the surface, and the same pin gates the Flux SageMaker TTS and STT classes. — *needs a call: whether to raise the ceiling, which needs a deployed SageMaker endpoint to verify 0.13.1 against — and a decision on whether this belongs to the Deepgram units or to a single AWS SageMaker owner*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `tag`, Deepgram's usage-reporting label, is a constructor argument on the Flux TTS services but not on the Aura ones (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — still documented as a query parameter in the REST `/v1/speak` reference (a string or list); `DeepgramFluxTTSService` and the Flux STT services already take a `tag` list and append one `tag=` per value. The Aura WebSocket reference still lists only `model`, `encoding`, `sample_rate`, `speed` and `mip_opt_out`, and an earlier probe showed the WebSocket opens happily with `tag=` — but equally happily with an invented parameter name, so tolerating it proves nothing about whether it is recorded. — *needs a call: whether Aura should grow the constructor argument, and whether Deepgram records a WebSocket `tag` in usage reporting at all (only the usage report can say)*
- [deepgram/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Settings `extra` never reaches the request in either Aura class, so any parameter without a declared field is silently dropped (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_connect_websocket` and `run_tts` build their query from named fields only, so `Settings(extra={...})` is accepted, stored and ignored — there is no escape hatch for `tag`, `callback`, `callback_method` or `bit_rate`, all four still in the REST reference, nor for anything Deepgram adds next. The WebSocket endpoint ignores unknown query keys rather than rejecting them, so forwarding `extra` would be safe but would also fail silently on a typo. The STT service has the mirror-image bug (it forwards `extra` into an SDK call that rejects undeclared keys). — *needs a call: whether Deepgram TTS should forward undeclared keys to the query string unvalidated, or reject them at construction instead of dropping them*
- [deepseek/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepseek/llm/2026-11-23.md) — DeepSeek's server-side `deepseek_web_search` / `deepseek_read_url` tools are reachable only through the custom-tools escape hatch, and their citations are dropped — launched 2026-11-13 for `deepseek-flash` on Chat Completions; a user reaches them today through `ToolsSchema` custom tools under `AdapterType.OPENAI`, and a probe confirms the request is accepted and text streams normally. The response's `server_tool_calls` and the `annotations` carrying url citations are ignored by the inherited OpenAI streaming path, so the model answers from the web with no sources surfaced, and with thinking enabled the tool becomes a multi-step deep search (up to ~20 calls) that voice latency would feel — *needs a call: whether Pipecat should name these tools (an `AdapterType.DEEPSEEK` entry or a documented recipe) and whether url citations should surface as frames*
- [deepseek/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepseek/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `reasoning_effort` (none/low/high/max) has no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the only way to ask for a cheaper reasoning pass than DeepSeek's default `high`; it reaches the API today only as `extra={"reasoning_effort": "low"}`. A typed field would collide with the shipped `thinking` default - the API documents `thinking` as taking precedence over `reasoning_effort`, so the field would silently do nothing unless the user also changes `thinking` — *needs a call: whether a typed field is worth adding given it only takes effect once `thinking` is enabled or cleared, or whether `extra` remains the answer*
- [deepseek/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepseek/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `LLMSettings.use_provider_thinking_default` has no effect on DeepSeekLLMService (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the service bakes `thinking=DeepSeekThinkingConfig(type="disabled")` into its constructor defaults instead of applying it at request-build time through `LLMService._apply_thinking_default`, as Google, OpenAI Responses and Perplexity do. A `BackendLLMWorker`, which sets `use_provider_thinking_default=True`, therefore still gets thinking disabled, and no per-model log line says which setting was sent — *needs a call: whether to move the disabled default into a `_maybe_apply_thinking_default` hook, which changes what `service.settings.thinking` reads back as and what an explicit `thinking=None` means*
- [elevenlabs/dialogue-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/dialogue-tts/2026-11-23.md) — `pronunciation_dictionary_locators` cannot be sent on a Text-to-Dialogue context (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the endpoint accepts them on a context's first message; ElevenLabsTTSService already takes them as a constructor argument, this class does not — *needs a call: a constructor argument to mirror the sibling class, plus a stance on word timestamps — dictionary substitutions shift every timestamp in the turn*
- [elevenlabs/dialogue-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/dialogue-tts/2026-11-23.md) — Only one voice per context is registered, so multi-speaker dialogue is unreachable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the websocket reference caps eleven_v3 at 10 voices per context and eleven_v3_conversational and eleven_v4 at one, and leaves eleven_v4_turbo unlisted; ElevenLabs' own skills repo contradicts it (v4_turbo one, v4 ten), as did the 2026-10-12 probe — *needs a call: whether a Pipecat TTS service should express per-utterance speakers at all, and through what API*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Default scribe_v2 is superseded by scribe_v3 (shipped 2026-11-09) — ElevenLabs calls scribe_v3 the successor to scribe_v2 and reports 27% fewer word errors on FLEURS across 102 languages; scribe_v2 remains available with no retirement date, and nothing in the class gates the model, so a user can already select it — *needs a call: a working account key — the bump needs a median-TTFB comparison against scribe_v2 and this run's probes all answer 401*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Default scribe_v2_realtime is superseded by scribe_v3_live (shipped 2026-11-09) — the realtime endpoint now accepts scribe_v2_realtime or scribe_v3_live, and ElevenLabs reports a 44% lower semantic word error rate for v3 Live on FLEURS across 73 languages; v3 Live also brings turn prediction, a multilingual mode and a 10,000-term keyterm library — *needs a call: a working account key — same 401 wall, and v3 Live's larger feature surface deserves a connect-and-transcribe probe before it becomes the default*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Entity detection and redaction are not reachable from either Settings class (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `entity_detection` / `entity_redaction` / `entity_redaction_mode` on batch; realtime takes the two redaction parameters and answers with `committed_transcript_entities` and `redacted_transcript`, which `_process_response` treats as unknown — *needs a call: a stance on the response side — entities and redacted text need somewhere to go beyond `TranscriptionFrame.result`, and ElevenLabs charges a surcharge*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Zero-retention mode (`enable_logging=false`) cannot be set on ElevenLabsSTTService (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the realtime class takes `enable_logging` as a constructor argument; the batch endpoint takes the same query parameter (default true) but the batch class never sends it, so enterprise customers cannot opt out of log storage on it — *needs a call: whether to match the realtime class's constructor argument or make it a Settings field on both*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The batch service always uploads WAV, never `file_format=pcm_s16le_16` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — ElevenLabs documents lower latency for raw 16 kHz mono PCM than for an encoded waveform; SegmentedSTTService hands `run_stt` a WAV, so using it means reaching past that conversion — *needs a call: whether the segmented base class should expose the raw PCM segment, and whether the saving is worth measuring first*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Scribe v3's `numbers_format` and `secondary_language_codes` are not reachable from ElevenLabsSTTSettings — both shipped with scribe_v3 on 2026-11-09; `numbers_format` is `digits` or `words`, `secondary_language_codes` is the batch twin of the realtime `secondary_languages` field we already have. - [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `commit_strategy=turn_prediction` cannot be selected (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — the `CommitStrategy` StrEnum still has only `manual` and `vad`. Unlike previous runs the value now has a model behind it — scribe_v3_live commits when a partial's `turn_probability` reaches 0.9, and other models reject the strategy with `invalid_request` and close the connection — *needs a call: how a server-side turn predictor should compose with Pipecat's VAD-driven manual commit, plus a guard so the strategy is only sent to a model that predicts turns*
- [elevenlabs/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Realtime diarization is not reachable, and its transcript messages are unhandled — the realtime endpoint takes `diarize` and answers with `diarized_transcript` and `speakers_merged`, both of which fall into the unknown-message branch of `_process_response`. The batch class gained `diarize` and `speaker_format`, so the two classes now disagree — *needs a call: whether realtime speaker runs map onto `TranscriptionSpeaker` the way the batch class does, given `speakers_merged` can relabel speakers after their text was already pushed*
- [elevenlabs/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/elevenlabs/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `clone_strength` is not reachable from Settings on either class (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — added to TTS `voice_settings` on 2026-10-26; controls how closely a v4 Professional Voice Clone follows its training samples (0-1). Since 2026-11-02 v4 PVC fine-tuning is open to all workspaces and a request that sets neither `clone_strength` nor `use_pvc_as_ivc` falls back to the clone's saved value; on 2026-11-09 the Agents Platform gained the same setting. ElevenLabsDialogueTTSService already has it as a Settings field with a v4-only guard; the two classes here share `build_elevenlabs_voice_settings`, whose key list is hard-coded — *needs a call: the field is v4-only and the WebSocket endpoint serves no v4 model and does not document `clone_strength` in its RealtimeVoiceSettings, so adding it means either an HTTP-only field or a shared builder that knows which class and model it is building for*
- [fal/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fal/image/2026-11-23.md) — `format` is SDXL's field name and is silently ignored on fal's other image endpoints (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — widened this run — the two newest text-to-image endpoints confirm the split, `microsoft/mai-image-2.7/text-to-image` names the field `output_format` and `kling/v4/text-to-image` has no format field and no `image_size`, `seed`, `num_inference_steps` or `enable_safety_checker` either; `fal-ai/nano-banana-2`, `fal-ai/z-image/turbo`, `fal-ai/gpt-image-1.5`, `fal-ai/flux/schnell` and `fal-ai/flux/dev` all take `output_format`, and `fal-ai/bytedance/seedream/v4.5/text-to-image` has no format field at all — *needs a call: whether the service should map the field per endpoint, add a parallel `output_format` setting, or stay SDXL-shaped and say so in the docstring*
- [fireworks/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fireworks/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `build_chat_completion_params` drops `seed` and `max_completion_tokens`, which Fireworks now supports (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the override was written when Fireworks rejected these parameters; the current chat-completions reference documents both (plus `service_tier`, where only `priority` has an effect), and a probe with all three set via `extra` succeeds. Set through `Settings`, they are silently dropped. — *needs a call: dropping the override makes two previously ignored Settings fields start taking effect for every Fireworks user, which is more than a value change*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `drama-3` performs directions, sounds and paired tags but gets no expression adapter (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `get_expression_adapter()` returns the S2 adapter only for models starting with `s2`, so `drama-3` falls back to `BaseExpressionAdapter` and performs nothing; drama-3's markup is its own — plain-language directions in square brackets, bracketed sounds and pauses, and seven paired tags (`<whisper>`, `<emphasis>`, `<soft>`, `<fast>`, `<slow>`, `<stress>`, `<forceful>`) that wrap the words they shape — *needs a call: whether drama-3 earns a second Fish adapter plus its own example and manifest entry, given the paired-tag syntax differs from S2's bracket tags*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Pronunciation dictionaries are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `TTSRequest.pronunciation_dictionary` takes up to 3 dictionaries in either a reference form (`{id, version}`) or an inline form (`{items: [{key, value, case_sensitive?}]}`), mutually exclusive in one request; the `/v1/pronunciation-dictionaries` CRUD and version endpoints that mint the reference IDs are in the public spec — *needs a call: a stance on which form Settings should carry, or whether to model both, since they cannot both be set in one request*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Fish's word-timestamp WebSocket endpoint is not used, so the service emits no word timestamps (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `wss://api.fish.audio/v1/tts/live/with-timestamp` takes the same start request and returns `audio` events carrying a cumulative `alignment.segments` snapshot per `chunk_seq` plus `chunk_audio_offset_sec`; `TTSService.add_word_timestamps` is the hook, but the service connects to the plain `/v1/tts/live` and never parses alignment — *needs a call: whether word timestamps are worth a second endpoint and cumulative-alignment bookkeeping, or whether the service should switch to the timestamped endpoint outright*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Inline voice references and multi-speaker synthesis are not expressible (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the start request takes `references` (inline zero-shot cloning) and accepts an array of model IDs in `reference_id` for multi-speaker dialogue with `<|speaker:N|>` text tags on the S2 family and `drama-3`; `Settings.voice` is a single string and nothing sends `references` — *needs a call: a stance on multi-speaker TTS — whether `voice` may hold a list and where reference audio bytes belong in Settings*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-23.md) — SenseVoice emotion and audio-event markers reach the LLM as emoji in the transcript (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `emo_dict`/`event_dict` in funasr 1.4.29's `postprocess_utils.py` still map `<|HAPPY|>`/`<|Laughter|>`/`<|BGM|>`/`<|Applause|>` to emoji, and `run_stt` calls `rich_transcription_postprocess` unconditionally, so `TranscriptionFrame.text` can be `🎼Play some music.` and flows into the context and back through TTS. A setting to drop non-speech markers, or stripping them before the frame, would fix it. SenseVoice's own `ban_emo_unk` is not a fix — it bans the emotion *unknown* token, so it yields more emoji, not fewer. — *needs a call: whether non-speech markers should be stripped by default or kept behind an opt-in, since the emotion/event labels are the reason some users pick SenseVoice*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Models can only be downloaded from ModelScope; FunASR's `hub="hf"` is unreachable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `AutoModel` documents `hub="ms"|"hf"` (default `ms`, propagated to VAD/punc/speaker sub-models) alongside `model_revision`, `ncpu` and `trust_remote_code`; the service passes only `model`, `device` and `disable_update`, so users restricted to HuggingFace mirrors cannot load a model. The probe's own run logs `download models from model hub: ms`. — *needs a call: whether this is a constructor argument next to `device` or a Settings field, given the model is loaded once in `__init__`*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `Settings(language=...)` is never sent to Gladia, so it silently does nothing (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_prepare_settings` builds the init body from `language_config` only; `s.language` is converted by the base class and then dropped, with no warning. Re-probed this run: `--setting language=fr` on an English clip returned the English transcript unchanged. docs.pipecat.ai documents the field as "Not sent by this service, so it has no effect". Keeps `language_to_gladia_language` off every request path, which is why the Burmese code on the branch had gone unnoticed. — *needs a call: a stance on precedence — whether `language` should populate `language_config.languages` when `language_config` is also set, or be rejected/warned instead*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Gemini's `custom_vocabulary` is not reachable from Settings; the service sends the deprecated `adaptation_phrases` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `custom_vocabulary` (up to 1000 terms, best under ~100) is the only biasing field the Live transcription docs document; `adaptation_phrases` is still marked "Deprecated" in google-genai 3.2.0, so nothing breaks yet. — *needs a call: whether to rename `adaptation_phrases` with a deprecation, add `custom_vocabulary` alongside it, or keep the Settings name and just send `custom_vocabulary` on the wire*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The gemini-3.6-flash default is the fastest of the candidates but the only one with a sub-99% aiewf-eval score; gemini-4-hydrogen is now Google's migration target from gemini-3.8-flash, which the Vertex subclass defaults to (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Interleaved medians through this class on a trivial prompt: gemini-3.6-flash TTFAT 1025 ms (5 runs, 685-1086 ms), gemini-4-hydrogen 1218 ms (5 runs, 1028-1603 ms, with the branch's thinking_level low), gemini-3.8-flash 1499 ms (3 runs, 1475-1693 ms); thinking_ms 2 throughout and no failures. aiewf-eval medium-context is unchanged since the last run - gemini-3.8-flash (low) 99.9% / 1411 ms P50, gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) 95.1% / 808 ms - and has no Gemini 4 row at all. Google's Hydrogen guide documents a migration from Gemini 3.8 Flash and earlier Gemini 3 Flash models but names no retirement date; none of the three has a shutdown date. The same question is open on google/llm and google/interactions-vertex-llm. — *needs a call: wait for an aiewf-eval row for gemini-4-hydrogen before touching the default, or settle the 3.6-vs-3.8 split across the four Gemini LLM classes now?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — temperature and top_p are forwarded to the Interactions API, which deprecates them and whose Gemini 3.8 and Gemini 4 backends ignore them (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `_build_request` warns when top_k, frequency_penalty or presence_penalty is set (the Interactions API has no such field) but puts temperature and top_p into generation_config silently. Google's Gemini 4 Hydrogen guide repeats the Gemini 3.8 Flash guide's wording: temperature, top_k and top_p are ignored and should be removed from requests. A probe with temperature=0.3 and top_p=0.9 still returns 200, so this is silent in both directions. — *needs a call: extend the existing _warn_unsupported path to temperature and top_p on Gemini 3.8 and later, or only document the deprecation on the Settings fields?*
- [google/interactions-vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — gemini-4-hydrogen (GA 2026-11-12) is faster than the gemini-3.8-flash default, which Google now calls previous-generation, but aiewf-eval has not benchmarked any Gemini 4 model — Interleaved medians of 5 through this class on a trivial prompt: gemini-4-hydrogen TTFAT 1150 ms (spread 878-1363 ms) against gemini-3.8-flash 1573 ms (spread 1297-2477 ms), 5/5 ok each. Google does not designate a Flash successor: the model page calls gemini-3.8-flash "previous-generation" and Hydrogen "cutting-edge intelligence for everyone", and 3.8 Flash's retirement is 2027-09-02 or later. aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard has no gemini-4 row, so the quality condition cannot be checked; on a reasoning prompt Hydrogen is also slower until it gets a reasoning default (see the item above). — *needs a call: re-read the leaderboard each run and propose the bump once a gemini-4-hydrogen row lands at or above gemini-3.8-flash's 99.9%, or does Pipecat wait for Google to name a Flash successor?*
- [google/interactions-vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — probe.py cannot construct GoogleInteractionsVertexLLMService, so this unit has no first-tier probe (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Unchanged this run. `build_kwargs` follows `**kwargs` into base classes, finds GoogleInteractionsLLMService's required `api_key` and passes GOOGLE_API_KEY, which this subclass rejects with ValueError; with the variable unset it exits 2 instead. The registry entry's include_params (credentials, project_id) map to GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS and GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID, while CI exports GOOGLE_VERTEX_TEST_CREDENTIALS and GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT_ID. Every probe below ran through a scratch wrapper that drops the api_key kwarg and maps those two names. The same gap is open on google/vertex-llm. — *needs a call: should build_kwargs skip inherited parameters a subclass rejects, and should the Vertex credential variables CI exports be the names probe.py looks for?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — gemini-3.8-flash (GA 2026-09-02) supersedes the gemini-3.6-flash default and outscores it on aiewf-eval, but is still ~45% slower in probes (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — The quality half of the bump passes — aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard has gemini-3.8-flash (low) at 99.9% pass against gemini-3.6-flash (minimal) at 95.1%. Latency still fails, re-measured this run: TTFAT 1287 ms vs 898 ms (median of 5, trivial prompt) and 1397 ms vs 869 ms (median of 3, river-crossing prompt), thinking_ms 0 throughout. 3.8 Flash's floor is thinking_level low; it has no minimal level. — *needs a call: is a ~400 ms TTFAT regression worth 99.9% over 95.1% on the benchmark, or does the default stay on the faster model?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — temperature/top_p/top_k are deprecated on Gemini 3.x and silently ignored, but LLMSettings still forwards them without a warning (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — Google's Gemini 3.8 Flash guide says the backend ignores temperature, top_k and top_p, so a user tuning them gets a 200 and no effect. `_build_generation_params` forwards all three whenever set. The harder cases (candidate_count, frequency_penalty, presence_penalty) raise an active API error, but the service never forwards those - only `extra` can reach them. — *needs a call: mirror `_warn_if_thinking_budget_ignored` with a per-model warning, or only document the deprecation on the Settings fields?*
- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Live transcription options are not reachable; both transcription configs are sent empty (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_connect` builds a bare `AudioTranscriptionConfig()` for input and output, so nothing shapes the `TranscriptionFrame`s the service emits. google-genai 3.2.0 carries nine fields on that type (`language_codes`, `language_hints`, `language_auto`, `custom_vocabulary`, `adaptation_phrases`, `word_timestamp`, `diarization`, `mode`, `source_text`); the API reference publishes `customVocabulary`, `wordTimestamp`, `diarization`, `sourceText` and `mode` (VERBATIM/SMART). The capabilities guide singles out `custom_vocabulary` (up to 1,000 terms, best around 100) for proper nouns. `GeminiSTTService` has the mirror-image gap on its own Settings. — *needs a call: a shared transcription settings shape with GeminiSTTService, or a Gemini-Live-only field?*
- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The service builds its setup through the deprecated `LiveConnectConfig.generation_config` wrapper (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — google-genai 3.2.0 still warns on every connect that the field is "deprecated ... removed in the next major version (not before 7/31/2026)" — so the removal now lands in 4.x, which our `<4` cap keeps out. Most of what `_connect` puts in `GenerationConfig` has a top-level `LiveConnectConfig` home; `frequency_penalty` and `presence_penalty` do not. — *needs a call: where `frequency_penalty` and `presence_penalty` go once the wrapper is dropped — deprecate them on Settings, or keep the wrapper until the SDK offers a home?*
- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Hybrid VAD is unreachable; the service never sends `audio_stream_end` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — The capabilities guide documents hybrid VAD as the latency-preferred shape — leave server VAD on for speech start and as a fallback, and have the client's VAD send `audio_stream_end` at end of speech so the server finalizes without waiting out its silence timer. `audio_stream_end` is a field on `LiveSendRealtimeInputParameters` and `audioStreamEnd` is published in the API reference, but it appears nowhere in the service, which offers only server-VAD-only or fully manual activity_start/end turns (where the guide notes the server prepends no pre-speech audio). Pipecat pipelines already run a local VAD. `LiveConnectConfig.explicit_vad_signal` still has no send-side counterpart and no published message. — *needs a call: whether the upstream VAD's stop signal should drive `audio_stream_end`, given the service deliberately emits no turn frames of its own*
- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Changing the system instruction or tools tears down the session instead of sending an in-session context update (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `_handle_context` calls `_reconnect()` when the effective system instruction differs from the one the session was configured with, or when the context brings tools — dropping the socket and re-seeding history mid-call. `session.send_context_update(system_instruction=..., tools=...)` exists in google-genai 3.2.0 and is processed in order with the rest of the inputs. The Live API reference still publishes no context-update message, and the SDK warns that updating the preamble invalidates the prefix cache. — *needs a call: is an in-session update worth probing as a replacement for the reconnect path, given the API reference has not published the message?*
- [google/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Default `latest_long` is a V1 model tag; the V2 model table lists `long` instead (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the API still accepts `latest_long` (probe ok, TTFB 1030 ms) and `long` is at parity (1079 ms, identical transcript, same 63 interim frames); the V2 comparison table lists only chirp_3/chirp_2/telephony, so it names neither tag, and Google still designates no successor to `latest_long` and schedules no retirement — *needs a call: a call on whether `long` covers the same languages as the undocumented `latest_long` alias before changing the model under every existing deployment*
- [google/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Selecting `chirp_3` needs a non-global `location`, and streaming returns no interim transcripts (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — chirp_3 is GA in the `us` and `eu` multi-regions only; at the service's default `location="global"` today's probe fails again with 400 "The model \"chirp_3\" does not exist in the location named \"global\"", and at location="us" it transcribes but emits only the final frame — *needs a call: a decision on whether the service should steer users (the `location` docstring, a location check) or leave Chirp 3 selection entirely to the caller*
- [google/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Streaming endpointing controls `endpointing_sensitivity` and `voice_activity_timeout` are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `StreamingRecognitionFeatures` carries both alongside the `enable_voice_activity_events` and `interim_results` we already set; the Chirp 3 page documents STANDARD/SHORT/SUPERSHORT sensitivity as a latency-accuracy trade — *needs a call: both need conversion (a sensitivity enum, Duration timeouts) and overlap with Pipecat's own VAD and turn strategies, so exposing them needs a stance on which layer owns endpointing*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The GenAI backend still drops `multi_speaker`, though the Gemini API supports two speakers on 3.8 TTS models (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `multi_speaker` is warned away in `_warn_unsupported_genai_settings` and never set by `_run_genai_tts`, while the speech-generation reference documents up to two prebuilt speakers per request via `generation_config.speech_config.speakers` with a `speech_metadata.speaker` on every turn; a two-speaker request through the installed google-genai returned audio from gemini-3.8-flash-tts on 2026-10-12. — *needs a call: `speaker_configs` is `{speaker_alias, speaker_id}` over one undivided text string - mapping it onto the Gemini API needs a way to label which part of the text each speaker says, so what shape should the setting take?*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — GoogleHttpTTSService sends plain text for Chirp voices, so its default voice ignores every SSML setting (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `run_tts` skips `_construct_ssml` whenever the voice name contains "chirp", and the default voice is `en-US-Chirp3-HD-Charon` - so `pitch`, `rate`, `volume`, `emphasis` and `gender` are silently dropped out of the box. Chirp 3 HD takes SSML on the synchronous endpoint (probed 2026-09-01: 160416 bytes with a `prosody` wrapper against 121004 unwrapped). SSML is still unsupported for streaming, so GoogleTTSService is unaffected. — *needs a call: Chirp 3 HD rejects `interpret-as="expletive"`/`"bleep"` and Journey is untested, so dropping the branch needs a stance on which voices still take the plain-text path*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `custom_pronunciations` is not reachable from Settings on either Cloud TTS class (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — IPA/X-SAMPA pronunciation overrides - the usual fix for brand and person names in a voice agent. The field is on both request types in the pinned SDK (`SynthesisInput.custom_pronunciations`, `StreamingSynthesizeConfig.custom_pronunciations`) and both were probed accepted on 2026-09-01. — *needs a call: the field is a nested proto (phrase, phonetic_encoding, pronunciation), so a Settings field needs a shape - a list of dicts converted in the request builder, or the proto type exposed directly?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — gemini-4-hydrogen is Google's designated successor to the gemini-3.8-flash default and is faster on Vertex, but aiewf-eval has not benchmarked it — GA on Vertex 2026-11-12; its model guide carries a migration guide `from Gemini 3.8 Flash and earlier Gemini 3 Flash models` and Google's press coverage calls it the Flash successor, with the Flash name retired. With the branch's thinking_level low it is faster than the current default on Vertex: interleaved medians of 5, TTFAT 965 ms (spread 754-1162 ms) against 1170 ms (1143-1357 ms) for gemini-3.8-flash. aiewf-eval's leaderboard-medium-context has no row for any gemini-4 model, so the quality side of a default bump cannot be checked yet; re-read the leaderboard next run. List price is roughly double gemini-3.8-flash's 2026 rate, which is a separate consideration for a default. — *needs a call: nothing to decide until aiewf-eval publishes a gemini-4-hydrogen row - but is a doubled list price on its own a reason not to make a successor the default?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Vertex AI express mode (API-key auth) cannot be used; the constructor rejects api_key and requires project_id (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — google-genai builds an express-mode client from `Client(vertexai=True, api_key=...)` with no project or location. `GoogleVertexLLMService.__init__` raises ValueError when api_key is passed and makes project_id a required keyword, so an express-mode key has no way in. Commit f99c81809 made GoogleLLMService keep the SDK's Vertex default when GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_VERTEXAI or GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_ENTERPRISE is set, so express mode now works through that class via the environment - which is a workaround, not a path through this one. — *needs a call: is the api_key guard deliberate protection against confusing this class with GoogleLLMService, or should it fall through to express mode when no service-account credentials are given?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — probe.py can no longer construct GoogleVertexLLMService, so this unit has no first-tier probe (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `build_kwargs` inserts api_key whenever `_init_parameters` finds it, and that walk follows `**kwargs` into GoogleLLMService, where api_key is required. This subclass raises ValueError on api_key. probe.py also maps credentials to GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS and project_id to GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID; CI exports GOOGLE_VERTEX_TEST_CREDENTIALS and GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT_ID, and GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS is empty, so the probe exits 2 on GOOGLE_PROJECT_ID before it gets that far. Every probe below ran through a scratch wrapper that drops the api_key kwarg and bridges the two names. — *needs a call: should build_kwargs skip inherited parameters a subclass rejects (and should a registry entry's include_params be the whole list when it has one), or is a per-unit wrapper the accepted answer here?*
- [google/vertex-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The Vertex override that suppresses NON_BLOCKING tool declarations is obsolete on 3.8 Live (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `_supports_non_blocking_tools` still returns False (added 2026-05-08 against the 2.5 endpoint), so Vertex sessions send no `behavior` on function declarations and no `scheduling` on responses; the 3.8 migration guide still documents `behavior=BLOCKING` and the changed `scheduling=interrupt` semantics for Vertex, and a probe with the override lifted called the tool fine (2026-10-12). Left as is, a synchronous tool takes 3.8's server-side asynchronous default instead of the BLOCKING declaration the Gemini API class sends — *needs a call: whether to version-gate the override at 3.8 rather than drop it, and whether `scheduling=interrupt`'s changed meaning on 3.8 (waits for the user to finish) needs the service to pick different values*
