Magistral models stream plain strings and are unaffected. — *needs a call: exposing the field is the easy half; the content-part shape has to be flattened in the OpenAI base path, which touches every OpenAI-compatible service — someone has to decide where that handling lives.*
- [mistral/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `service_tier` is accepted by the inherited constructor but never sent — `MistralLLMService.build_chat_completion_params` omits it (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Mistral's API reference documents `service_tier` with values `auto` and `standard_only`; our override drops the value silently, where `BaseOpenAILLMService` would have sent `self._service_tier`. — *needs a call: Mistral's enum (`auto`/`standard_only`) differs from the OpenAI values the shared constructor argument documents, so wiring it through needs a call on whether to reuse the argument or give Mistral its own typed setting.*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Realtime 3's session fields `context_bias`, `diarize` and `speaker_references` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the `mistralai<3` pin that blocked these is gone (now `>=2.0.0,<4`), and 3.8.0's `connect()` takes all three; `context_bias` (a list of keyphrases) is the one that maps cleanly to a Settings field, `diarize`/`speaker_references` imply speaker-labelled output Pipecat has nowhere to put — *needs a call: under the locked 2.10.1 environment a new field would be silently dropped, so a pass-through needs the same `inspect.signature` gate `language` uses — or the lockfile moved first; which of the two is the call*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Realtime 3's built-in end-of-turn detection is not wired into Pipecat's turn strategies (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — SDK 3.x exposes a `turn_detection` session config and `turn.start` / `turn.eager_end` / `turn.resume` / `turn.end` events; the service pushes neither, and today it flushes audio off VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame. Mistral documents `turn_detection` as incompatible with `diarize`. — *needs a call: whether a provider-side turn signal should drive a user turn start/stop strategy, which is a design call beyond a Settings field*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Voxtral 3 speech parameters (expressiveness, phoneme_hints, conversation_history) are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the SDK pin now allows 3.x, and `expressiveness` already reaches the API on the locked mistralai 2.10.1 through `complete_async(additional_properties=...)` — the server validates it (0.5 is rejected with `greater_than_equal` ge 1.0) — but voxtral-mini-tts-2603, today's default, rejects it with 400 `expressiveness is not supported for this model`. `phoneme_hints` is a nested word -> IPA-phone mapping and `conversation_history` is restricted to preset `pvc` voices. — *needs a call: whether to expose a parameter the shipped default model rejects, or hold it until the default moves to Voxtral TTS 3*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Mistral's one-off reference audio (`ref_audio`, zero-shot voice cloning) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `ref_audio` (base64 clip) is an alternative to `voice_id` on both the pinned 2.10.1 and the current 3.8.0 speech request; a Settings field passed into `complete_async` would cover it — *needs a call: `run_tts` calls `assert_given(self._settings.voice)`, so voice is mandatory today; exposing `ref_audio` needs a stance on making voice optional and on which wins when both are set*
- [moondream/vision](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moondream/vision/2026-11-23.md) — The Moondream 3 line is not reachable from MoondreamService (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — moondream3.1-9B-A2B (unchanged since 2026-07-08) still ships config.json + model.safetensors with no auto_map, so trust_remote_code loading cannot work. moondream3-preview (unchanged since 2026-04-09) does carry hf_moondream.HfMoondream, but load_model's Transformers5Moondream shim is moondream2-shaped: it copies a 2-D tril into module.attn_mask, where moondream3's buffer is 4-D. — *needs a call: whether to add a Photon-based backend or wait for a transformers-loadable Moondream 3 release*
- [moondream/vision](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moondream/vision/2026-11-23.md) — `revision` is hard-coded to the moondream2 tag 2025-06-21 for every model (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the constructor's revision default is a tag that only exists in vikhyatk/moondream2, so Settings(model="moondream/moondream3-preview") fails to resolve unless the caller also passes revision=None — *needs a call: whether revision should move into Settings or default to None for non-moondream2 repos*
- [moondream/vision](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moondream/vision/2026-11-23.md) — Moondream's `query` affordances (reasoning, sampling settings, streaming) are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — query(image, question, reasoning, spatial_refs, stream, settings) is unchanged at the pinned revision; MoondreamSettings adds no field beyond model, and run_vision calls query(image_embeds, text) positionally then yields one VisionTextFrame after the whole answer — *needs a call: whether reasoning and sampling belong in Settings, and whether streaming should change the emitted frame shape*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Keyterm biasing (`set_keyterms` / `set_context`) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — moonshine-voice 0.1.6 `Transcriber.set_keyterms(list)` and `set_context(text, max_terms)` bias decoding toward supplied terms; neither is called, and `keyterm_boost` (default 2.0, ineffective above 4.0) sets the strength at load time — *needs a call: both raise on non-streaming architectures (`tiny`, `base`), so exposing them needs a stance on what happens when the configured model cannot bias*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Alphanumeric spelling mode is never enabled although its model is downloaded (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `get_model_for_language` pre-fetches the language's `spelling_cnn.ort` best-effort, but `Transcriber` is built without `spelling_model_path` and `transcribe_without_streaming` runs with the default `flags=0`, so `MOONSHINE_FLAG_SPELLING_MODE` never applies — *needs a call: whether spelling fusion should be a Settings flag or on by default, and whether it costs latency on the CPU path*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Transcription is batch-only; Moonshine's streaming API and its partial results are unused (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the service is a `SegmentedSTTService` calling `transcribe_without_streaming` once per VAD segment; `Transcriber.add_audio` plus a listener emits interim text at `transcription_interval`, and 0.1.6 adds `create_stream`/`get_default_stream` for concurrent streams — *needs a call: a decision on whether Moonshine gets a second, streaming service class alongside the segmented one*
- [nebius/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nebius/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507 is unbenchmarked while Nebius hosts models with published aiewf-eval scores (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — aiewf-eval still has no medium-context row for the default; the best-scoring Nebius-hosted row is now deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 (98.4%) but it answers at 3864 ms through our class against the default's 447 ms, and the one candidate at latency parity (thinkingmachines/Inkling-2-Small with reasoning off, 480 ms) matches a leaderboard row whose model id is not stated — *needs a call: Nebius designates no successor to the default, so picking a new one is a Pipecat quality/cost call with no latency case behind it*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Neuphonic's word timings (`timestamps` / `alignment`) are not requested or surfaced (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — added to the TTS websocket in September 2026; `TTSService.add_word_timestamps` exists, but `NeuphonicTTSService` extends `InterruptibleTTSService`, which is explicitly the base for services without word timestamps and reconnects the socket on every interruption — *needs a call: adopting timings means moving the websocket class off `InterruptibleTTSService` onto word-timestamp interruption handling — a structural change, not a value*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The WebSocket service ignores Neuphonic's `stop`, `context_id` and `<RESET>` signals and never closes its audio context (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_receive_messages` reads only `data.audio`; every other WebSocket TTS service in Pipecat calls `remove_audio_context` when the provider signals end-of-generation, and `<RESET>` would drop buffered text on interruption instead of reconnecting — *needs a call: keying audio contexts off Neuphonic's `context_id`/`stop` changes interruption and turn behavior, not just a value — needs a maintainer's call on the shape*
- [nvidia/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `/no_think` in the NVIDIA examples' system prompt does not disable reasoning (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — re-probed this run with the examples' prompt verbatim: the default still reasoned (376 ms of thinking, thought frames emitted), while the documented switch `extra_body={"chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": false}}` answered in 686 ms TTFAT with no thought frames — *needs a call: dropping the dead prefix or passing the documented switch is a choice, and aiewf-eval still has no row for super-vl-120b to say what the switch costs in quality*
- [nvidia/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/llm/2026-11-23.md) — NIM's chat_template_kwargs reasoning controls have no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `enable_thinking` is the switch the super-vl-120b model page documents, but it is reachable only as `extra={"extra_body": {"chat_template_kwargs": {...}}}`, since `Settings.extra` is spread as top-level OpenAI kwargs — *needs a call: a pass-through field would have to define what happens when a user also sets `extra_body` directly*
- [nvidia/omni-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/omni-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Omni reasons on every turn and the reasoning switch has no Settings field (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — reasoning is on by default on the model card; re-probed, a trivial prompt still spends 282 ms thinking before the first answer token (TTFAT 431 ms) while `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` answers in 191 ms with no thought frames; reachable only as `extra={"extra_body": {"chat_template_kwargs": {...}}}`, and `examples/voice/voice-nvidia-omni.py` sets neither — *needs a call: a pass-through field has to define what happens when a user also sets `extra_body` directly (nvidia/llm tracks the same question), and no benchmark says what capping Omni's reasoning costs in quality*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — docs.pipecat.ai still documents canary-1b-asr and its retired function id as the segmented default — the NVIDIA STT page gives "canary-1b-asr" / b0e8b4a5-217c-40b7-9b96-17d84e666317 for `NvidiaSegmentedSTTService`; that hosted API stopped serving on 2026-11-23, so anyone copying the page's defaults gets a gRPC NOT_FOUND — *needs a call: the docs live in the docs repo — the page needs the same change as the branch once it merges*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — NvidiaSTTService never applies the NVIDIA locale map, so base language codes reach the NIM unmapped (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `language_to_nvidia_nemotron_speech_language` is defined in `nvidia/stt.py` but only `NvidiaSegmentedSTTService` wires it into `language_to_service_language`; the streaming class passes the enum value straight into `language_code`, so `Language.PT` goes out as `pt` where the support matrix only names `pt-BR` and `pt-PT`. The hosted streaming endpoint tolerates `language=en`, so the remaining risk is unmapped locales on self-hosted multilingual NIMs — *needs a call: sign-off on the behavior change — base codes would start resolving to locales (EN to en-US, PT to pt-BR, ES to es-ES) for deployments that rely on today's pass-through*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The locale map covers 13 of the 40 language-locales Nemotron ASR Streaming supports (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — mapped locales are ar-AR, de-DE, en-GB, en-US, es-ES, es-US, fr-FR, hi-IN, it-IT, ja-JP, ko-KR, pt-BR, ru-RU; six of Nemotron's 19 transcription-ready locales (nl-NL, fr-CA, pt-PT, tr-TR, uk-UA, vi-VN) and both lower tiers fall back to a bare base code, and the segmented service's new multilingual Parakeet default reaches 27 locales of which the map names 13 — *needs a call: verification requires a self-hosted multi-type NIM — the hosted streaming deployment is en-US only, so added entries cannot be probed*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Settings.model is never sent in the Riva RecognitionConfig, so self-hosted multi-model NIMs cannot be told which model to use (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — the streaming `_create_recognition_config` pins `model=""` and the segmented one omits the field; on the hosted NVCF path the function id selects the model, and a 2026-09-14 ad-hoc probe had a hosted deployment answer INVALID_ARGUMENT for a model name it does not serve, so a plain pass-through would break hosted users — *needs a call: whether to send `Settings.model` only when the caller overrode it or when `server` is not the NVCF endpoint — either way it needs a rule for telling the two deployment shapes apart*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — NvidiaSageMakerSTTSettings exposes none of the NIM realtime session options (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — the class adds no fields of its own, and `_send_session_config` hard-codes `recognition_config.enable_automatic_punctuation: true` and sends nothing else; NIM's realtime `transcription_session.update` also takes `max_alternatives`, `enable_word_time_offsets`, `enable_profanity_filter`, `enable_verbatim_transcripts`, `speaker_diarization`, `word_boosting` and `endpointing_config`, all of which the gRPC classes already expose — *needs a call: a deployed SageMaker endpoint to probe the pass-through against — no NVIDIA SageMaker endpoint name is configured in this environment*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The sagemaker extra pins aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2 below 0.10, five releases behind PyPI (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `pyproject.toml` still has `aws_sdk_sagemaker_runtime_http2>=0.6.0,<0.10`; 0.10.0, 0.11.0, 0.12.0 and now 0.13.0/0.13.1 (2026-11-18) are excluded. The package calls itself experimental with breaking changes expected before 1.0, so the pin is defensible, but it has not moved since August — *needs a call: whether the bidi-stream client in `services/aws/sagemaker/bidi_client.py` still works on 0.13 — no SageMaker endpoint here to run it against*
- [nvidia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Magpie's word timestamps (`enable_word_time_offsets`) are neither requested nor consumed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the customization docs still document per-word start/end times in `meta.words` for Magpie models (streaming returns one consolidated timeline on the final response); `_build_base_request` never sets the flag and both response loops read only `item.audio` — *needs a call: a call on wiring Riva's streaming response metadata into TTSService's word-timestamp API (start/reset/add), which is more than a request-field pass-through*
- [ollama/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ollama/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default model llama2 predates tool calling and is not what Ollama tells users to run (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Still reproduces on 218a7f836: `__init__` hardcodes `self.Settings(model="llama2")` and a construction check prints `default model: llama2`. llama2 remains pullable (library page still "Updated 2 years ago", tags latest/7b/13b/70b, text-only, 4K context, no tools capability, no deprecation notice) and has slipped one more place to #26 by pulls (7.7M), so `OLLamaLLMService()` with no settings cannot do function calling. Every in-tree caller overrides it (examples use `llama3.2` and `llama3.1:8b`, the eval judge and CLI templates `gemma4:12b`). aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard is still 50 rows with no llama row and no Ollama-served row, so no candidate can be ranked the way an LLM default bump requires. — *needs a call: Ollama designates no successor and the default only matters for a model the user has already pulled — does a local-runtime service want an opinionated default at all, or should the model become required?*
- [openai/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/image/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-image-2.5-flare predates gpt-image-2.6-flare (released 2026-11-04) (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — 2.6 is the replacement OpenAI names for every image model it has scheduled for shutdown (gpt-image-1-mini, gpt-image-1.5 and chatgpt-image-latest on 2026-12-01, gpt-image-2 and its snapshot newly dated 2027-02-10), and 2.6-flare generated in half the time of 2.5-flare in this run's probe; 2.5-flare is still undeprecated and its model page still calls it "Fast, high-quality everyday image generation", the guide's examples still use 2.5, and `quality` values `xhigh`/`max` remain documented for the 2.5 models only — *needs a call: a call on whether to follow OpenAI's replacement recommendation while 2.5-flare is still current, given that a bump would reject the `xhigh`/`max` quality tiers users can set today*
- [openai/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/image/2026-11-23.md) — Images API request parameters background, output_format, output_compression, n and stream/partial_images are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — none is a bare pass-through - `background=transparent` constrains `output_format`, `output_compression` is only valid with webp/jpeg, and `n`/`stream` change what `run_image_gen` yields (it sends `n=1` and reads `data[0]`); 2.6 adds the same shape again with `output_format=svg|gif` plus `svg`, `frame_count` and `frame_duration_ms`, which no longer decode into a `URLImageRawFrame` — *needs a call: a call on whether image generation should yield more than one frame per prompt and whether non-raster output (SVG, animated GIF) is in scope at all*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Default gpt-live-1 is behind gpt-live-1.1, the only GPT-Live model OpenAI now lists — gpt-live-1.1 shipped 2026-11-19 with better pronunciation, accent consistency and multilingual transcription, at the same $0.05/min active price, and the models index now lists it as the single GPT-Live entry while gpt-live-1 has dropped out of the index (its model page survives and the deprecations page names neither). `probe.py run --model gpt-live-1 --model gpt-live-1.1` passes for both, and a raw session.start on gpt-live-1.1 is accepted with the same config the service sends (audio echoed back as `{type: audio/pcm, rate: 24000}`, voice marin). Two of the three bump criteria cannot be evidenced here: probe.py is connect-only for realtime so it reports no latency, and aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard is text-mode only — neither gpt-live-1 nor gpt-live-1.1 has a row — *needs a call: whether dropping out of the models index counts as OpenAI designating gpt-live-1.1 the successor, given gpt-live-1 is undeprecated and the two models hold separate concurrent-session limits, so a bump moves existing bots onto a different quota*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Outbound SIP calling cannot be started or attached to (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — an outbound call is placed with `POST /v1/live/sessions` carrying `transport.type: "sip"` plus `transport.destination` and `transport.trunk`, and the app then attaches to `wss://api.openai.com/v1/live/sessions/{id}/attach` **without** sending `session.start`. The service only ever dials `base_url` over WebSocket and sends `session.start` from `_send_session_config`, so neither half is reachable; `transport.ringing` / `transport.answered` / `transport.failed` have no models in events.py either. The endpoint is live on this account (checked last run: a private-IP trunk is refused by parameter name). The installed openai SDK 3.46.0 also carries `session_accept_params`, `session_reject_params` and `session_refer_params`, so inbound answer/reject and call transfer sit on the same unreachable surface. In a SIP session the audio never touches the pipeline, leaving Pipecat as the control plane (client-delegation backend, tools, transcripts) — *needs a call: whether Pipecat wants a mode where OpenAI owns the call audio and the pipeline carries no audio frames, given it needs session creation over REST, an attach-without-start path and transport events — and whether that same attach path should also serve forking*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Prompt-generated custom voices cannot be selected (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — the Live API types a voice as built-in-name-or-object — openai SDK 3.46.0 generates `AudioOutputVoice = BuiltInVoice | CustomVoiceParam` from the OpenAPI spec, where `CustomVoiceParam` is `{"id": str}` and `BuiltInVoice` is the 23 named voices — while `OpenAILiveLLMSettings.voice` and `events.AudioOutputConfig.voice` are both `str | None`. Probed last run: the object form is validated as a voice reference ("No voice found with id") and the same id as a plain string is refused as an unknown built-in, so widening the type is the only way in — *needs a call: whether `voice` widens to `str | <typed ref>` or `str | dict`, and whether the docs' advice to repeat the voice description in `instructions` should be reflected in the service*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Decisions transcript triggers are not expressible (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `session.triggers` runs a Decisions question over every `session.input_transcript.delta` or `session.output_transcript.delta` and returns the answers as `decision.event`, which is how an avatar picks an expression or gesture while the conversation runs. It is a top-level session field in the SDK's generated `session_config_param.py`; `SessionConfig` has no `triggers` field and events.py has no `decision.event` model, so a session started this way is accepted (the server echoed `triggers` back) but nothing in the pipeline would see the answers — *needs a call: where trigger answers should surface — a new frame, an event handler, or the thinking channel — since the config half is useless without a route for the results*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Pipecat's reasoning defaults are not applied to the delegated backend model (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `_responses_delegation_config` sends `reasoning` only when the caller configured it, while `OpenAIResponsesLLMService` fills in the model's `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` entry (the lowest documented effort) when it is unset. A delegation to `gpt-6-sol` therefore thinks at the server's default effort where the same settings object through the Responses service would send `effort: none` — *needs a call: whether the delegated path should adopt the reasoning-defaults table, which changes latency and cost for existing Responses-delegation bots that never set reasoning*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Developer and system messages the app appends mid-session never reach the API (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `_handle_context` deliberately forwards nothing from a later context, because user and assistant messages cannot be told apart from the service's own transcript recordings. A newly appended developer or system message can only have come from the app, and `instructions` stays appendable for the life of the session via `session.instructions.append` — the event class already exists in events.py and the service never sends it — *needs a call: whether the service should diff each incoming context for new developer/system messages, and whether they belong in instructions or in the thinking channel*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — GPT-6.2 models cannot call functions through OpenAILLMService — gpt-6.2-astra and gpt-6.2-sol are OpenAI's recommended lineup since 2026-11-17 and answer plain text fine through this class, but a request carrying tools returns 400 "Function tools with reasoning_effort are not supported for <model> in /v1/chat/completions. To use function tools, use /v1/responses or set reasoning_effort to 'none'." — and neither model accepts reasoning_effort 'none' (supported: low, medium, high, xhigh). So the escape hatch the error names does not exist, and function calling on GPT-6.2 is only reachable through OpenAIResponsesLLMService. Nothing in the service or its docstrings says so. — *needs a call: whether to say this in the service docstring and point users at the Responses service, or detect the combination and raise before the request*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — No first-class reasoning-effort setting on the chat-completions service (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — effort is both the deciding latency lever and, on GPT-6.2, the gate on tool calling, yet the untyped `extra` dict is the only way to reach it. The sibling Responses service has a typed `reasoning` field. Accepted values keep diverging: gpt-6.1-terra and gpt-6-luna take `none`, gpt-6.2-astra and gpt-6.2-sol take low/medium/high/xhigh only. — *needs a call: where the field would live — `OpenAILLMService.Settings` is `BaseOpenAILLMService.Settings`, shared by every OpenAI-compatible service, and the accepted values differ per model and per provider*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `pipecat eval`'s OpenAI default, DEFAULT_OPENAI_MODEL = "gpt-4o", shuts down 2027-01-14 (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — src/pipecat/evals/services.py:352 — the model a scenario gets when its judge or persona block says `service: openai` with no model. OpenAI names gpt-6-luna as the gpt-4o replacement, but the constant decides eval verdicts. — *needs a call: which model should judge and play personas once gpt-4o is gone — the named gpt-6-luna, or a stronger tier, given the verdicts depend on it*
- [openai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The turn-completion benchmark's gpt-5-mini entry stops working on 2026-12-11 — evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml:69-72 runs OpenAILLMService with model gpt-5-mini and `extra: {reasoning_effort: minimal}`; OpenAI shuts gpt-5-mini down on 2026-12-11 and names gpt-6.1-terra as the replacement, whose lowest documented level is `none`, not `minimal` (see #6434). So it is not a string swap — the row's effort setting changes with it, and with it what the benchmark measures. Four more rows in the same file (gpt-4.1, gpt-4.1-mini, gpt-4o, gpt-4o-mini) go on 2027-01-14. — *needs a call: which model the gpt-5-mini benchmark row becomes, given the effort value changes too and the row is a comparison point across runs*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Four server-event fields the GA spec marks optional are still required, so a lawful payload kills the receive loop (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — Re-checked against openai 3.58.0, byte-identical to 3.46.0 in `openai/types/realtime/`: `ConversationItemInputAudioTranscriptionDelta` requires `content_index` and `delta` (GA: both optional, plus an unmodeled `logprobs`); `ConversationItemInputAudioTranscriptionFailed.error` is our `RealtimeError`, which requires `type` and `message` (GA gives that event its own Error with all four fields optional — a partial failure report would kill the session it is reporting on); `ItemContent.type` is a required Literal, but the GA user-message content marks `type` optional, as do `response.content_part.added`/`.done` for `part.type`; `RealtimeConversation` requires `id` and `object`, while GA's `conversation.created` marks both optional. Their models are shared with client events and the `error` event, which is why earlier work deferred them — *needs a call: whether the shared models get split (server-side copies with optional fields) or the receive loop stops raising on validation failure at all, which would subsume this and the MCP item-type gap*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — server_vad turn detection has no create_response or interrupt_response (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — GA's ServerVad carries both, and SemanticTurnDetection already does; TurnDetection does not. Unlike idle_timeout_ms, which the branch adds, these two change who drives responses — *needs a call: under server VAD the service never sends response.create itself, so create_response=false would silence the bot permanently — does the pass-through ship with the hazard documented, or with service-side handling?*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — parallel_tool_calls is not reachable from SessionProperties (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Now the only one of GA's 14 session fields we do not model — truncation landed in PR 5547. OpenAI documents it as "Only supported by reasoning Realtime models such as gpt-realtime-2"; probed in an earlier run, gpt-realtime-2.1 accepts it and gpt-realtime-1.5 fails the session with `invalid_value`, the same hazard `_strip_unsupported_reasoning` already absorbs for `reasoning` — *needs a call: whether the model-capability stripping generalizes to a second field, or parallel_tool_calls stays out until it does*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The reasoning default for gpt-6-luna is effort="none", the configuration aiewf-eval could not complete a conversation with (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `_THINKING_DEFAULTS` holds each model's lowest documented effort, so gpt-6-luna gets "none"; the aiewf-eval medium-context run of gpt-6-luna at "none" completed 0 of 30 multi-turn conversations while the same model at "low" scores 91.8%. A user who selects gpt-6-luna — the model OpenAI recommends in place of gpt-4.1 — gets "none" unless they set `reasoning` themselves — *needs a call: whether a model's documented lowest effort should give way to a working one when the lowest degrades multi-turn behavior, or whether a docstring note is enough*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The system instruction is sent as top-level `instructions`, which gpt-6.2 places after the input instead of before it — the Responses adapter sets params["instructions"] = system_instruction whenever the input list is non-empty, and falls back to a leading developer message only when the input is empty. OpenAI's GPT-6.2 guide states that models from gpt-6.2 onward place top-level `instructions` after `input`, and directs stable reusable instructions into a developer message as the first input item so they stay prompt-cacheable, reserving top-level `instructions` for request-specific guidance. Nothing breaks today — gpt-4.1 is the default — but a bot that selects a gpt-6.2 model loses prefix caching of its system prompt and has it read last — *needs a call: whether the adapter should always lead with a developer message, or switch on the model family, given it changes the request shape for every existing Responses user*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — OpenAI's mid-turn and long-running-work controls (async tool calling, mid-turn steering, configuration_update) are not supported (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — async tools set `async: true` on the tool definition and return results tagged INTERRUPT / WHEN_IDLE / SILENT, which would let a voice bot keep talking through a slow tool call; mid-turn steering is a WebSocket message the OpenAIResponsesLLMService transport could carry; the `configuration_update` input item changes reasoning.effort mid-conversation on the gpt-6 family. None is a pass-through parameter — each needs adapter and function-call-result work — *needs a call: whether any of the three is worth designing for now, and which one voice users need first*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — OpenAIRealtimeSTTService still defaults to gpt-realtime-whisper, which OpenAI's realtime transcription guide no longer lists (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the docs case is unchanged - gpt-live-transcribe is still the guide's recommended starting point, gpt-realtime-whisper is still absent from the guide, still absent from the deprecation table, and still a GA model in the 3.58.0 SDK (the delay field is documented as gpt-realtime-whisper only, in GA Realtime sessions). The latency case moved against the bump this run: interleaved --repeat 3 through the class gives gpt-realtime-whisper TTFB 1504 ms and gpt-live-transcribe 1936 ms, 29% worse and outside the 10% bar, where 2026-11-09 measured parity (701 vs 698 ms) on clean ad-hoc sessions. Both figures are polluted by the start-of-session loss above, so neither is a clean reading. gpt-live-transcribe was the more reliable of the two (3/3 against 2/3). The bump also swaps which Settings the default accepts: delay is gpt-realtime-whisper only, keywords and languages are gpt-live-transcribe and gpt-transcribe only, and gpt-live-transcribe supports no server-side turn detection — *needs a call: whether to bump once the commit-empty teardown is fixed and latency can be measured through the class without the start-of-session loss, given that it also swaps which Settings fields the default model accepts*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The languages list, and the realtime session's keywords hints, have no Settings field (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — unchanged and re-confirmed against the current guides and the 3.58.0 SDK schema (realtime transcription accepts delay, keywords, language, languages, model, prompt; file transcription adds stream, chunking_strategy, known_speaker_*). Both guides say languages replaces the singular language and that the two must not be sent together, while both services always send language, so the field has to suppress language rather than pass through. Realtime keywords is documented for gpt-transcribe and gpt-live-transcribe but not for the default gpt-realtime-whisper, so it needs the per-model omission _omit_unsupported_prompt already does for prompt — *needs a call: whether setting languages should silently drop the language the services always send, and whether realtime keywords should be silently dropped for models that reject it*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — OpenAISTTService's diarization path only matches gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize, which shuts down 2027-02-26 with no diarizing replacement named — _is_diarization_model matches on the substring 'diarize', and gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize is the only model OpenAI offers that the substring reaches. It is in the deprecation table with a 2027-02-26 shutdown, and its listed replacements (gpt-live-transcribe, gpt-transcribe) do not diarize - the speech-to-text guide itself notes its speaker-label examples use deprecated models. So speaker_format, the diarized_json branch and the service docstring's diarization example all stop working on that date. Nothing to change yet; no example pins the string, only the docstring and tests/test_openai_stt.py do — *needs a call: whether to wait for OpenAI to name a diarizing successor or to document that OpenAI diarization ends 2027-02-26 and point users at another provider*
- [openai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Custom voice IDs are rejected by the VALID_VOICES allowlist (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the SDK's `SpeechCreateParams.Voice` is `Union[str, Literal[...13 built-ins...], VoiceID]` where `VoiceID` is `{"id": "voice_1234"}`; `run_tts` yields an ErrorFrame for anything outside the 13 names, so the request never leaves the process. Prompt-created voices remain Live-only; only audio-sample custom voices work on /v1/audio/speech — *needs a call: whether to let unknown voice strings (and the custom voice object shape) through and give up the client-side typo check; it cannot be probed end to end without an account that has a custom voice*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — OpenRouter's normalized `reasoning` field is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `reasoning={enabled, effort, max_tokens, exclude, context, mode, summary}` with `effort: "none"` is how OpenRouter turns thinking off uniformly across the catalogue; `GET /models` reports per-model `supported_efforts`, `default_effort` and `mandatory`. All of it reaches the API today only through `extra={"extra_body": {...}}`. The successor OpenAI names for our default reasons by default, so the off switch bears on the default-model decision — *needs a call: OpenAI-family models also take `reasoning_effort` through `extra`, and OpenRouter accepts it as a top-level shorthand for `reasoning.effort`, so a first-class field has to say which wins*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Model-level fallback (the `models` array) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — OpenRouter retries the next model in `models` when the first errors; a voice bot would use it to survive a model outage mid-call. The companion `route` field stays documented as deprecated in favour of `provider.sort.partition`, so the gap is the array alone — *needs a call: a stance on how a fallback list interacts with `Settings.model` and with runtime model updates*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `service_tier` reaches the request only through the deprecated InputParams path (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `BaseOpenAILLMService` sends `service_tier` from the constructor argument it stores in `self._service_tier`, and `OpenAILLMSettings` still has no counterpart, so the field is neither runtime-updatable nor reachable once InputParams goes in 2.0.0. OpenRouter extends the tier vocabulary beyond OpenAI's set — *needs a call: the field lives in the OpenAI base shared by every compatible service, so promoting it to Settings is a base-class change rather than an OpenRouter one*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Service sends the deprecated `preset` field; Perplexity renamed it to `effort` (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Perplexity renamed the Agent API's `preset` to `effort` in October 2026 — "a naming change only", with `preset` kept as a deprecated alias and still no removal date announced. The API reference's request schema lists `effort` and never mentions `preset`. Pipecat writes `preset` in two places: the `PerplexityAgentLLMSettings.preset` field, forwarded into `extra_body` by `_build_response_params`, and `_THINKING_DEFAULT`, which every default request sends. Probed today, the alias is still live and still validated server-side — a default request succeeds, and `preset="pw-not-a-level"` comes back HTTP 400 "the preset name or version does not exist" — so nothing is silently ignored and nothing is urgent. The `Settings.preset` docstring is also behind the provider's level list: it stops at `xhigh` and misses `ultra` (`ultra@2026-11-05.0`, running `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`). — *needs a call: whether to rename the public `preset` Settings field to `effort` with a deprecation, or only switch the wire field now and leave ours named after an alias — a second run confirms Perplexity has published no removal date, so nothing forces the breaking half*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` is the `fast` effort's fallback model, not its model (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Perplexity's `fast` effort — the exact configuration the service sends when nothing is configured — runs `openai/gpt-6-luna` and names `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` only as the fallback when it is unavailable. Latency favours the newer model (measured 2026-11-09: median TTFAT 1098 ms against 1205 ms over `--repeat 5`, and 1050 ms against 1364 ms on a reasoning-triggering prompt). aiewf-eval still blocks the straight bump, re-read today unchanged: at reasoning off, which is what `fast` sets, `gpt-6-luna (none)` is unranked on `aiwf_medium_context` — 0 of 30 conversations completed, fixed-900 pass 25.0% — against `gpt-5.6-luna (none)` at 84.4% / 708 ms P50. The only pairing that beats today's default is `gpt-6-luna (low)` at 91.8% / 917 ms P50. Neither `gpt-6.1-*` nor the new `gpt-6.2-*` models have a leaderboard row. — *needs a call: whether to bump the model together with raising the service's reasoning default off `fast`, since gpt-6-luna only beats the current default at `low` reasoning and that trades roughly 200 ms of leaderboard TTFAT for the pass rate*
- [piper/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/piper/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The piper extra omits piper-tts's zh, ja and th extras, so Chinese, Japanese and Thai voices cannot be phonemized (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — voices.json ships zh_CN, ja_JP and th_TH voices; piper.phonemize_chinese fails at import (unicode_rbnf missing) and phonemize_japanese / phonemize_thai import pyopenjtalk / tltk lazily, so those voices fail at synthesis time — *needs a call: the zh extra pulls transformers, g2pW and sentence-stream, ja pulls pyopenjtalk-plus and th pulls tltk and pandas — a call on whether pipecat-ai[piper] carries them or the docs tell users to install them*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Our pin `pocket-tts>=2.1.0,<3` excludes pocket-tts 3.x and 4.x (4.0.1, 2026-10-21) (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — every call site we use (`TTSModel.load_model`, `get_state_for_audio_prompt`, `generate_audio_stream`, `sample_rate`) has the same signature in 4.0.1, and bare predefined voice names such as `alba` still resolve, now per language; behind the pin sit 15 extra configs, retrained French/Italian/Dutch/German/Spanish/Portuguese weights, a `stop: threading.Event` on `generate_audio_stream` that would let an interruption end decoding, `sampler_decode_steps` (replacing the deprecated `lsd_decode_steps`), a `checkpoint` argument, and a 5 ms chunk fade-in that removes the onset click — *needs a call: 3.x/4.x replace `load_model`'s hard `temp=0.7` default with a per-config `default_temperature` of 0.3 and the `english` alias now points at `english_2026-10`, so the bump changes how every language sounds for callers that never set `temp` — needs a call on whether that is acceptable*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-23.md) — French is mapped to the 24-layer model and Dutch is unmapped (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — pocket-tts 3.2.0 added a distilled 6-layer `french` and 3.3.0 added `dutch`, both retrained in 4.0.0; the code comment stating French has no distilled model is still accurate for the 2.1.0 the pin resolves to, and `language_to_pocket_tts_language` has no Dutch entry — *needs a call: both configs exist only in 3.x/4.x, so the map change cannot land before the pin bump above — needs that decision first*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-23.md) — No way to select a pocket-tts model variant or a custom config (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `PocketTTSSettings` inherits `model` but the service ignores it, so `english_2026-01`, the 24-layer variants, 4.0's six sub-20M English models (18M, 7M and 1.9M, each with a `refl` variant) and the 14 community configs the README lists are unreachable; `load_model` takes either `language` or `config` (local path, `https://` or `hf://`) — *needs a call: `load_model` rejects `language` and `config` together and weights load once at construction — needs a call on whether `model` names a pocket-tts config, or a separate `config` constructor argument does*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default qwen-plus trails Alibaba's current generation (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — qwen4-max went GA on 2026-11-16 as the flagship, and the model overview now recommends qwen4-max / qwen3.7-plus / qwen3.8-flash without listing qwen-plus at all. qwen-plus itself is unchanged: still "functionally equivalent to qwen-plus-2025-12-01", no deprecation notice, thinking off by default, 1061 ms TTFAT. Both candidates reason by default and are much slower on a trivial prompt — qwen4-max 1761 ms TTFAT (2023 ms with thinking forced off), qwen3.7-plus 4018 ms with 2609 ms of thinking. aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard still carries no DashScope-hosted row, so parity cannot be read off it — *needs a call: which successor to adopt, and whether a hosted model with no aiewf-eval row can replace a default that has no row either*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-23.md) — No first-class way to control Qwen's default-on thinking (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — this now blocks the default bump outright: every model Alibaba recommends reasons by default, and the thinking-off candidates are the models being phased out of the recommendations. qwen4-max's own model page documents `reasoning_effort` (low | high | xhigh, default high) alongside `enable_thinking` (default true) and `thinking_budget` (default 131072), while the deep-thinking page still documents `reasoning_effort` for qwen3.8-omni-flash only; `Settings` exposes none of the three. Today the knob is `extra={"extra_body": {"enable_thinking": False}}`, which works on qwen4-max (thinking 0 ms) but is undiscoverable — *needs a call: whether to add `enable_thinking`/`thinking_budget` Settings fields or follow the Anthropic precedent and disable thinking by default for thinking-capable Qwen models*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — A fixed ~1 s jitter buffer delays the first audio frame (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_jitter_buffer_bytes = 44100` withholds audio until ~1 s has accumulated at 22050 Hz; median TTFB is 468 ms over 5 runs this time (356-960 ms), in line with the 534-687 ms of previous runs and well above the ~200 ms time-to-first-sound earlier runs read off the WebSocket page — *needs a call: whether the buffer can shrink without playback gaps - the code comments cite 300-450 ms gaps between Resemble's audio bursts, which nobody has re-measured*
- [rime/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Prism's reply review (`prism_review`) is unreachable from the service — Prism reviews each reply chunk with the backend before speaking it (`prism_review.on_timeout` stop/speak/say, `timeout_ms` 100-5000, fixed at the first session update). `rimelabs_sdk` ships it as `realtime.Review` and `Realtime.connect(review=...)`, but `RimeRealtimeLLMService` never passes it and has no constructor argument for it, so Pipecat bots cannot gate what Prism says. — *needs a call: review is a callback that must answer with allow/replace/stop inside a deadline, so exposing it means a new constructor parameter plus a Pipecat-level decision API, not a settings pass-through*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Coda's custom pronunciation (`customLexicon`) is unreachable from any Rime TTS class (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Rime documents `customLexicon` (array of `{spelling, pronunciation}` in X-SAMPA, up to 500 entries, en/de/es/fr/it/pt) as Coda's pronunciation control; a probe POST with it returns 200 and WebSocket v1 accepts `custom_lexicon`. Coda ignores `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` without error, so `RimeTTSService.PRONOUNCE()` silently does nothing on the default model. — *needs a call: `RimeTTSSettings` is shared by the websocket and HTTP classes and the websocket class pastes settings into the URL query string unencoded, so a shared field needs a stance on how (or whether) the websocket class sends a JSON array*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — RimeHttpTTSService pins a legacy HTTP path and takes no base-URL override (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `__init__` hard-codes `https://users.rime.ai/v1/rime-tts`; Rime's cheat sheet documents per-model hosts (`https://coda.api.rime.ai/`, `https://mist.api.rime.ai/`) as where HTTP TTS must go and does not mention the pinned path, which still answers 200. — *needs a call: exposing the host means a new constructor parameter (`base_url=`), and moving the default to the per-model hosts is a behavior change — both are public-API decisions*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Rime recommends `segment=never` for voice agents, but Pipecat flushes only at end of turn (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — under `segment=never` Rime synthesizes nothing until it receives a `flush`, and `TTSService` sends one only when the bot turn ends, so a user following Rime's guidance gets no audio until the whole turn is written. The services leave `segment` unset and rely on Rime's `bySentence` default. — *needs a call: following Rime's recommendation means flushing per aggregated utterance rather than per turn — a change in synthesis cadence, not a settings pass-through*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — RimeV1TTSService never requests WebSocket v1 word timestamps and its docstring says v1 has none (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the v1 `start` message carries `timestamps.enable`; a probe with it set came back with five aligned spans in `done`. Rime documents word timestamps for Coda v1 in de/en/es/fr/it/pt and Mist v3 in en, so a bot moving from `/ws3` to v1 loses the word timestamps the older class reports. — *needs a call: v1 delivers every span in the terminal `done` event, after the context's audio, so turning them on means deciding how late timestamps should feed word tracking*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — provider-watch does not cover RimeV1TTSService (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `inventory.py` keys unit type on the module stem, and `rime/tts_v1.py` is not `tts`, so RimeV1TTSService lands in no unit: the rime units are `rime/tts`, `rime/stt` and the new `rime/realtime`, none of them it. `probe.py run --service RimeV1TTSService` fails with "unknown service class". — *needs a call: a call on whether `tts_v1` folds into the `rime/tts` unit or becomes its own variant unit*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `RimeTTSSettings` labels mist-era fields "(mistv2 only)" and the request branch that carries them is written for models retiring on 2026-12-03 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — After 2026-12-03 the non-coda branch reaches only Mist v3, where Rime's migration guide says `speedAlpha`'s direction is reversed (use `timeScaleFactor`), `noTextNormalization` has no effect, and `phonemizeBetweenBrackets` is English-only; `reduceLatency` and `saveOovs` appear in no current Mist v3 reference. `speedAlpha` is also sent for Coda, which accepts it. — *needs a call: Rime documents no replacement for `reduceLatency` and `saveOovs` on Mist v3, so relabelling (or dropping) them needs a ruling on what the service should still send*
- [sambanova/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sambanova/llm/2026-11-23.md) — SambaNova's non-OpenAI body parameters (`top_k`, `repetition_penalty`, `chat_template_kwargs`) are unreachable from Settings (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — unchanged in code and spec — `build_chat_completion_params` never sends `top_k`, and `params.update(self._settings.extra)` feeds `chat.completions.create(**params)` as kwargs, so only `extra={"extra_body": {"top_k": 40}}` reaches the API — *needs a call: `base_llm.py` documents that top_k is ignored for every openai-client service; making SambaNova the exception needs a stance on that and on how an `extra_body` we build merges with a user's own `extra["extra_body"]`*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `reasoning_effort` accepts `medium` everywhere and forbids `none` and `max`, neither of which matches the API (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `SarvamLLMSettings` types it `Literal["low", "medium", "high"]`. /v1 documents the full union `none`/`low`/`medium`/`high`/`max` with a `medium` default and null to disable; /v2 documents the same union taken in per-model subsets — `low`/`high`/`max` on `glm5.3`, `glm5.3-flash` and `gemma4`, all five on the DeepSeek models and `qwen38-27b`, and `low`/`medium`/`high`/`max` on `kimi-k3.1-pro` where `medium` runs as `high`. — *needs a call: whether to widen the Literal to the documented union and validate per model, or split the allowed values by model the way `_REASONING_MODELS` already splits support*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — sarvam-105b-conversations is far faster to first answer token and is the variant Sarvam positions for voice agents (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — 681 ms vs 1456 ms median TTFAT on a trivial prompt and 687 ms vs 3196 ms on a reasoning prompt, both GA and both now on the Sarvam-105B-1711 weights. The model page still calls sarvam-105b the flagship, so this is not a documented succession; the conversations variant has a 32K context window against 128K, is /v1-only, and never reasons. — *needs a call: aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard, re-read this run, still has no Sarvam rows, so the quality half of a default bump is unmeasured*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `_WIKI_GROUNDING_MODELS` names a parameter no Sarvam reference documents (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `wiki_grounding` appears in neither the /v1 nor the /v2 chat reference nor on any current model page, yet `_WIKI_GROUNDING_MODELS` holds `gemma4` and `sarvam-105b` and the service merges it into `extra_body` for them. The capability sets err the other way too: `sarvam-105b-conversations` is in neither set, so Pipecat raises an error frame for `reasoning_effort` on it, while /v1 documents the model as accepting any value and ignoring it. — *needs a call: HTTP 200 does not prove a parameter takes effect, and tightening the sets turns silently-ignored parameters into error frames — needs a stance on validating against the docs or against what the API accepts*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `stream_options` is stripped from every request, so streaming token usage is unavailable (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `build_chat_completion_params` pops `stream_options` unconditionally. /v2 documents it as valid whenever `stream` is true and required to be top-level, with `include_usage: true` adding a final chunk carrying usage including `reasoning_tokens` — the counts Pipecat's LLM metrics would report. /v1, which serves `sarvam-105b-conversations`, documents no `stream_options` at all, so lifting the pop has to be per API version. — *needs a call: whether a /v2-only pop is acceptable given the service picks its base URL from the model, or the field should be gated on something the user sets*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — CLI registry offers only SarvamSTTService, whose endpoint Sarvam labels legacy (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the beta caveat that held this open is gone. The realtime page no longer contains the words "beta", "preview" or "GA" anywhere, and the streaming page now reads "This page documents the legacy WebSocket (`/speech-to-text/ws`)" and recommends realtime for new integrations, citing interim transcripts, millisecond VAD tuning and live reconfiguration. No sunset date either way. `sarvam_stt` in `cli/registry/service_metadata.py` still maps to `class_name=["SarvamSTTService"]` alone, and the unit has three classes — *needs a call: whether SarvamRealtimeSTTService, or SarvamHttpSTTService, should join the registry's Sarvam STT entry now that Sarvam calls realtime neither beta nor legacy*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `keyterms` cannot be changed mid-call on SarvamRealtimeSTTService — Sarvam's realtime page now states "`keyterms` can also be updated mid-call (`saaras:v4` only)", and an ad-hoc `config.update` carrying `keyterms` came back `config.updated` with `applied:["keyterms=['Pipecat']"]`. Here `keyterms` is a constructor argument, absent from both `SarvamRealtimeSTTSettings` and `_RUNTIME_CONFIG_FIELDS`, so `update_config(keyterms=...)` raises `ValueError`. The sarvamai 0.1.49 `RealtimeConfigUpdate` schema omits `keyterms` too, so the generated SDK disagrees with the live server and with the docs — *needs a call: whether `keyterms` moves from a constructor argument to a Settings field, since a runtime value would otherwise contend with the connection-time one in `_query_params()`*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Pitch and loudness are not clamped to the model's range, and the deprecated `InputParams` still validates against bulbul:v2's wider bounds (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `TTSModelConfig` carries a range for pace only and clamps it; pitch and loudness have no range and are passed through. The branch widens the exposure, since v3 now sends both fields too — *needs a call: whether to add `pitch_range`/`loudness_range` to `TTSModelConfig` and clamp as pace does, or only tighten the stale `InputParams` bounds*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — bulbul's `dict_id` (pronunciation dictionary) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — a `dict_id` field on `SarvamHttpTTSSettings` passed through in `run_tts` and `_send_config` would do it; the REST reference and the WebSocket AsyncAPI both carry it and the Bulbul page says "Supported on both models".
