- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Sarvam's cloned and saved voices are unreachable from either class (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — the Voice Cloning API synthesizes through its own endpoints — `POST /voices/clone`, `/voices/clone/stream` and a `GET /voices/clone/ws` that takes incremental text — not through `/text-to-speech`, so reaching it means a new class rather than a Settings field. Sarvam keeps building it out; `POST /voices/watermark/detect` was added on 2026-11-09 (needs sarvamai >= 0.1.37 for the cloning calls, >= 0.1.45 for the denoise fields; our pin is 0.1.28, latest 0.1.49) — *needs a call: whether a saved `voice_id` also works as `speaker` on `/text-to-speech` (unverified — checking it means creating a voice on the account) and, if not, whether Pipecat wants a cloned-voice TTS class*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Sarvam retires bulbul:v3 on Voice Agents on 2026-11-25 while Pipecat defaults to it (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — the deadline moved from 2026-11-10 to 2026-11-25 on 2026-11-11, and the Text-to-Speech API both Pipecat classes call is still exempt — the Bulbul page calls v3 "the API default when `model` is omitted" and the notice still reads "Bulbul V3 stays available on the Text-to-Speech API", so no Pipecat user is affected by the date and the provider has not designated a successor on this API. Two of the old blockers are gone — Sarvam now documents ml-IN and od-IN as working on v4-flash with any persona, and v3's named speakers have a published V3-to-V4 mapping — but v4-flash still rejects v3's short speaker names, so `voice="shubh"` configs break. The latency argument has also gone: v4-flash measured 560 ms against v3's 549 ms this run (medians of 5), where it was 550 ms against 646 ms on 2026-10-28 — *needs a call: whether Pipecat follows Sarvam's voice-agent guidance to v4-flash even though the TTS API keeps v3 as its default, and if so how v3's speaker names are migrated*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `output_audio_codec` and `output_audio_bitrate` are reachable only through the deprecated `InputParams` (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — on `SarvamTTSService` both are constructor-time `InputParams` fields with no `SarvamTTSSettings` equivalent, and `InputParams` is marked for removal in 2.0.0, so the WebSocket codec choice disappears with it; the REST class never sends a codec at all and always decodes WAV. Sarvam's Bulbul page now lists `opus` as working on both models at 8000, 16000, 24000 and 48000 Hz, where the references previously called it rejected on v3 and v4 — *needs a call: the receive path builds `TTSAudioRawFrame` from the payload as PCM, so a non-`linear16` codec would need decoding before the fields can be promoted to Settings — decide whether to carry them forward or drop them*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-23.md) — Trinity-2's live camera, background and foreground controls are not reachable (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — simli-ai 2.1.1 exposes `setCamera(camera, lerp_seconds=...)`, `setBackground(id)` and `setForeground(id)` on `SimliClient`, which send the `CAM_`/`BG_`/`FG_` websocket messages; `SimliVideoService` sends only the session's starting framing (`SimliVideoService.Framing`), so nothing can move the camera or swap an asset once the session is up, and Simli's Trinity-2 page advertises both as live changes — *needs a call: whether a mid-session control becomes a frame, a method, or both — AGENTS.md prefers a frame, and these are the first Simli controls that act during a session*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-23.md) — `enable_logging` is accepted but never used — simli-ai 2.x has no logging toggle (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the constructor and the deprecated `InputParams` both take `enable_logging`, but it reaches neither `SimliConfig` nor `SimliClient`; simli-ai 2.1.1's reference documents no logging option either, only the stdlib logger named `simli_client` — *needs a call: a call between deprecating the argument and honouring it by configuring the `simli_client` logger*
- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-23.md) — Simli session events are never registered, so a session that dies mid-call is silent (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — a failed `start()` and a failed `send()` raise with Simli's own reason, which the service already turns into an `ErrorFrame`; a session Simli ends or errors *mid-call* still only ends the two stream iterators, with no `registerEventCallback` registered, `stopReason` and `error` unread, and nothing pushed upstream — *needs a call: which events become Pipecat event handlers versus an internal `push_error` on ERROR/STOP, and whether SPEAK/SILENT duplicate the audio frames the service already pushes*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Server error frames (`type: error`) are discarded, so a rejected connection has no diagnosis — Smallest answers a rejected connection with a JSON frame - this run's probe of `model=pulse-2` got `{"type":"error","error_code":"MODEL_NOT_ENABLED","message":"Pulse 2.0 is currently disabled for this account... Use model=pulse in the meantime.","region":"us-west-2"}` - and then closes the socket. `_process_response` reads only `transcript`/`is_final`/`is_last`, so the message is dropped and the base reconnect loop reports only "connection failed 3 times immediately after connecting" before marking the processor unusable. The same path hides LANGUAGE_NOT_SUPPORTED_BY_MODEL and LANGUAGE_NOT_ENABLED_IN_REGION, which is what makes the language-map item user-hostile. — *needs a call: decide whether a server error frame should become a fatal or non-fatal `push_error` (and whether it should stop the reconnect loop, since these rejections never succeed on retry)*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The session is closed without `close_stream`, so pending audio is dropped and `is_last` never arrives (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `_disconnect_websocket` calls `websocket.close()` directly. The response-format page documents `{"type":"close_stream"}` followed by an `is_last: true` final as the way to end a session, warning "Do not close the WebSocket connection immediately after sending", and the AsyncAPI spec lists it as the `sendClose` operation. The `_process_response` docstring says `is_last` is "Only ``True`` after ``close_stream`` is sent at call end", so the branch it guards is unreachable. Four probe runs of `pulse` now (2026-10-12, 2026-10-28, 2026-11-09, this run) returned a final truncated at the same mid-sentence point, consistent with the tail being lost between the VAD-triggered `finalize` and teardown. — *needs a call: decide whether `stop()` should send `close_stream` and await the `is_last` final under a timeout before closing, or whether the docstring should simply be corrected to say the service never sends it*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `finalize_on_words` and `max_words` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Pulse's documented agentic pattern is `finalize_on_words=false` plus a client `finalize` per turn, which is what the service already sends on VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame; the AsyncAPI bindings still default `finalize_on_words` to true, and the pulse-2 card tells clients not to send either parameter at all. — *needs a call: these change when finals arrive and pulse-2 must not receive them, so exposing them needs a stance on per-model gating and on whether the service should adopt the agentic pattern by default*
- [soniox/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Soniox's real-time `translation` config object is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — v6 streams translation tokens on the same socket; the config takes `{type: one_way, target_language}` or `{type: two_way, language_a, language_b}` — *needs a call: translated tokens arrive interleaved with originals and marked `translation_status`, so exposing this needs a decision on how translations reach the pipeline (separate frames, or filtered out of the transcript)*
- [soniox/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The service never pings Soniox, so a silently dropped socket is not noticed — Soniox now documents `{"type":"ping","id":N}` / `pong` and recommends it over `keepalive` for new clients; `_send_keepalive` sends the unanswered `keepalive` message, and only when no audio has been sent for a second, so while audio flows a dead connection is invisible and the bot goes deaf for the rest of the call — *needs a call: a call on where liveness lives — pinging every 5 s regardless of audio and reconnecting on a late pong is a change to the websocket lifecycle, and the deadline handling arguably belongs in `WebsocketSTTService` rather than this service*
- [soniox/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `audio_format` accepts formats the service cannot configure or play back (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Soniox's format reference gives sample rates and bitrates per format, while the service keeps one flat `VALID_SAMPLE_RATES` set for every `pcm_` format and sends `sample_rate` only for `pcm_` ones; the 2026-10-28 probe had `audio_format="pcm_mulaw"` at 24000 Hz pass the local check unwarned and Soniox answer 400 `invalid_request` ("allowed [8000]"), and mp3/opus/aac get neither `sample_rate` nor `bitrate`. Every non-PCM16 format also arrives as undecoded bytes in `TTSAudioRawFrame`. Format table and service code both unchanged this run. — *needs a call: whether to model Soniox's per-format rate/bitrate table and expose `bitrate`, or to narrow `audio_format` to the formats the receive path can actually play*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Word timestamps collapse into a single word for languages written without spaces, which simba-3.5 made reachable (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `_SpeechMarkAccumulator` releases a word only when the next character in the text is whitespace or the text ends, so in Japanese, Chinese, Thai, Burmese and the other scriptio-continua languages simba-3.5 speaks, every mark abuts the previous one and merges into one. Re-probed live this run: a 13-word English sentence yields 13 word frames, a two-sentence Japanese one yields 1, audio fine in both. What degrades is `add_word_timestamps`, so bot-speaking text sync and word-level interruption tracking get one mark at the first word's time. Pipecat already has the shape of a fix twice over - `cartesia/tts.py` and `elevenlabs/tts_base.py` both detect Chinese/Japanese from the language setting and pass `includes_inter_frame_spaces=True` - but neither covers Thai or Burmese, and this service's problem is the merge rule rather than per-character tokens. — *needs a call: whether to follow the Cartesia/ElevenLabs CJK precedent and what it should cover, given simba-3.5 also speaks Thai, Burmese and Lao*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The language map covers 6 of the 48 languages simba-3.5 speaks (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `language_to_speechify_language` maps DE, EN, ES, FR, IT, PT - the six simba-3.0 routes. `GET /v1/audio/models` lists 48 for simba-3.5, so every other `Language` falls through `resolve_language` to its BCP-47 string with a `not verified` warning; seen again this run on the Japanese probe. Synthesis is unaffected. The Pipecat docs page still repeats the six-language list. — *needs a call: which tag each of the 48 gets - an identity mapping is right for es-MX and for the bare codes simba-3.5 lists, but en-GB or pt-PT would be a locale substitution that needs a stance*
- [speechify/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechify/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Speechify's WebSocket streaming-text endpoint `/v1/audio/stream/ws` has no Pipecat class; text is re-POSTed per aggregated utterance over HTTP (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Added 2026-10-08. Takes LLM text as it arrives and speaks each completed sentence, with `speech_marks=true` giving word marks on one session-wide timeline rather than the per-request timelines the service offsets itself. Settings are query-string parameters with the HTTP endpoint's names, so the payload logic carries over, `pronunciations` included (sent as a `session.pronunciations` message, and the 2026-11-17 English support names the ws input stream among the affected endpoints). Session limits shape the design - one concurrent request, closed after 60 s idle and after 5 minutes total. `GET /v1/audio/models` still omits the endpoint from every model's `endpoints`, naming it only in simba-3.5's description. — *needs a call: whether to add a websocket sibling class at all, and what reconnect behavior the 5-minute session cap implies for a long call*
- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Default base URL is the undocumented `eu2` host although all four documented hosts serve `/agent` (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The service defaults to `wss://eu2.rt.speechmatics.com/v2/agent` (stt.py:475), still matching the SDK's own `DEFAULT_RT_URL` in 0.3.0. Unchanged in the code this run; an earlier run established that `global`, `eu`, `us` and `au` all transcribe on `/agent`, so US and AU users are routed to the EU by default unless they set `base_url`. — *needs a call: whether to follow the SDK's default or switch to `global`, which routes to the nearest region but has data-residency implications*
- [speechmatics/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/tts/2026-11-23.md) — A sample rate other than 16 kHz makes every request 422 with an opaque error (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the API's `output_format` is validated against `^(wav_16000|pcm_16000)$` (preview host's OpenAPI spec), but `_get_endpoint_url` builds `output_format=pcm_{sample_rate}`; with `sample_rate=None` (adopt the pipeline rate, commonly 24 kHz) this run's probe again got `Service unavailable [422]` and no audio, after only a warning at construction — *needs a call: whether the service should pin 16 kHz regardless of the caller's sample rate or raise at construction — pinning also has to force the rate on the frames it yields, so neither is a one-line change*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-23.md) — Tavus renamed persona/replica to PAL/Face; the class still takes `replica_id` only (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The create-conversation reference still documents only `face_id`/`pal_id` — `replica_id`/`persona_id` appear nowhere in its request schema — and Tavus's Pipecat page shows `TavusVideoService(api_key=..., face_id=..., session=session)` with `TAVUS_FACE_ID`, a snippet our constructor rejects with TypeError. Tavus's new speech-to-speech page now documents the divergence instead of the alias: "Pipecat's Tavus transport still takes a `replica_id` argument. Pass your Tavus Face ID there." Nothing is gated server-side: the legacy body returned 200 today, as did the same call with `face_id`/`pal_id`, and the list-faces page still records `/v2/replicas` and `replica_id` as supported aliases with no removal date. — *needs a call: a `face_id`/`pal_id` alias is a new constructor parameter plus a registry env-var change — needs a call on whether to follow the rename and how to deprecate `replica_id`*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-23.md) — Conversation creation fields are not reachable — `properties`, `policy`, `audio_only`, `callback_url` (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `TavusApi.create_conversation` posts a fixed body of `replica_id` and `persona_id`, so every Pipecat Tavus session takes the API defaults, including the 3600 s `max_call_duration` cap and the 300 s `participant_absent_timeout`, and cannot set `apply_greenscreen`, the recording fields, `policy: "eu"` (creation-time only), `audio_only`, or the `callback_url`/`callback_secret` pair the 11-18 changelog added for signed webhooks. A probe posting `properties` alongside the rest returned 200, so a pass-through would work. — *needs a call: which knobs to expose, and where — the conversation is created once in `setup()` from constructor args, so a pass-through means new constructor parameters threaded through TavusVideoService, TavusTransportClient and TavusApi*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `Settings.language` is never sent to the transcription endpoint (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_connect_websocket` still builds the URL from `model`, `turn_detection` and `input_audio_format` only. docs.pipecat.ai states the field is not sent and has no effect, so the harm is a capability gap (non-English users cannot force a language) rather than a misleading promise. The 2026-09-01 raw-socket probe showed the endpoint honours `&language=` — *needs a call: the stored default is `Language.EN`, so always sending it flips today's autodetect to forced English — send the stored value, or default the field to `None` and send only when set?*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Interim transcriptions can never fire — the delta handler reads `transcript` but the event carries `delta` (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — `_handle_transcription_delta` does `evt.get("transcript", "")`; Together's streaming page still documents the delta event's text field as `delta` and reserves `transcript` for the completed event, so the `InterimTranscriptionFrame` push is unreachable, as this run's probes again showed — no interim frame on any run. It is doubly unreachable because the class hard-codes `turn_detection=none`, under which the endpoint emits only a single `completed` event. docs.pipecat.ai still claims the service supports interim results — *needs a call: is the delta path meant to be reachable at all? `turn_detection=none` is deliberate (the class commits on `VADUserStoppedSpeakingFrame`), so fixing the key alone changes nothing — this needs a stance on whether interim results are a supported feature or the handler is dead code to drop*
- [together/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/together/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Together's streaming ASR models are usable but undocumented and untried as a default (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` and the two `nemotron-*-asr-streaming-0.6b` models work through the class with no code change (no allowlist); parakeet again beat Whisper on this run's single sample (TTFB 336 ms vs 480 ms) with an equivalent transcript — *needs a call: Together's STT page still names no recommended model and no successor to Whisper; a default bump would need a stated successor plus repeat-median latency*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Ultravox's server-VAD turn messages are neither requested nor handled, so the service emits no turn frames — `user_started_speaking` / `user_stopped_speaking` are sent to any connection that asks for them via `medium.serverWebSocket.dataMessages` (probed: POST /api/calls returns 201 with `dataMessages: {userStartedSpeaking: true, userStoppedSpeaking: true}` echoed back). The service hardcodes `medium` to `{"serverWebSocket": {"inputSampleRate": ...}}`, so it never asks, and both message types fall through `_receive_messages` to the debug-log default case. The class docstring documents the consequence — no RTVI speech events, no `TurnTrackingObserver`, no `AudioBufferProcessor` turn recording, no `UserIdleController`, no mute strategies, no voicemail detection — and points users at a local VAD approximation instead of Ultravox's own turn decisions — *needs a call: whether server turn frames should be on by default or opt-in, and how they coexist with the local-VAD path the docstring recommends today*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Tool results are always sent with Ultravox's defaults; `agentReaction` and `responseType` are not expressible (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `client_tool_result` accepts `agentReaction` (speaks | listens | speaks-once) and `responseType` (default tool-response; hang-up, new-stage), plus `errorType`/`errorMessage` and `updateCallState`; `_send_tool_result` sends only `invocationId` and `result`, so a handler cannot make the agent listen after a tool, end the call, or switch call stage — *needs a call: a call on how a function handler carries these — a result-string convention, a new frame, or a service-level default*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `user_text_message.urgency` is not user-expressible; injected text is never `immediate` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_send_user_text` takes an `urgency` argument, but only the async-tool progress path passes it (soon | later); text from `InputTextRawFrame` and async-tool final results omit it and take Ultravox's documented default of `soon`, so nothing can interrupt the agent (`immediate`) and an application has no control at all; `threadId` (default `UI`) is omitted everywhere — *needs a call: urgency is per-message, so it needs a stance — a Settings default for all injected text, or a per-frame field*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Call-creation query parameters are unreachable; `extra` merges into the body only (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — POST /api/calls takes `priorCallId` (start from a prior call's message history), `enableGreetingPrompt` (default true) and `throttle` as query parameters, and POST /api/agents/{id}/calls takes `throttle`; both `_start_one_shot_call` and `_start_agent_call` post to a bare URL and merge `extra` into the JSON body, so none is settable. The greeting prompt is visible in every probe this run — each call opened with "Hello, this is [Your Name]." before the real turn — and a user cannot turn it off. Workaround: create the call yourself and pass its join URL via `JoinUrlInputParams` — *needs a call: whether params should grow a query-parameter escape hatch (e.g. `extra_query`) or the `JoinUrlInputParams` workaround is the intended answer*
- [ultravox/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/ultravox/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — WebSocket medium options are unreachable because the service builds `medium` itself — `medium.serverWebSocket` also takes `clientBufferSizeMs` (default 60; the docs recommend a large value such as 30000 for clients that implement `playback_clear_buffer`, which this service does), `mediaIdleTimeout` (recommended 30–60s, and the docs say it "may become the default in the future") and `outputSampleRate`. The service writes `medium` with only `inputSampleRate`, and `extra` is a shallow top-level merge, so setting any of them means replacing the whole `medium` object through `extra` and restating the sample rate the service picked — *needs a call: whether the medium sub-object should get its own params fields or `extra` should deep-merge*
- [whisper/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/whisper/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The default distil model ignores prompt conditioning, and both fields together drop speech (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — on `Systran/faster-distil-whisper-medium.en`, `hotwords` and `initial_prompt` each leave the transcript at "Pippecut", and setting both makes faster-whisper return only the first of the clip's two segments (reproduced again on this run's commit; earlier runs showed the surviving segment's `no_speech_prob` is 0.058, so our threshold is not what drops it). On `small.en` all four combinations keep both sentences and either field corrects "Pipecat". — *needs a call: whether to move the default off a distil checkpoint, suppress one field when the other is set, or just document that the pair is unsafe on distil models*
- [whisper/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/whisper/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Transcription knobs beyond hotwords and initial_prompt are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `beam_size`, `temperature` (WhisperSTTServiceMLX has it, WhisperSTTService does not), `vad_filter`, `condition_on_previous_text` and `word_timestamps` are 5 of `transcribe()`'s 35 parameters that neither class passes, and `WhisperSTTServiceMLX.Settings` has neither `hotwords` (mlx-whisper has no such parameter) nor `initial_prompt` (it does); `BatchedInferencePipeline` (faster-whisper 1.1, ~4x faster) is unused. — *needs a call: which of these belong in Settings versus init-only inference config, whether batched inference pays off for single-utterance segments, and who can probe the MLX class, which needs Apple Silicon*
- [xai/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/llm/2026-11-23.md) — xAI's live search is unreachable from GrokLLMService; chat completions rejects it and the replacement lives on the Responses API — `search_parameters` and `web_search_options` are still documented on the chat-completions reference but both return 410 from the live API, and a server-side `{"type": "web_search"}` tool entry returns 422; xAI labels chat completions a legacy endpoint and says new features land on Responses first, with no sunset date given — *needs a call: reaching live search means a Responses-API path for Grok (new service or endpoint switch), not a Settings field — is that worth building for this provider?*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `audio.output.transport` accepts "binary" but the service only reads the JSON wire path (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the spec still says output "emits on the configured transport only", so setting it leaves `parse_server_event` with raw codec bytes it cannot parse and the conversation silent; input is safe either way because the server dual-accepts both channels, so only the output half is broken — *needs a call: a call on whether to implement binary framing on the receive side or drop `transport` from `AudioOutput` so the field cannot be set to a value the service does not honor*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — SIP calls are unreachable because the WebSocket URL is always built with `model` and never `call_id` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the spec lists `call_id` as a query parameter that binds the socket to an inbound SIP call and makes `model` ignored; the service already handles `input_audio_buffer.dtmf_event_received`, which xAI emits on SIP sessions only, so that path cannot be exercised today — *needs a call: a constructor or settings shape for `call_id` (it is per-call, not per-service) — more than a pass-through field*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Session resumption cannot work because the service never reconnects with `?conversation_id=` (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — `SessionResumption.enabled` is settable and `_conversation_id` is captured from `conversation.created`, but it is never read and `_connect` builds `?model=<model>` only, so opting in caches turns server-side that nothing ever replays — *needs a call: a call on whether the service should reconnect with its own cached `conversation_id` automatically or expose it so the application decides when to resume*
- [xai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `web_search` and `x_search` tool options are unreachable, and passing them as a raw dict drops them silently (since 2026-09-28, 8 weeks) — xAI documents `location`, `allowed_domains`, `excluded_domains` and `enable_image_understanding` on `web_search`, and `excluded_x_handles`, `from_date`, `to_date`, `enable_image_understanding` and `enable_video_understanding` on `x_search`; `WebSearchTool` carries only `type` and `XSearchTool` only `allowed_x_handles`, and because the `GrokTool` union matches the typed model before `dict[str, Any]`, a raw dict is coerced to the model and the extra keys vanish without a warning — *needs a call: the domain and handle lists are documented as mutually exclusive pairs with maxItems caps, so exposing them needs a stance on validating that client-side versus letting the server reject*

- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — xAI's new `redact` parameter (PII/PCI/PHI redaction) is not reachable from Settings — `redact` is now documented as a repeatable query parameter on both the streaming and batch endpoints (groups `pii`, `pci`, `phi`, plus individual types such as `ssn`, `bank_account`); the live socket accepts `redact=pii` and returns placeholder-substituted text. Note the server emits no interim results while redaction is on, confirmed live, so `interim_results` and `redact` interact. — *needs a call: same probe blocker as `format`, plus a stance on `interim_results` being silently ineffective when `redact` is set*
- [xai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/xai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The service never sends xAI's `finalize` client message, so a final transcript waits for the silence timer (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — xAI accepts `{"type": "finalize"}` (confirmed again on the live socket) to close the current utterance immediately; Pipecat only sends `audio.done` on disconnect, so end-of-turn finals cost up to `endpointing` ms (server default 400ms) — *needs a call: a call on what triggers it — wiring it to the user turn-stop path is a behavior change that interacts with the turn strategies and with server-side `smart_turn`*

- [zoom_ai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/zoom_ai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Service sends `language` at the top level of `session.update`; Zoom's Live schema now documents it as `config.language` (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the live-mode field table lists `config.language` and every example on live-mode, text-polishing and speech-configuration nests it; `_session_update()` sets `update["language"]` and puts only model/language_detection/diarization/vocabulary in `config`. If Scribe ignores the top-level field, every session silently runs Scribe's default en-US. — *needs a call: a live check with a key — set a non-English language and read the effective `language` back from `session.updated` — before moving the field; no ZOOM_AI_API_KEY in this environment*

- [assemblyai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/assemblyai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — No HTTP TTS class for AssemblyAI's /v1/speech routes (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — AssemblyAI also exposes POST /v1/speech, POST /v1/speech/events (SSE with word timings) and OpenAI-compatible routes; Pipecat ships only the WebSocket class, where several providers ship an Http variant too — *needs a call: a new service class, not a settings change — confirm there is demand given the WebSocket session already covers the real-time path*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `speed_control` and `stability` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Documented request fields on both HTTP endpoints (`speed_control` number 0.7–2.0, `stability` integer 0–100) that `AsyncAITTSSettings` adds nothing for — it is an empty subclass of `TTSSettings`, and neither class reads `Settings.extra` when building a request. Async's model page still restricts both to `async_flash_v1.0`, so on the default `async_flash_v1.5` they are accepted and ignored, and the websocket init message has no slot for them at all. — *needs a call: a stance on exposing settings that the default model accepts but does not honor, and that the websocket class cannot send*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The language map offers ten codes the default model does not support (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `language_to_async_language` maps 16 Language values, but per Async's model page `async_flash_v1.5` covers 6 (en, es, fr, de, it, pt) and only `async_flash_v1.0` covers the other nine; `nl` appears in neither the model page nor the HTTP reference's 15-code language list. A call with an unsupported code on the default model still succeeds, so a user gets no signal that it was ignored. — *needs a call: whether to warn per model, document the split, or leave the map model-agnostic*
- [asyncai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/asyncai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Async's word-timestamp endpoint is not used by either class — `POST /text_to_speech/with_timestamps` returns `audio_base64` plus an `alignment` object (`words`, `word_start_times_milliseconds`, `word_end_times_milliseconds`) for all three models, and neither Async class calls it, so Pipecat's word-timestamp plumbing stays empty for this provider. It is a non-streaming JSON endpoint: the websocket protocol has no timestamp message at all, and wiring it into `AsyncAIHttpTTSService` would mean giving up the chunked `/text_to_speech/streaming` response it uses today. — *needs a call: whether word timestamps are worth a non-streaming HTTP path, given the websocket class cannot have them either*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Newer Converse request fields are not reachable: `serviceTier`, `Tool.systemTool` (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `serviceTier.type` is one of priority/default/flex/reserved and AWS positions priority as the fastest tier, but neither the default model nor Claude Haiku 5.5 accepts it in the probe account, so there is nothing to verify a pass-through against; `Tool.systemTool` is how Nova 2's built-in web grounding and code interpreter are requested, and the adapter only emits `toolSpec` tools — *needs a call: whether to add fields the probe account cannot exercise, or leave both until a model that accepts them is in reach*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Docs and docstring describe the service as Nova and Claude only, though every Bedrock Converse model works (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Bedrock's text catalogue is 95 model ids including `openai.gpt-6.2-sol`, `xai.grok-4.7`, `moonshotai.kimi-k3.1` and `zai.glm-5.3`; the class docstring and docs.pipecat.ai name only Amazon Nova and Anthropic Claude, and the docs page's example still pins `us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929-v1:0`, which Bedrock marks LEGACY — *needs a call: whether to widen the wording, and whether the docs change belongs in this repo or in the docs repo*
- [aws/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The turn-completion eval manifest pins LEGACY us.meta.llama3-3-70b-instruct-v1:0 — `evals/turn-completion/manifest.yaml` runs the no-tools row on `us.meta.llama3-3-70b-instruct-v1:0`, which the catalogue now marks LEGACY with no EOL date published; it still answers through the class (TTFAT 520 ms). Every Meta Llama on Bedrock is LEGACY, so there is no like-for-like replacement, and Bedrock's lifecycle policy can withdraw a LEGACY model from an account after 15 days of inactivity. — *needs a call: which model takes over the manifest's no-tools row, given that no ACTIVE Meta Llama remains on Bedrock*
- [aws/other](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/other/2026-11-23.md) — Nothing in the repo exercises an AgentCore invocation (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `tests/test_aws_credentials.py` constructs AWSAgentCoreProcessor to check that its client signs with renewed default-chain credentials, but no test or example drives an LLMContextFrame through process_frame, and the AgentCore samples live in pipecat-examples. Nothing here can be run against a deployed agent, which is why this unit has no live probe. — *needs a call: Whether an AgentCore example belongs in this repo, given it needs a deployed agent runtime to run.*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — aws_sdk_bedrock_runtime is pinned below 0.10 while 0.13.1 is current (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The pin `>=0.6.0,<0.10` resolves to 0.9.0; the ceiling is deliberate, because 0.10.0 renames `Config` to `AsyncBedrockRuntimeConfig` and `BedrockRuntimeClient` to `AsyncBedrockRuntimeClient`, the names `create_client()` imports. PyPI's latest moved 0.12.0 to 0.13.1 (2026-11-18) this cycle, so the distance grew but the blocker is the same rename. — *needs a call: whether the 0.10+/0.13 rename migration is worth doing now or the pin holds until the SDK reaches 1.0*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The SYSTEM_SPEECH content role is missing from the Nova Sonic adapter's Role enum (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Nova Sonic accepts SYSTEM_SPEECH as the `role` on a text contentStart to pick the transcription script for Hindi code-switching (Latin / Devanagari / mixed); the enum in `adapters/llm/services/aws_nova_sonic_adapter.py` has only SYSTEM, USER, ASSISTANT, TOOL — *needs a call: a stance on whether Hindi script selection is worth a user-facing knob, and what it would be called*
- [aws/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `endpointing_sensitivity` is typed `str` rather than the documented HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW set (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — a typo reaches the API instead of failing at construction; AWS documents exactly three values — *needs a call: whether to narrow the type now or keep it open for values AWS may add*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `send_sample_rate` clamps to 16000 Hz although AWS accepts 8000-48000 Hz (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The relabelling bug is fixed — `STTService` now resamples every input frame to `send_sample_rate` before `run_stt`. What survives is the clamp itself: 24 kHz and 48 kHz pipeline audio is downsampled to 16 kHz even though `MediaSampleRateHertz` is documented as 8000-48000, and the 2026-08-31 run connected presigned URLs declaring both. The `sample_rate` docstring in `__init__` still says "AWS Transcribe only supports 8000 or 16000 Hz; other values are clamped to 16000 Hz at connect time", which contradicts the API reference and reads as if nothing is converted. — *needs a call: Whether to pass the pipeline rate through when it is within 8000-48000 Hz rather than resample to 16 kHz — the docstring correction follows whichever way that goes.*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `transcript-format` (spoken vs written rendering of numbers and dates) is unreachable (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — AWS documents `x-amzn-transcribe-transcript-format` (`spoken | written`, default written) on StartStreamTranscription, but the presigned-URL websocket endpoint appears not to implement it: the 2026-09-14 run found a signed URL carrying `transcript-format=bogus` connected and transcribed normally, while `partial-results-stability=bogus` on the same endpoint was rejected with "Member must satisfy enum value set: [high, low, medium]". A Settings field would be a silent no-op. — *needs a call: Whether AWS honours `transcript-format` over `/stream-transcription-websocket` at all — worth an AWS support question before spending the plumbing.*
- [aws/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/stt/2026-11-23.md) — No Pipecat service for the new synchronous TranscribeAudio API — AWS announced `TranscribeAudio` on 2026-11-20 — one request carrying a complete clip (25 MB inline, 0.5 s to 1 hour), transcript in the response, at $0.0070/min against $0.01/min for streaming, with diarization, language identification, custom vocabulary and channel identification included. It maps onto `SegmentedSTTService`, not onto the existing websocket class. Only 9 language families, and 13 regions. — *needs a call: Whether a segmented AWS Transcribe service earns its place — it trades the streaming path's interim results and latency for roughly 30% off, so it is a new class and a product call, not a settings change.*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The SynthesizeSpeech LanguageCode parameter is not exposed (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — bilingual voices pick their language from LanguageCode, and the 2026-10-21 generative additions Arjun and Kiara are bilingual (en-IN and hi-IN); the service only emits an SSML `<lang>` wrapper built from the `language` setting — *needs a call: whether `language` should also feed LanguageCode or a separate field is added — the two can disagree*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — OutputFormat is hard-coded to pcm, which Polly caps at 16 kHz (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — generative voices are natively 24 kHz (the SynthesizeSpeech reference gives 24000 as their default sample rate) and only expose that through mp3/ogg_vorbis/ogg_opus; pipelines running at 24 kHz get 16 kHz audio upsampled by the service. Polly also added mulaw/alaw output on 2026-03-25 — *needs a call: reaching 24 kHz means decoding mp3 or ogg in the service; needs a call on the dependency*
- [aws/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/aws/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Speech marks (word timestamps) are not requested (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — SpeechMarkTypes returns word/sentence/viseme/ssml marks, but only as a separate json-format request, so word timing costs a second billed synthesis — and the generative engine does not produce speech marks at all — *needs a call: whether doubling Polly requests is acceptable for word timestamps*
- [azure/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Azure-only chat-completions fields (data_sources, user_security_context) have no named Settings field (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `extra` reaches the request body verbatim (`params.update(self._settings.extra)` in base_llm), so On Your Data and the Defender end-user context are usable today; not probed, since data_sources needs a provisioned AI Search index. This is discoverability, not capability. — *needs a call: whether `extra` stays the documented answer for Azure-only request fields*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Azure's realtime transcription and translation session types are not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Azure exposes dedicated flows for `gpt-realtime-translate` (`/openai/v1/realtime/translations`) and for transcription (`session.type = "transcription"`, models `gpt-realtime-whisper`, `gpt-live-transcribe`). The service only speaks the voice-agent session. — *needs a call: a call on whether these belong in Pipecat as a realtime STT/translation service rather than in this LLM class*
- [azure/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — docs.pipecat.ai has no page for AzureRealtimeLLMService (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `/api-reference/server/services/llm/azure` documents only `AzureLLMService` (200), and `/api-reference/server/services/s2s/azure` still returns 404, so the deployment-name rule for `Settings.model` and the v1-endpoint requirement live only in the class docstring. — *needs a call: the docs site is a separate repo, so this needs an owner there rather than a change here*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The `azure` extra floor (`azure-cognitiveservices-speech>=1.47.0`) is behind the SDK features this class could use (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the floor predates the 1.48.2 CRL fix Microsoft required for Linux/Android by 2026-07-01 and the 1.52 features above (streaming model selection, commit boundary); fresh resolves already get 1.53.1, so only pinned-old installs are exposed — *needs a call: whether provider-watch may move dependency floors, or whether that is a maintainer-owned change.*
- [azure/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Azure's `en-Latn-IN` real-time locale has no `Language` enum member (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — October 2026 added a real-time model that returns romanized Indic transcripts for English, Hindi, Bengali, Kannada, Marathi, Tamil and Telugu without language identification. `STTSettings.language` accepts a raw string, so `Settings(language="en-Latn-IN")` reaches `SpeechConfig` today; only the typed enum path is missing — *needs a call: `Language` is shared by every service, so adding a member is a cross-service call rather than an Azure one.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Personal voice / instant voice cloning is not reachable (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the cloned voice is addressed with `<mstts:ttsembedding speakerProfileId='...'>` wrapping the text (supported by every MAI model, documented as unsupported by both HD families); `_construct_ssml` has no element for it — *needs a call: it is gated access (Limited Access Review) and adds a nesting level to the SSML builder, so it needs a decision on whether Pipecat carries a feature most users cannot enable.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The text streaming input API is not used, so synthesis starts only once a sentence is aggregated (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `SpeechSynthesisRequestInputType.TextStream` on the websocket v2 endpoint accepts LLM tokens as they arrive; Microsoft's sample writes chunks into `request.input_stream` — *needs a call: the streaming input accepts no SSML — only voice name and output format — so every `Settings` field this class exposes would be dropped on that path; whether that is a second mode or a replacement is a product call.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The sentence-boundary silence is hard-coded at 20 ms (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `_construct_ssml` always emits `<mstts:silence type='Sentenceboundary' value='20ms' />`; Azure also defines Leading/Tailing/Comma-exact and the `-exact` variants, none of which a user can select, and both HD families ignore the element entirely — *needs a call: the current value is a deliberate low-latency choice for agents, so exposing it means deciding what the default stays and which silence types are worth a field.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — An unlisted pipeline sample rate silently yields 24 kHz audio tagged with the pipeline's rate (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `sample_rate_to_output_format` falls back to `Raw24Khz16BitMonoPcm` for anything outside {8000, 16000, 22050, 24000, 44100, 48000}, while `TTSAudioRawFrame` is built with `self.sample_rate`, so the audio plays at the wrong speed instead of failing — *needs a call: whether an unsupported rate should raise, resample, or keep today's silent fallback.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — One HD expression adapter serves both HD families, and its style list matches neither (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `_voice_expression_adapter()` returns one `AzureHDExpressionAdapter` for every `:DragonHD*` voice except Flash. Against the HD voices page (updated 2026-11-17), its `_STYLES` declares `ecstatic`, `joking`, `proud`, `relieved`, `shocked` and `terrified`, which only the Omni table lists, and `whispering`, which only the DragonHD table lists. A marker is never spoken aloud on any HD family (word boundary counts are unchanged by it), so the cost is a delivery that may do nothing; last run's duration evidence that `[ecstatic]` is absorbed does not reproduce — plain `en-US-Ava:DragonHDV3Neural` ranges 3.33–3.87 s across three calls, which spans the marked durations. — *needs a call: splitting the adapter per HD family means a second capabilities list and a second class, so it needs a call on whether a style the family may ignore is worth that.*
- [azure/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/tts/2026-11-23.md) — MAI voices perform styles but get no expression adapter — `get_expression_adapter()` returns an adapter only for `:DragonHD*` voices, so expressive-conversation tags are stripped on the MAI family even though Azure documents `mstts:express-as style` for every MAI voice. The static `style` setting does reach them: `en-US-Harper:MAI-Voice-2.1-Flash` with `style=happy` synthesized fine. Each MAI voice has its own style list, and an off-list name is a hard error rather than ignored — `style=happiness` (the value in Azure's own MAI example) failed with `Bad MAI Request ... invalid_request`. — *needs a call: per-voice style lists plus hard failure on an unknown name mean an adapter would have to pick capabilities per voice, so it needs a call on whether to declare only the styles common to all MAI voices or build a per-voice table.*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Provider-hosted tools can only be passed as raw dicts, and their server events are dropped (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — api-version 2026-07-15 documents MCPTool and FoundryAgentTool alongside function tools, plus hosted web-search and file-search calls (`web_search_call`, `file_search_call` items). `VoiceLiveTool` is `FunctionTool | dict`, so a user can declare a hosted tool as a dict, but `parse_server_event` models none of `mcp_list_tools`, `mcp_call`, `mcp_approval_request`, `foundry_agent_call`, `web_search_call` or `file_search_call`, and there is no way to send `mcp_approval_response`. With `require_approval: always` (the default) the session stalls silently. — *needs a call: whether provider-hosted tools belong in a Pipecat realtime service at all, since the approval round-trip needs new client events rather than a settings field*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `avatar`, `animation`, `output_audio_timestamp_types` and `metadata` session properties are unreachable (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — All four are documented session fields dropped by `SessionProperties`. `metadata` and `output_audio_timestamp_types` are pure pass-throughs; `avatar` (video or photo avatars driven by vasa-1/vasa-2, with `scene`, `output_protocol` and `output_audit_audio`) and `animation` (`blendshapes`/`viseme_id`) additionally need their server events (`response.video.delta`, `session.avatar.*`, `response.animation_viseme.*`, `response.audio_timestamp.*`) modelled and pushed, which `parse_server_event` currently returns None for. — *needs a call: whether avatar video output belongs in this service or in a separate processor, before the plain pass-through fields are worth adding on their own*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Image input (`input_image` content part) is not modelled (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `ItemContent.type` is `Literal["input_text", "input_audio", "text", "audio"]`; RealtimeRequestImageContentPart adds `input_image` with `image_url` and `detail` for user messages. The how-to notes image input is not supported with microsoft-realtime-flash-1, so support is per-model. — *needs a call: whether a realtime service should accept vision frames at all, and which Voice Live models support them*
- [azure/voice_live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/azure/voice_live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `InputAudioTranscription.model` allows values the current docs no longer list, including the retired mai-transcribe-1 — The how-to's transcription table (updated 2026-11-19) lists azure-speech, mai-transcribe, mai-transcribe-2, mai-transcribe-2-streaming, whisper-1, gpt-4o-transcribe, gpt-4o-mini-transcribe and gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize. Our Literal additionally allows azure-mrs, mai-transcribe-1, mai-transcribe-1.5 and mai-transcribe-medical; the Foundry schedule retired MAI-Transcribe-1 on 2026-09-15 (replacement MAI-Transcribe-1.5), and MAI-Transcribe-2-Streaming 2026-08-06 is Preview with retirement 2026-12-04. Extra values block nobody, so this is hygiene, but the list now advertises a model the provider has shut down. — *needs a call: whether the undocumented values are still accepted on older deployments, since dropping them from the Literal is a breaking change for anyone passing one*
- [baseten/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/baseten/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Default zai-org/GLM-5.3 is three times slower to first answer token than Baseten models that score higher on aiewf-eval (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — The latency half of this item no longer reproduces. Two interleaved --repeat 5 runs today put GLM-5.3 at TTFAT 872 ms median on a trivial prompt and 1009 ms on a reasoning-triggering one, against 687 ms and 1710 ms for deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 and 1046 ms for deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017; the 1745 ms median recorded on 2026-11-09 looks like serverless congestion. What remains is quality only: aiewf-eval's aiwf_medium_context still ranks deepseek-v4-pro-0813 (low, Baseten) 98.4% / 755 ms above glm-5.3 (low, Z.AI) 97.7% / 2376 ms. The run's other reference point, deepseek-v4-flash-0731 (high, Baseten) 98.6%, is on Baseten's 2026-12-18 retirement list, and its designated replacement deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017 has no leaderboard row. — *needs a call: Whether 0.7 points of aiewf-eval pass rate, with latency now at parity, is reason to move the default across model families when Baseten designates no successor to GLM-5.3.*
- [baseten/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/baseten/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `reasoning_content` from prior assistant turns is not sent back, which the always-on-reasoning models Baseten now serves ask for (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Unchanged: the shared OpenAI chat-completions path still neither surfaces nor replays `reasoning_content` (only `deepseek_adapter.py` and `nvidia/llm.py` handle it), while Baseten's docs list reflection/Beam-1.0 and meta-models/muse-spark-open as always reasoning and Beam's announcement asks callers to preserve the field across turns. — *needs a call: Whether a thin wrapper should carry an adapter for models that are not its default; the fix is adapter work in the shared OpenAI path, not a one-line setting.*
- [baseten/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/baseten/llm/2026-11-23.md) — docs.pipecat.ai's Baseten page lists gpt-oss among the families reachable through Model APIs, which stops being true on 2026-12-18 — Baseten retires openai/gpt-oss-120b and nvidia/NVIDIA-Nemotron-3-Ultra-550B-A55B at 5pm PT on 2026-12-18; the Pipecat page names both families as available. Nothing in this repository pins either slug, so the correction is prose in the docs repo. — *needs a call: The page lives outside this repository, so the edit has to be made there.*
- [bland/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/bland/tts/2026-11-23.md) — A turn over 4,000 characters is rejected with `context_overflow` and nothing in the service splits or warns (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `speak.text` is capped cumulatively per `context_id` (4,000 chars) and `/v2/tts` at 5,000 per request; a long-form turn ends mid-sentence with an ErrorFrame — *needs a call: a stance on whether a long turn should be split across context_ids or the cap just documented*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Camb's `inference_options` (stability, temperature, inference_steps, speaker_similarity) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — MARS-8-family-only tuning knobs, documented as unsupported on the two 8.1 beta models; accepted by the SDK's `tts()` but still absent from the endpoint's published request schema, which shows them in prose and an example only. — *needs a call: a stance on per-model applicability — the fields silently do nothing on the beta models*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `enhance_named_entities_pronunciation` is not exposed (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Top-level boolean on the streaming TTS request, default false, documented as unsupported on the two 8.1 beta models; a one-field pass-through. — *needs a call: a live probe with the field set, which the rejected credential blocks*
- [camb/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/camb/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Camb's inline expressive controls have no expression adapter (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — The streaming endpoint documents inline controls in `text` for two model groups: the 8.1 betas take six non-verbal tags (`[laughter]`, `[sigh]`, `[confirmation]`, `[question]`, `[surprise]`, `[dissatisfaction]`) plus bracketed CMU phoneme overrides, and `mars-instruct` — a GA model — takes free-text delivery tags (`[angry]`, `[gentle, reassuring]`) and SSML-style `<break time="500ms"/>` pauses. There is no `camb_adapter.py` in `src/pipecat/adapters/tts/services/` and `CambTTSService` does not override `get_expression_adapter()`, so expressive conversation emits nothing for Camb. Neither of the two models a real-time bot would pick (mars-flash, mars-pro) performs any of it. — *needs a call: a call on whether a capability confined to two betas and a model Camb says is not for real-time earns an adapter, plus universal names: `laughter`/`sigh` map to `ExpressionSound.LAUGH`/`SIGH`, but `confirmation`, `question`, `surprise` and `dissatisfaction` are affect labels with none*

The reference also documents the value as a `cartesia_version` query parameter rather than the header the service sends, and the header is accepted. The same pin appears in the turns service. — *needs a call: what 2026-08-14 changes for STT - Cartesia's API-versioning page is still behind a login, so the diff could not be read*
- [cartesia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `add_phoneme_timestamps` is not exposed (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — Cartesia can return phoneme-level timestamps alongside the word timestamps the WebSocket service already consumes — *needs a call: a use for phoneme timestamps in the pipeline (viseme/lipsync output) before adding a setting nothing reads*

Reconnecting is correct for `ink-2` keyterms, which the docs say cannot change mid-stream. — *needs a call: whether a threshold change mid-call is worth a second update path, and confirmation the config command is honored under the API version the service pins*

- [cartesia/turns-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/cartesia/turns-stt/2026-11-23.md) — `ink-preview` word timestamps are dropped — `turn.update`, `turn.eager_end` and `turn.end` now carry an optional `words` array (`word`, `start`, `end`) on `ink-preview`; the service passes the raw event through as `result` but surfaces nothing from it — *needs a call: whether per-word timing belongs on STT frames at all, given it exists only on a preview model*

- [deepgram/flux-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-stt/2026-11-23.md) — The `flux_encoding` docstrings say linear16 is required, but Flux documents more encodings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `DeepgramFluxSTTService.__init__` and `DeepgramFluxSTTBase.__init__` both say the encoding "Must be `linear16`", while Flux's `/v2/listen` reference still accepts linear16, linear32, mulaw, alaw, opus and ogg-opus. - [deepgram/flux-tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/deepgram/flux-tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `url` argument says nothing about Deepgram's regional endpoints, and the default host becomes globally routed — Deepgram made `api.us.deepgram.com` GA on 2026-11-23 and will start routing `api.deepgram.com` globally "by the end of January", after which the default host carries no residency promise. - [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `features` flag list (`["quality-guard"]`) is not exposed (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `TTSLiveStartEvent.request` is a `$ref` to the same `TTSRequest` that carries `features`, but a previous run's probe showed the endpoint accepts `["quality-guard"]`, an invented flag name and an invented top-level key alike without error, and `quality-guard` has no documented behavior beyond the field description — *needs a call: whether to expose a backend-dependent flag list whose only documented value has no documented effect and whose misspellings fail silently*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Generation knobs `max_new_tokens`, `repetition_penalty` and `early_stop_threshold` are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — documented start-request fields that sit next to the exposed `temperature` and `top_p`; `early_stop_threshold` is described as applying to batch processing, so its relevance to a streaming pipeline is unclear — *needs a call: whether these are worth the Settings surface area for a voice pipeline*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `latency` docstring names only "normal" and "balanced" (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the spec enumerates `low`, `normal`, `balanced` ("low: lowest latency"); the field is a plain `str`, so `low` already works, it is just undocumented here — *needs a call: whether to recommend `low` when Fish's own real-time streaming guide only mentions normal and balanced*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `mp3_bitrate` and `opus_bitrate` are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — they only apply to the mp3/opus outputs, and `output_format` is an init-only constructor argument defaulting to pcm — *needs a call: whether non-pcm output is a use case worth widening the audio-format arguments for*
- [fish/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/fish/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The `prosody_normalize_loudness` docstring claims drama-3 support (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `ProsodyControl.normalize_loudness` in the spec still scopes the field to the S2 family (`s2-pro`, `s2.1-pro`, `s2.1-pro-free`) and says it is a no-op on `s1`; it says nothing about `drama-3`, which our Settings docstring and the Pipecat docs page both name — *needs a call: whether to drop the drama-3 mention or confirm with Fish that drama-3 honors it*
- [funasr/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/funasr/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Non-SenseVoice models load but get SenseVoice-shaped calls (Fun-ASR-Nano, MLT-Nano, Paraformer) (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Nothing in the service gates the model, but every per-call argument is SenseVoice's. Fun-ASR-Nano's toolkit path builds a Chinese prompt from `language` ("语音转写成{language}") and reads `itn`, not `use_itn`, so our `language="en"` lands in the prompt as a bare code and ITN is ignored; `hotwords` (Nano) and `hotword` (contextual/SeACo-Paraformer) are unreachable; `_FUNASR_LANGUAGES` maps anything outside SenseVoice's `lid_dict` to `auto`, which blocks MLT-Nano's 31 languages; and `rich_transcription_postprocess` is written for SenseVoice output. Not a default candidate: the 1.4.29 model zoo still lists SenseVoiceSmall as the 234M zh/en/ja/ko/yue option beside the 800M GPU Nanos, and no SenseVoice successor exists (SenseVoiceLarge is still unreleased). — *needs a call: a GPU probe before the docstring recommends Nano, and a stance on per-model parameter handling (per-model call building vs documenting the service as SenseVoice-only)*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Gladia's live `post_processing` (summarization) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `/v2/live` accepts `post_processing.summarization` plus `summarization_config.type` (general | bullet_points | concise) — 201 again this run — and `messages_config.receive_post_processing_events` is already exposed, but there is no field to turn the processing on and `_receive_messages` ignores post-processing messages. — *needs a call: whether end-of-session summaries belong in a real-time pipeline at all, and what frame would carry them*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Buffered audio is resent as one websocket frame after a reconnect (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_send_buffered_audio` base64-encodes the whole buffer (up to `max_buffer_size`, 20 MB by default) into a single `audio_chunk` message; Gladia's websocket guidance is 20-50 ms chunks, and Gladia's own SDK 2.0.0 line resends buffered audio in smaller chunks. — *needs a call: confirmation that Gladia rejects or truncates an oversized resend before changing the reconnect path*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `RealtimeProcessingConfig.words_accurate_timestamps` is no longer in Gladia's live schema (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — The field is absent from api.gladia.io/openapi.json and the live init reference, but the endpoint still accepts it (201 this run). `/v2/live` validates strictly, so a user setting it breaks hard the day Gladia drops it. — *needs a call: whether to deprecate the field now or wait until Gladia actually rejects it*
- [gladia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gladia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The shipped GLADIA_TTFS_P99 (1.49 s) only holds when the client is near the processing region (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `region` defaults to Gladia's eu-west. From the US runner, TTFS (speech end → final transcript, the service's own TTFB metric) was 1014 ms median of 3 (986–1419 ms) in eu-west against 919 ms median of 3 (782–948 ms) with `region=us-west`. Both medians are inside the published 1.49 s this run, and the penalty has shrunk every run since the 8653 ms of 2026-09-14, so this is now a documentation question rather than a latency one. — *needs a call: whether to say this in the `region` / `ttfs_p99_latency` docstrings (and which region the published constant was measured in), or to leave it to the stt-benchmark*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Request path uses google-genai's deprecated `language_hints` and `language_auto` instead of top-level `language_codes` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both fields still carry "will be removed in a future version" in google-genai 3.2.0; the 3.0 major passed without removing them. — *needs a call: `language_auto=False` (disable auto-detection with no hints) has no equivalent under `language_codes`, so the migration drops a documented Settings behavior*
- [google/gemini_live-stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/gemini_live-stt/2026-11-23.md) — Voice editing (`source_text`) shipped for both transcribe models and has no Settings field — announced 2026-11-17; `AudioTranscriptionConfig.source_text` takes up to 64 KiB of text the speaker edits by voice, SMART mode only. Mechanically a one-field pass-through, but it only works alongside `mode="SMART"` and inherits that mode's cumulative-transcript behavior. — *needs a call: whether dictation-style voice editing belongs in a conversational STT service at all, and if so it should wait on the SMART-mode item above*
- [google/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/image/2026-11-23.md) — Image controls beyond aspect ratio and size are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the SDK marks `output_mime_type` and `output_compression_quality` "not supported in Gemini API", so the real candidates on `types.ImageConfig` are `person_generation` and `prominent_people`; `thinking_level` is reachable through `GenerateContentConfig.thinking_config` and Search grounding through `tools`, and the service sends neither — *needs a call: which of these users actually ask for — neither `person_generation` nor `prominent_people` appears on Google's image-generation page, and thinking levels differ per model (minimal/medium/high on 2.1, minimal/high on 3.1 Flash Image) and are billed*
- [google/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/image/2026-11-23.md) — Google documents image generation only on the Interactions API (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — every example on the image-generation page is `client.interactions.create` / `/v1beta/interactions`; the migration guide still says generateContent "remains fully supported" with no removal date, and our generate_content calls succeeded 8/8 today, but multi-turn editing via `previous_interaction_id`, video input as context and image_search grounding are documented only on that surface — *needs a call: whether to keep the service on generate_content or plan a move to interactions, which is a rewrite of `run_image_gen` rather than a settings change*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — `service_tier` is not a Settings field (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Priority inference is an Interactions-API-only tier aimed at second-level, non-sheddable responses - squarely a voice-agent concern. It is in preview and costs 75-100% more than standard, with rate limits at 0.3x. It is reachable today without a code change: `extra={"service_tier": "priority"}` passes straight through as a top-level request key. — *needs a call: does a dedicated field earn its place while the tier is in preview and `extra` already reaches it?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Interactions function results accept image, document, audio and video blocks, which the service cannot send (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — A function_result step takes document, audio and video alongside text and image, inline or by URI (2026-11-05), and since 2026-11-18 those payloads can be sliced and include Office formats. Pipecat returns function-call results as text/JSON through the Gemini Interactions adapter, so a tool that produces media has no way to hand it back. The same gap is open on google/llm. — *needs a call: does multimodal tool output belong in the universal LLMContext function-result shape before any one provider exposes it?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Tool retrieval, an Interactions-only way to keep deferred tool schemas out of the prompt, is not reachable from the adapter — Preview since 2026-11-19, for Gemini 3.6 and later including all Gemini 4 models. A caller marks function tools `defer_loading: true` and adds a `{"type": "tool_search"}` tool; the model asks for a schema by name mid-request, producing tool_search_call and tool_search_result steps. `to_provider_tools_format` builds every function tool from the context's ToolsSchema with no room for per-tool flags, and the adapter's `_to_steps` has no form for the two new step types, so a retrieved turn would not round-trip into the next request's context. google-genai carries the types since 3.4.0. — *needs a call: is a tool-heavy agent a shape Pipecat wants to serve here, and should per-tool provider flags live in ToolsSchema rather than in one provider's adapter?*
- [google/interactions-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/interactions-llm/2026-11-23.md) — A continued (long-decode) response logs a spurious incomplete warning and reports only its last hop's tokens (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — google-genai's automatic_continuation defaults to True, and with stream=True each intermediate hop ends with an interaction.completed event whose status is requires_continuation before the next hop's events arrive. `_handle_event` warns 'response incomplete' for any status outside completed/requires_action, and `usage = ... or usage` keeps the last hop's usage, which the SDK documents as per-hop rather than cumulative. Out of reach at the service's 4096-token max_tokens default; a user who raises max_tokens past the per-request decode limit meets it. — *needs a call: should interaction.completed with status requires_continuation be skipped and per-hop usage summed, or is long decode out of scope for a voice pipeline?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Gemini function responses accept audio, video and Office/OpenDocument content and file URIs, which the service cannot send (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Added 2026-11-05 (google-genai `FunctionResultSubContent`; function result schemas landed in the public SDK in 3.6.0). Pipecat returns function-call results as text/JSON through the Gemini adapter, so a tool that produces an image or a file has no way to hand it back to the model. — *needs a call: does multimodal tool output belong in the universal LLMContext function-result shape before any one provider exposes it?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `media_resolution` is not a Settings field on GoogleLLMService (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — Gemini 3 documents `media_resolution` for image/document/video input, and GeminiLiveLLMService already exposes it. It is reachable today through `extra` without a code change. — *needs a call: does a dedicated field earn its place when `extra` already passes it through, and should it be top-level only when Gemini 3 also accepts it per content part?*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The Gemini 4 models are GA and Google points new projects at gemini-4-hydrogen, but none is a default candidate yet — Carbon (2026-11-10), Hydrogen (2026-11-12) and Neon (2026-11-18) are all stable. aiewf-eval's medium-context leaderboard has no row for any of them, and Hydrogen at its lowest level (thinking_level low) probes TTFAT 1426 ms (median of 5) against gemini-3.6-flash's 898 ms, at $1.50/$7.50 per 1M tokens. Hydrogen is positioned for coding and agentic work, not as a Flash successor. — *needs a call: nothing yet — re-check when aiewf-eval benchmarks a Gemini 4 model.*
- [google/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/llm/2026-11-23.md) — ThinkingConfig documents thinking levels for Gemini 2.5 and 3 only, and its Literal omits xhigh — Gemini 4 Hydrogen and Neon accept low/medium/high/xhigh (xhigh added to the SDK's ThinkingLevel in google-genai 3.3.0) and Carbon low/medium/high. `thinking_level` is typed `Literal[...] | str`, so xhigh reaches the API today; only the docstring and the completion list are behind. — *needs a call: list every current model's levels in the docstring, or keep it to the families and point at Google's model pages?*
- [google/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `activity_handling` and `interim_transcript_timestamp_enabled` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — The two `RealtimeInputConfig` fields the service cannot express; the service only ever sets `automatic_activity_detection` and `turn_coverage` on that config. Both are on the SDK type and in the published API reference. `NO_INTERRUPTION` turns off server-side barge-in; unlike `turn_coverage` it moves interruption ownership — the pipeline's own VAD would still interrupt locally while the server keeps generating. — *needs a call: how a server-side NO_INTERRUPTION setting should interact with `broadcast_interruption()` and the pipeline's own interruption handling*
- [google/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Recognition features beyond the transcript flags are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `diarization_config`, `multi_channel_mode`, `max_alternatives`, `transcript_normalization`, `translation_config`, `custom_prompt_config` (Chirp 3, preview), and language-agnostic recognition via `language_codes=["auto"]`, which today is reachable only through the deprecated `language_codes` field — *needs a call: which of these a voice-agent pipeline actually consumes — most produce data no downstream processor reads today*
