- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `markup` input and `advanced_voice_options` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Both Cloud TTS classes always send `SynthesisInput(text=...)`/`StreamingSynthesisInput(text=...)`. `markup` carries Chirp 3 pause tags and Gemini structured markup (probed 2026-09-01: 197804 bytes against 121004 for plain text); `advanced_voice_options` carries `enable_textnorm`, `relax_safety_filters` and `safety_settings`, and the Cloud TTS page now marks the legacy top-level `relax_safety_filters` deprecated in favour of the one inside `advanced_voice_options`. — *needs a call: pause control and custom pronunciations are Preview on Chirp 3 HD, and relaxed safety filters need a monthly-invoiced billing account - is a Settings field worth it for gated features?*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — GeminiTTSService's AVAILABLE_VOICES holds 30 names while the Gemini API voice library has hundreds, and the custom-ID exemption misses `voicekey_` IDs (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — The speech-generation reference documents 30 prebuilt names, Extended Voice Library entries from `GET /v1beta/voices`, `voice_...` IDs from voice design, replication and remixing, and stateless `voicekey_...` keys. Anything outside the hard-coded list logs "not in known voices list. Using anyway.", and the exemption tests `startswith("voice_")`, which `voicekey_...` does not match. Effect is log noise only; the voice is still sent. — *needs a call: should the list be dropped in favour of the Voices API (or the warning relaxed), given the catalogue is now too large and backend-dependent to vendor?*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Gemini API audio output format is not reachable; GeminiTTSService always requests 24 kHz L16 (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — The speech-generation reference documents `response_format.mime_type` (`audio/wav`, `audio/l16`, `audio/mulaw`, `audio/alaw`) and `sample_rate` (8000/16000/24000), which would let a telephony bot take 8 kHz mulaw straight from the model. The service streams `generate_content_stream` at a fixed 24 kHz and resamples to the pipeline rate, so this is now about saving the resample and the bandwidth, not about correctness. — *needs a call: reaching those knobs means calling the dedicated `interactions` TTS endpoint instead of `generate_content_stream` - is a second GenAI request path worth 8 kHz telephony output?*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — docs.pipecat.ai contradicts itself on the GeminiTTSService default (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — The settings table now gives the per-backend defaults, but the Notes section below it still says `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview` is the default on both backends and repeats the superseded November 17 shutdown date. The page lives in the docs repo, not here. — *needs a call: who updates the Notes section in the docs repo to match the per-backend default the code ships?*
- [google/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The google-genai >= 2.25.0 floor the service enforces at runtime is below the extra's pin — `_check_genai_model_supported` pushes an INVALID_REQUEST error when `genai.types.SpeechMetadata` is missing, and the class docstring states the 2.25.0 requirement; the extra now pins `google-genai>=3.2.0,<4`, so the branch is unreachable for anyone installing `pipecat-ai[google]`. — *needs a call: removing an error path is a behavior change - keep the guard for users who pin google-genai themselves, or drop it now the extra floors well above it?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The thinking_level Literal does not list xhigh, which Gemini 4 Hydrogen and Neon accept — google-genai 3.3.0 added XHIGH to ThinkingLevel, and the Hydrogen and Neon model guides list LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/XHIGH. `GoogleLLMService.InputParams.thinking_level` (google/llm.py:113) is `Literal["low", "high", "medium", "minimal"] | str`, so xhigh still reaches the API; only the hint and the completion list are behind. The field is shared with google/llm and google/realtime, whose report may cover the same enum. — *needs a call: add xhigh to the Literal as part of one change across the Gemini services, or leave the `| str` escape hatch to carry new levels?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Vertex AI generative-AI docs are folded into the Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform; the class docstring Reference URL double-redirects (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — The `Reference:` link at services/google/vertex/llm.py:54 (cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/model-reference/inference) still takes two 301s to reach docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/reference/models/inference. Host, model IDs and auth are unchanged - the probes pass - so this is docs drift only. — *needs a call: refresh the docstring link (and any docs.pipecat.ai wording) to the Agent Platform pages, or leave "Vertex" naming alone until Google retires the endpoint?*
- [google/vertex-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The location docstring says global is the only location serving the Gemini 3 series, and single regions now serve it too (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — services/google/vertex/llm.py:89-91 says global is "the only location that serves the Gemini 3 series; the regional endpoints serve the 2.5 series". The us multi-region answers (TTFAT 1123 ms), and the 2026-11-09 release note makes gemini-3.8-flash GA on the northamerica-northeast1 and asia-northeast1 regional endpoints. A probe at northamerica-northeast1 returned 404 for the probe project, which the error attributes to either the model or project access, so the regional claim rests on the release note rather than on a probe. Global is still the right default. — *needs a call: spell out the multi-regions and the two single regions Google now lists, or keep the simpler global-only guidance so nobody reads it as endorsing single regions?*
- [google/vertex-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Vertex custom voices (`replicated_voice_config`) are not reachable from the service (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — the session config is built with `VoiceConfig(prebuilt_voice_config=PrebuiltVoiceConfig(voice_name=...))` and nothing else, so the `voice` setting can only name one of the 30 prebuilt voices; `VoiceConfig.replicated_voice_config` (a 10–20 s PCM sample plus its mime type, per session) has no way in, and `Settings.extra` is never read when the Live config is assembled. Vertex-only — the Gemini Developer API's Live guide has no equivalent — *needs a call: a voice sample is bytes, not a string, so exposing it needs a stance on how audio reaches a setting; access is also limited to customers Google allowlists*
- [google/vertex-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/google/vertex-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The Vertex override that suppresses `history_config` looks obsolete (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `GeminiLiveVertexLLMService._get_history_config` still returns None (added 2026-03-27); the 3.8 migration guide tells Vertex users to seed history with `initial_history_in_client_content=True`, and a Vertex 3.8 session recalled seeded history identically with and without the flag (2026-10-12) — *needs a call: whether the flag changes anything on Vertex's audio-input history-recall path — both checks seeded history as text, not audio*
- [gradium/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gradium/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `temp` and `padding_bonus` are not exposed in Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `temp` is a documented json_config knob (0.0-1.5 on the settings guide, spelled `temperature` and capped at 1.0 default 0 on the endpoint reference). `padding_bonus` stays contested: the settings guide lists it in the STT quick reference at -4.0 to 4.0, the endpoint reference documents it for TTS only. Out-of-range values are a server error — *needs a call: probe verification is blocked on GRADIUM_API_KEY, and the temperature range/key name plus whether `padding_bonus` applies to STT at all need settling against one of the conflicting pages*
- [gradium/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gradium/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `delay_in_frames` default, allowed-value list and flush_id type drift from Gradium's documented values (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — we default `delay_in_frames` to 12 where Gradium documents 10 everywhere it states a default, and the deprecated `InputParams` docstring still says 10. Four pages still disagree on the allowed values: our docstring's ten match the endpoint reference, the turn-taking recipe lists eleven (adding 32), the settings guide says 7-56 and model-dependent, the WebSocket STT guide says 7-55 while suggesting 16. 12 is valid under all four. Separately `_send_flush` sends `flush_id` as a string where the reference types it an integer — *needs a call: a call on whether to align our default with Gradium's 10, and confirmation the server keeps accepting a string flush_id*
- [gradium/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gradium/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `run_tts`'s inline reconnect keeps stale entries in `_setup_context_ids` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_setup_context_ids` is cleared only in `_disconnect_websocket`. The `WebsocketService` reconnect path is fine — `_reconnect_websocket` calls `_disconnect_websocket` before reopening — but `run_tts`'s own `state is State.CLOSED` branch calls `_connect()` directly, so a context already set up on the dead socket keeps its id and the new socket receives `text` without a `setup`. Narrow window: ids are discarded on `end_of_stream`, and with the session cap at 3000 s it needs a socket drop mid-context. — *needs a call: whether a mid-context socket drop is worth handling here, or whether clearing the set in `_connect` is simply the right thing to do*
- [gradium/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gradium/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `Settings.language` has no effect on synthesis (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Gradium's TTS setup still has no language field — language follows the voice (and Voice Localization, which mints ordinary voice ids), and text normalization follows `rewrite_rules` in `json_config`. We default it to None and never send it, but Gradium's Pipecat page advertises `settings.language` as the synthesis language. — *needs a call: decide whether language should map onto `rewrite_rules` or be documented as unsupported for this service*
- [gradium/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/gradium/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Telephony and low-rate output formats are not used (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `output_format` is hard-coded to `pcm` (48 kHz) with `SAMPLE_RATE = 48000`; Gradium also offers `ulaw_8000` (the older reference page also spells it `mulaw_8000`), `alaw_8000`, lower PCM rates, `wav` (the server default) and `opus`, so a telephony pipeline pays for a 48 kHz downsample it could ask the server to skip. Gradium's telephony recipe says outright to request a format the bridge can play and avoid resampling. — *needs a call: whether asking Gradium for the transport's rate is worth diverging from the fixed 48 kHz the docs promise*
- [groq/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/llm/2026-11-23.md) — GroqLLMService leaves Groq's reasoning default in place: gpt-oss-120b thinks on every turn (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — fourth measurement, and `reasoning_effort="low"` won both prompts again — 249 ms TTFAT against 372 ms shipped on a trivial prompt (median of 5 each, spreads 99–282 vs 219–539) and 376 ms against 497 ms on a reasoning-triggering one (median of 3 each, 363–429 vs 469–514, non-overlapping). Across four runs `low` leads six of eight comparisons; the two exceptions are both from earlier runs (2026-10-28 trivial, 2026-10-12 hard). — *needs a call: a call on whether GroqLLMService should ship `reasoning_effort="low"` — it changes default behavior, so it is a maintainer decision rather than an automatic bump*
- [groq/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Groq's `reasoning_format` and `include_reasoning` are not reachable as Settings fields (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — both are Groq's own parameters, so they need an `extra_body` pass-through (`BaseOpenAILLMService._merge_extra_body`) rather than a plain kwarg; `Settings.extra` reaches them today. Groq's reasoning docs still say `reasoning_format` is unsupported on the GPT-OSS models, and no catalogue model leaks reasoning into the spoken text. — *needs a call: Groq documents the two as mutually exclusive, so exposing both needs a stance on a user setting both; `reasoning_effort` covers the common case*
- [groq/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/llm/2026-11-23.md) — qwen/qwen3.8-27b is a Groq preview model and has no Groq-hosted aiewf-eval row (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — leaderboard unchanged again — qwen3.8-27b reads 96.0% / 649 ms (Baseten FP8) and 95.1% / 101 ms (local RTX 5090), neither on Groq, and no Groq-hosted row exists at all since the gpt-oss-120b (groq) row was archived. — *needs a call: re-check when Groq promotes qwen3.8-27b to production and a Groq-hosted row appears on the leaderboard; preview models are never defaults*
- [groq/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Groq's `xhigh` reasoning effort is outside the `Literal` on GroqLLMSettings.reasoning_effort (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — Groq documents `xhigh` (the default) on `meta-models/muse-spark-open` only, an Enterprise limited-preview model. `Settings` is a plain dataclass, so the `Literal` is a type-checker and docstring gate, not a runtime one, and `Settings.extra` reaches the parameter regardless. — *needs a call: the model is Enterprise limited preview, so the value cannot be probed from this account; adding `xhigh` means widening a Literal for a model we cannot verify against*
- [groq/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Groq's 10-second minimum billed length per request is not surfaced anywhere in Pipecat (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Groq bills a 10-second minimum per transcription request; SegmentedSTTService sends one request per user utterance, and conversational turns are typically 1–3 s, so real cost is several times the audio duration. Neither the service docstring nor the docs page mentions it. — *needs a call: a note belongs in the docs repo, not this one — confirm the docs team wants it before opening anything*
- [groq/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Segments are uploaded at the pipeline's sample rate even though Groq downsamples everything to 16 kHz — SegmentedSTTService gained a `segment_sample_rate` hook on 2026-11-10 and the local models (Whisper, Moonshine, FunASR) override it to 16 kHz; the whisper HTTP services do not, so a 24 kHz pipeline uploads 1.5x the bytes Groq will use. Groq's speech-to-text page states the models automatically downsample to 16 kHz mono and that client-side downsampling exists to shrink the upload. — *needs a call: whether the smaller upload measurably lowers TTFB at conversational segment lengths, and whether the override belongs on BaseWhisperSTTService (reaching OpenAI's gpt-4o transcribe models too) rather than GroqSTTService alone*
- [groq/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Settings.speed is accepted by the API but has no effect on the audio (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the 2026-11-09 run measured speed=1.5 returning the same 4.96 s of audio as the default; the field's docstring still calls it a "Speech speed multiplier" with no caveat, and only an inline code comment records that Groq ignores it — *needs a call: whether to document the no-op in the Settings docstring or warn when a user sets a value other than 1.0*
- [groq/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/groq/tts/2026-11-23.md) — No guard on Orpheus's documented 200-character input limit (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Groq still documents that "The input text length is limited to 200 characters"; earlier runs synthesized a 235-character request without complaint, and Pipecat's sentence aggregation routinely exceeds 200 characters — *needs a call: confirm whether over-limit input degrades quality before adding any splitting or warning — the API does not reject it*
- [heygen/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/tts/2026-11-23.md) — HTTP sentence requests carry no `previous_request_ids` / `next_request_ids`, so prosody restarts at each sentence — HeyGen's continuity IDs (up to 3, valid two hours, instant clones only) stitch consecutive requests; HeyGenHttpTTSService sends one request per sentence and discards the response's request ID — *needs a call: plumbing request IDs out of responses and back into the next request is more than a pass-through field, so it needs a design call*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-23.md) — LITE session field agora_config is not on LiveAvatarNewSessionRequest (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — still the one LITE session field Pipecat cannot set (`AgoraConfigSchema`; app id, token, channel, uid); it swaps LiveKit for Agora as the avatar transport — *needs a call: nothing in Pipecat consumes an Agora-hosted avatar today — the client only knows how to join the LiveKit room from the start response — so the field would be unreachable from the service*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-23.md) — avatar_persona is exposed on the LITE session request but LiveAvatar ignores it (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — today's copy of `LiteSDKSessionTokenConfigDataSchema` still has no `avatar_persona`, so voice, context and language set there silently do nothing while Pipecat supplies the audio — *needs a call: whether to drop the field from the LITE request model or document it as FULL-mode only*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-23.md) — agent.speak_started / agent.speak_ended events are not handled (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both fired in order again in today's session, each carrying the utterance id as `source_event_id`; TTFB instead keys off the first BotStartedSpeakingFrame decoded from the LiveKit track — *needs a call: whether avatar-speech metrics should come from these events rather than from the LiveKit track*
- [heygen/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/heygen/video/2026-11-23.md) — No handling for the per-caller rate limits LiveAvatar enforces (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — 10 req/s per API key, and 5 req/s per session token on sessions start/stop/keep-alive; over-limit requests return 429 with Retry-After and X-RateLimit-* headers. `LiveAvatarApi._request` raises LiveAvatarApiError on any non-ok status, so a 429 during session setup surfaces as a setup failure rather than a retry. — *needs a call: whether a retry belongs in the service or in the application spawning sessions; a single bot never approaches the limit*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Per-modality usage in `response.done` (`usage.tts`, `usage.stt`, `usage.llm.model`, cached and reasoning token details) is dropped (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_handle_evt_response_done` reads only `total/input/output_tokens` into `LLMTokenUsage`; a probe's `response.done` carried `llm`, `tts`, `input_token_details` (`cached_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`) and `output_token_details` alongside them, and those are the canonical billing signals for the cascade — *needs a call: which Pipecat metric a cascaded realtime service should report TTS/STT seconds and cached tokens under*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `idle_timeout_ms` is settable but its server event `input_audio_buffer.timeout_triggered` is ignored (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — a user can arm the server idle timer through `TurnDetection.idle_timeout_ms`, but `_server_event_types` has no `input_audio_buffer.timeout_triggered` entry and the receive loop drops it — nothing in the pipeline learns the user went quiet. The sibling smart-turn events `input_audio_buffer.turn_suggestion` / `.turn_suggestion_revoked` are unmodelled too, as are `output_audio_buffer.*`, `rate_limits.updated` and `conversation.item.deleted` / `.retrieved` / `.truncated` — *needs a call: which Pipecat frame an idle timeout should become, given the service already proposes turn boundaries through `ProposedUserStoppedSpeakingFrame`*
- [inworld/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The inherited `LLMSettings` fields (`max_tokens`, `top_p`, `top_k`, penalties, `seed`) are still logged as unhandled (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the branch on this run makes the same controls reachable under Inworld's own names on `SessionProperties`, so the capability is there; what remains is that a user who sets the generic names gets a warning and no effect — *needs a call: whether the generic names should be mapped onto Inworld's, or documented as unsupported for this service*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `languageSettings.candidates` (the shortlist Inworld picks from when auto-detecting language) is not reachable (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — WebSocket create config only, up to 10 BCP-47 tags; the service sends a single `language` or nothing — *needs a call: whether a WebSocket-only language shortlist belongs on the shared Settings beside the existing single `language`, and whether it takes `Language` values or raw tags*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The Pipecat docs page still offers `inworld-tts-1.5-max` / `inworld-tts-1.5-mini` and does not mention `inworld-tts-2-flash` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both 1.5 models are deprecated at Inworld; the page lives in the docs repo, not here — *needs a call: someone with access to the docs repo to update the model list*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `temperature=1.1` is inert in both Inworld examples (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — in `examples/realtime/realtime-ultravox-text.py` it is passed as a constructor argument that does not exist and is swallowed by `**kwargs`; in `examples/voice/voice-inworld.py` it reaches the request, but Inworld documents `temperature` as ignored on `inworld-tts-2` — *needs a call: whether the examples should drop `temperature` outright, given the default model ignores it, rather than move it onto `Settings`*
- [inworld/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/inworld/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `audioConfig.bitRate` and `timestampType: CHARACTER` are not reachable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `bitRate` only matters for the compressed encodings the `encoding=` argument already allows (MP3, OGG_OPUS); `timestampType` is hard-coded to `WORD` — *needs a call: a call on whether either is worth surfacing, given word timestamps are what the word-timestamp plumbing consumes*
- [keenable/tool](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/keenable/tool/2026-11-23.md) — Keenable's `session_id` is never sent, so an agent's calls are not grouped (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — on both web tools (max 128 chars); Keenable asks for one value repeated across a session, which is a per-session identifier the wrapper could mint and pin rather than a knob a user tunes — left to the model today, it varies call to call or is omitted — *needs a call: whether the wrapper should mint and pin a session id by default, which changes what every call sends*
- [keenable/tool](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/keenable/tool/2026-11-23.md) — Keenable's integrator side channel `_meta["keenable/overrides"]` is unreachable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — it is the documented way to pin `mode` and the only way to reach `skip_cache`; the wrapper pins `mode` as a tool argument instead, which the live schema still accepts, and `MCPClient._call_tool_text` passes no `meta=` to `session.call_tool` although mcp 2.3.0 still takes one — *needs a call: whether `skip_cache` is worth a request-`_meta` hook in MCPClient, given the argument path already pins the mode*
- [kokoro/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/kokoro/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The fp16/int8 and kokoro-v1.1-zh model variants can only be selected by downloading them by hand (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `Settings.model` is unused; the fp16 (164 MB) and int8 (114 MB) exports and the Chinese/English kokoro-v1.1-zh model with its 103-voice pack are reachable only through the `model_path`/`voices_path` constructor arguments. — *needs a call: whether a `model` value should map to a download URL pair, and whether the zh model's separate voice pack fits that mapping.*
- [kokoro/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/kokoro/tts/2026-11-23.md) — kokoro-onnx's `trim`, `sentence_pause` and `clause_pause` controls are not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — they tune the silence between synthesis batches, which is what a listener hears as sentence pacing. — *needs a call: not a plain pass-through — pauses are only inserted when `trim` is on, so the three settings have to be exposed as a group with that interaction documented.*
- [mem0/memory](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mem0/memory/2026-11-23.md) — Service drives the synchronous Mem0 clients through `asyncio.to_thread` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — mem0ai 2.x ships `AsyncMemoryClient` and `AsyncMemory`; using them would drop the thread hop on every context frame — *needs a call: a call on whether the churn is worth it, since `to_thread` already keeps the event loop free*
- [mem0/memory](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mem0/memory/2026-11-23.md) — mem0 User Profiles (SDK 2.2.0) are not used to seed context (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `MemoryClient.get_profile()`/`generate_profile()` return a schema-shaped summary of an entity's memories, refreshed about every 10 messages — a cheaper session-start alternative to the per-turn search this service runs. Still beta, enabled per organization on request to mem0 support — *needs a call: whether a beta, request-only feature belongs in the service at all, and if so whether a profile replaces or supplements the per-turn search results*
- [meta/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/meta/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The file-transcription endpoint has no Pipecat service (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `POST https://api.meta.ai/v1/asr/transcribe` (multipart `request` + `audio`, WAV, 10 min / 32 MB cap) shares models, modes, `languageBias` and `keywords` with the realtime socket; it would fit a `SegmentedSTTService` subclass. — *needs a call: a call on whether a batch Meta STT class is wanted at all, given the realtime socket covers the voice-agent case*
- [minimax/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/minimax/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `timbre_weights` voice mixing is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — up to four voice_id/weight (1-100) pairs blended into one timbre; the T2A reference labels the field legacy — *needs a call: a nested list-of-objects field on a field MiniMax calls legacy, so it needs a call on whether to expose it at all*
- [minimax/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/minimax/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Audio output format is fixed at pcm/mono with no way to request opus or pcmu_raw (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `_audio_bitrate` (128000), `_audio_format` ("pcm") and `_audio_channel` are init-only private attributes; the reference now scopes `force_cbr` to streamed mp3 and `output_format` to non-streaming requests, so neither applies to our streaming pcm path — only `format` itself (mp3, flac, wav, pcmu_raw, pcmu_wav, opus) is genuinely out of reach — *needs a call: pcm is what the pipeline consumes, so exposing format at all needs a reason beyond parity with the API*
- [mistral/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Mistral-specific request parameters have no Settings fields — `prompt_cache_key`, `safe_prompt`, `parallel_tool_calls`, `response_format`, `stop`, `guardrails`, `prompt_mode` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the provider-only ones (`safe_prompt`, `guardrails`, `prompt_mode`) are reachable only through `extra={"extra_body": {...}}`, since the OpenAI client rejects unknown keyword arguments. `prompt_cache_key` is the one with a concrete payoff (Mistral bills cached prompt tokens at 10%). — *needs a call: whether `extra["extra_body"]` is the intended home for provider-specific knobs on OpenAI-compatible services, or whether the common ones earn typed fields.*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-23.md) — uv.lock keeps the dev and CI environment on mistralai 2.10.1 although the pin now allows 3.x — `uv lock --upgrade-package mistralai --dry-run` reports 2.10.1 -> 3.8.0; a fresh `pipecat-ai[mistral]` install gets 3.8.0, so the `language` hint path and every realtime-3 session field are exercised by users but never by our tests (the language tests mock `connect`) — *needs a call: whether to refresh the lock for mistralai on its own, given it also moves MistralSTTService and MistralTTSService's runtime*
- [mistral/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `target_streaming_delay_ms` is constructor-only, not runtime-updatable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the main accuracy/latency knob (multiples of 80 from 80 to 1200, or 2400; provider default 480 ms). Moving it into Settings would work for realtime 3 too, since `_update_settings` reconnects and realtime 3 locks session fields after the first audio chunk. — *needs a call: moving it into Settings would duplicate the existing constructor argument, so it needs a stance on deprecating that argument*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `language` is accepted in MistralTTSSettings but never sent (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the speech request still has no language parameter in 3.8.0; docs.pipecat.ai already describes the field as sentence-splitting only, while the `MistralTTSSettings` docstring says "Language for speech synthesis" — *needs a call: whether unsupported inherited fields should be documented as no-ops here or dropped from the docstring*
- [mistral/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/mistral/tts/2026-11-23.md) — No Pipecat service for the realtime TTS model voxtral-mini-tts-realtime-3-0 (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Mistral's audio overview routes realtime TTS over a websocket at `/v1/audio/speech/realtime`; the model is still absent from our account's catalogue, and a websocket TTS service is a new class, not a settings change — *needs a call: whether a realtime Mistral TTS service is wanted at all, and whether the model is generally reachable yet*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Speaker diarization (`identify_speakers`) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — diarization runs locally via the `identify_speakers` transcriber option plus `diarization_model_dir` from `get_diarization_model()` (an 8.2 MB download), filling `TranscriptLine.speaker_spans`; per-VAD-segment transcription makes it near-useless for a single-user turn, so it matters only for multi-speaker audio — *needs a call: `TranscriptionFrame` cannot carry speaker spans, so surfacing them needs a stance like Speechmatics' speaker-label formatting*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Word timestamps are not requested or surfaced (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `get_model_for_language(..., include_word_timestamps=True)` plus the `word_timestamps` transcriber option fills `TranscriptLine.words`; the service asks for neither — *needs a call: Pipecat's `TranscriptionFrame` has nowhere to carry word timings, so this needs a frame-level decision first*
- [moonshine/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/moonshine/stt/2026-11-23.md) — moonshine-voice ships on-device TTS with no Pipecat service to reach it (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the package exposes `list_tts_voices`, `get_tts_voice_catalog`, voice cloning and a streaming synthesizer, and 0.1.6's docs added a Phonon TTS backend; Pipecat has no `MoonshineTTSService`, so nothing in the inventory watches it — *needs a call: whether a local Moonshine TTS service is wanted next to Kokoro*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The language map covers 10 of Neuphonic's 44 documented languages (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — docs and the live voice catalogue now agree on the same 44 codes (the catalogue's extra `ms` is gone, and it is down to 120 voices); unmapped languages still reach the API through `resolve_language(..., use_base_code=True)` but log a warning — *needs a call: whether to hand-verify the remaining codes against a matching voice before adding them, since the map is meant to be the verified set*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Default sample rate is 22050 while Neuphonic's documented default is 24000 (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both classes hard-default `sample_rate=22050`; Neuphonic supports 8000/16000/22050/24000 and documents 24000 — *needs a call: raising the shipped default changes output for every existing user — is the quality worth it?*
- [neuphonic/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/neuphonic/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `Settings(model=...)` is silently ignored (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the store sets `model=None` and no request carries a model; the docs now state that every cloud voice runs on NeuTTS-Max and there is still no model parameter, so there is nothing to send — *needs a call: whether to reject a model in `Settings`, document it as unsupported, or leave the no-op*
- [novita/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/novita/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Novita's reasoning models need `chat_template_kwargs` to turn thinking off, reachable only via the `extra_body` nesting in `extra` (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `settings=Settings(extra={"extra_body": {"chat_template_kwargs": {"thinking": False}}})` probes green on kimi-k3.1-pro (1899 ms -> 1498 ms median); nothing in the class docstring or docs.pipecat.ai says so — *needs a call: whether this belongs in the class docstring here or only in the docs repo*
- [nvidia/omni-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/omni-llm/2026-11-23.md) — No TTFAT metric in audio input mode (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `service_metadata_frame()` reports `is_realtime_service=True` in audio input mode, which makes `LLMService.reports_ttfat` False, so no `TTFATMetricsData` is emitted even though Omni answers in text tokens; re-probed, the same prompt reports TTFAT 431 ms with `audio_input=False` and none with the default — *needs a call: whether the realtime flag should keep gating TTFAT for a service that answers in text rather than audio*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — The default boosted_lm_score of 4.0 is above the range 26.10.0 documents for RNNT and TDT models (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — 26.10.0 widened word-boosting scores to -200..200 for CTC models and Nemotron ASR Streaming but kept 0.5..2.0 for other RNNT and TDT models; both services default `boosted_lm_score` to 4.0. The hosted multilingual Parakeet RNNT deployment accepts 4.0 and the boost works (probed this run), so the out-of-range case is unobserved — *needs a call: whether to lower the default or document it per model — the out-of-range case needs a self-hosted Parakeet TDT or RNNT NIM to observe*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Hosted offline deployments return words without speaker tags, so diarization has no visible effect — an ad-hoc Riva `offline_recognize` against the hosted multilingual Parakeet with diarization enabled returned 18 words, all with `speaker_tag` 0, which the service maps to no speaker; the clip has a single speaker, so this is suggestive rather than conclusive — *needs a call: a multi-speaker clip (or a self-hosted NIM with the Sortformer diarizer profile) to tell a missing diarizer apart from a one-speaker result*
- [nvidia/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Nemotron Transcribe is not reachable from any NVIDIA STT class (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — 26.10.0's Nemotron Transcribe (40 languages, instruction-driven offline transcription, translation to and from English) is served only over an OpenAI-compatible HTTP API (`/v1/audio/transcriptions`, `/v1/chat/completions`), which NVIDIA's own ASR reference excludes from the gRPC and realtime docs both of our paths use. `OpenAISTTService` already takes a `base_url`, so a self-hosted deployment is reachable that way — *needs a call: whether NVIDIA Nemotron Transcribe deserves a class or docs of its own, or stays an `OpenAISTTService` base_url recipe*
- [nvidia/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/nvidia/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Zero-shot prompt transcript (`zero_shot_data.transcript`) is not exposed (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the service sets `audio_prompt`, `encoding` and `quality` but leaves `transcript` (and `sample_rate_hz`) unset, both of which riva-client 2.28.0 accepts alongside the prompt audio — *needs a call: the transcript belongs with the constructor's `zero_shot_audio_prompt_file`, so exposing it means a new constructor parameter, and hosted zero-shot needs NVIDIA approval to probe*
- [openai/image](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/image/2026-11-23.md) — Images API `safety_identifier` is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — a plain optional string OpenAI uses for abuse monitoring, and the documented replacement for the deprecated `user` parameter; `SpeechifyTTSService` is the only Pipecat service that exposes one today — *needs a call: a call on whether per-user safety identifiers belong in each OpenAI service's Settings - the LLM, STT and TTS services accept the same parameter and none exposes it, so doing it here alone sets the pattern by accident*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Native Decisions delegation is not modeled (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — a session can delegate straight to Decisions with `delegation = {"type": "decisions", "decisions": {...}}` — a third member of the SDK's delegation union alongside client and responses. The service's union is `ClientDelegationConfig | ResponsesDelegationConfig` and its constructor takes `ResponsesDelegation | ClientDelegation`, so the mode cannot be chosen. Answers arrive as `decision.event` correlated by `delegation_id`, which events.py also lacks — *needs a call: whether a bounded-choice backend is a mode Pipecat wants to own, given it needs a third delegation class plus decision-event routing*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Client delegation cannot delay the model's acknowledgement (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — `acknowledgement_delay_ms` holds back the live model's automatic acknowledgement so the app can send a silent update first, which is exactly the window a Pipecat client-delegation backend needs before the model fills the silence. The SDK carries it on the client and decisions delegation configs; `events.ClientDelegationConfig` carries only `type` — *needs a call: whether the knob belongs on the `ClientDelegation` dataclass as a new field, or should be derived from the backend's own timing*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Input audio cannot be muted server-side (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `session.input_audio.mute` / `.unmute` and their `muted` / `unmuted` acknowledgments are not in events.py, so the only way to stop the model listening is for the pipeline to stop forwarding audio, which leaves a gap in the session timeline rather than a mute the server acknowledges — *needs a call: whether a pipeline that withholds audio is good enough, and if not what the surface should be (a service method, or a frame the transport can trigger)*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The session audio format is always 24 kHz PCM16 (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `events.AudioFormat` models `audio/pcm`, `audio/pcmu` and `audio/pcma`, but `_send_session_config` never sets `audio.format`; input is resampled to 24 kHz and `SpeechOutputAudioRawFrame` is hard-coded to `OPENAI_SAMPLE_RATE`. The server confirms the default, echoing `format: {type: audio/pcm, rate: 24000}` in every started session. A telephony bot therefore resamples 8 kHz G.711 both ways instead of letting the session carry it — *needs a call: whether an 8 kHz G.711 session is worth threading the chosen format through the input resampler and the output frame rate*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Stored sessions cannot be forked (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — a stored session is forked by connecting to `wss://api.openai.com/v1/live/sessions/{id}/fork` and sending `session.start` with overrides (`store`, Responses delegation settings, `audio.format`). Both halves are technically reachable today — `on_session_started` hands the app the `SessionResource`, whose `id` it keeps, and `base_url` is a constructor argument that can name the fork endpoint — so what is missing is a supported way to say "resume this session" — *needs a call: whether forking deserves a first-class surface (a `fork_from` argument, say) or whether pointing `base_url` at the fork endpoint is the documented answer*
- [openai/live-realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/live-realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Server-side audio fixtures cannot be played — `session.output_audio.fixture.play` hands the session one complete clip (base64 mono PCM16 at 24 kHz, 30 s max) that the server validates in full, then interrupts model speech and mutes input while it plays, acknowledging with `fixture.playing` and `fixture.played`. Probed this run on a plain WebSocket session: 200 ms of silence returned both acknowledgments, so the whole feature is reachable over the transport the service already uses. events.py models neither the client event nor the two acknowledgments. The pipeline can of course push its own audio frames, but only the fixture gets the model to stop talking and the input dropped for the duration — *needs a call: how a bot would ask for a fixture — a service method, or a frame the service intercepts — and whether the dropped input and interrupted speech should surface as the usual interruption frames*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — tool_choice cannot name a specific function or MCP tool (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — ours is `Literal["auto", "none", "required"]` on both SessionProperties and ResponseProperties; GA's RealtimeToolChoiceConfig also takes an object forcing one function or MCP tool — *needs a call: whether forcing a tool is worth supporting on a realtime service, where the service drives responses itself*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — A custom voice object cannot be passed to AudioOutput.voice (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `voice` is typed `str | None`, so every built-in voice works, but GA's `{"id": "voice_1234"}` custom-voice form does not. The prompt-to-voice voices OpenAI released on 2026-10-16 do not widen this — voices created from text prompts work only in Live; Realtime custom voices must come from audio samples — *needs a call: whether Pipecat exposes OpenAI custom voices at all — no other OpenAI service in the repo does*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — The documented OpenAI-Safety-Identifier connection header has no supported setting (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Connection headers live in an overridable `_auth_headers()` hook (AzureRealtimeLLMService already overrides it), so a subclass can add the header without copying `_connect`. The hook is private and undocumented, so there is still no configuration path. OpenAI's Realtime guide documents the header and probing in an earlier run showed the API accepts it (full turn, TTFB 477 ms) — *needs a call: whether the private hook is answer enough, or the identifier wants a named setting — the automatic-PR rules exclude a new constructor argument, and a Settings field would be the first that configures the connection rather than the session*
- [openai/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Out-of-band responses cannot be built — ResponseProperties has no input or conversation field (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `send_client_event` is public, so a user can send a `ResponseCreateEvent`, but `ResponseProperties` models only seven fields and GA's response-create params also take `conversation` ("none" for an out-of-band response) and `input` (a fresh item list for this response alone, which since openai 3.41.0 may cite session items as `{"type": "item_reference", "id": ...}`) — *needs a call: whether out-of-band responses are a pattern Pipecat wants to support on a service that drives its own responses, and if so whether they belong on ResponseProperties or on a dedicated API*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — Responses request fields text.verbosity, prompt_cache_key/prompt_cache_retention/prompt_cache_options, context_management, image.context_compression, background_options, truncation, tool_choice, parallel_tool_calls, max_tool_calls, moderation and reasoning.context have no named Settings field (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — `extra` is merged into the request verbatim at the end of `_build_response_params`, so this is discoverability, not capability. New this run: `background_options`, which selects the two-hour and eight-hour background completion windows added on 2026-11-17 and is of no use to a streaming voice turn. `reasoning.context` remains the one that is not a clean pass-through, since it interacts with the encrypted-reasoning round-trip — *needs a call: a stance on whether these become named fields or `extra` stays the documented answer for Responses-only knobs*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — service_tier is a constructor argument only, so the tier cannot be changed at runtime (since 2026-08-25, 12 weeks) — Ultrafast is access-controlled per model and OpenAI lets an organization restrict which tiers its projects may use, so picking a tier per model means rebuilding the service; every other request knob lives in Settings. The same docstring lists "flex", which OpenAI said on 2026-11-17 is winding down and which new models will not support, though no shutdown date is published — *needs a call: whether service_tier should move into Settings, which would leave two ways to set it, and whether the docstring should mark "flex" as winding down before OpenAI dates its removal*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — gpt-6-astra, gpt-6.2-astra and gpt-6.2-sol reject temperature and top_p, and the service passes them through without warning (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — setting temperature on any of the three returns 400 "Unsupported parameter", and OpenAI's GPT-6.2 guide tells integrations to omit temperature, top_p and top_logprobs outright. Only users who set them are affected (both default to NOT_GIVEN); the service already has the `_warn_if_reasoning_unsupported` pattern for exactly this kind of per-model constraint — *needs a call: whether per-model parameter support is worth tracking in the service, or the provider's own error message is good enough*
- [openai/responses-llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/responses-llm/2026-11-23.md) — The approval-gated reasoning models gpt-6-rosalind, gpt-6-cyber and gpt-6.2-astra-law have no reasoning-defaults entry, and OpenAI publishes no effort list for any of them (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — all three are Responses models behind trusted-access approval, so none appears in our catalogue and none can be probed. gpt-6.2-astra-law is new this run — the openai SDK 3.53.0 added the identifier on 2026-11-17 but the model page 404s, as gpt-6-rosalind's does. A bot that selects any of them runs at OpenAI's medium default and logs the unlisted-model warning — *needs a call: whether approval-gated specialist models belong in the table at all, given no published effort list and no way to verify a value*
- [openai/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/stt/2026-11-23.md) — OpenAISTTService does not use the Audio API's streaming transcription responses (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — the speech-to-text guide still lists stream=true for gpt-transcribe, emitting transcript.text.delta and a final transcript.text.done; the service awaits the whole response, so a segment's ~916 ms TTFB is all dead time. OpenAIRealtimeSTTService already covers users who want streaming partials — *needs a call: whether SegmentedSTTService should push InterimTranscriptionFrame frames from a streamed Audio API response, which is more than a Settings field*
- [openai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Default voice is alloy while OpenAI's guide recommends marin or cedar (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both are already in the allowlist and marin probed fine in an earlier run; alloy is not deprecated — *needs a call: a call on changing what every existing OpenAI TTS user hears by default*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `plugins` is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — a pass-through array covering auto-router, auto-beta-router, pareto-router, switchyard-router, jev-router, moderation, web, web-fetch, file-parser, response-healing, context-compression, fusion, alignment and armor. Within it `cost_quality_tradeoff` stays deprecated in favour of `cost_tier` — *needs a call: whether a plugin list belongs in a voice pipeline at all, or stays an `extra` escape-hatch feature*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — OpenRouter's caching and sticky-routing fields are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — `prompt_cache_options` (`mode: explicit` plus a `ttl`), `prompt_cache_key`, `cache_control` and `session_id` bear on cost and latency as a call's context grows; all reach the API today only through `extra={"extra_body": {...}}` — *needs a call: whether prompt caching is worth first-class fields here rather than in the OpenAI base settings all compatible services share*
- [openrouter/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/openrouter/llm/2026-11-23.md) — A docs page example model slug is not in OpenRouter's catalogue (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the page's "With a Different Provider Model" example names `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`, which `GET /models` does not list -- OpenRouter's oldest Sonnet is `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5` — *needs a call: the page lives in pipecat-ai/docs, so it cannot be fixed from this repo*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-23.md) — The `web_search` tool Pipecat attaches is not configurable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `PerplexityAgentLLMSettings.web_search` is a bool, and `_build_response_params` appends a bare `{"type": "web_search"}`. The tool documents `search_type`, `max_results`, `max_tokens`, `max_tokens_per_page`, a `filters` object (domain, recency, published-before/after, last-updated-before/after) and `user_location`, none reachable by name. Dropped from medium because the cost question is now answered: the web-search tool page states that the `fast`, `low`, `medium`, `high` and `xhigh` effort levels set `search_type: "fast"` themselves, so the service's default configuration is already on the $1.00-per-1k path, not the $2.50 `web` one. What is left is ergonomics, and there is an escape hatch — `extra={"tools": [...]}` is merged before the append, and a `web_search` entry already there is left alone. — *needs a call: whether `web_search` should grow into a bool-or-dict (or gain a sibling field) at all, now that the one setting with a price attached is already right by default*
- [perplexity/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/perplexity/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Class docstring still tells users Anthropic models need `max_completion_tokens` set — `PerplexityAgentHttpLLMService`'s docstring ends with "Anthropic models need ``max_completion_tokens`` set." Perplexity lifted that requirement in November 2026: `max_output_tokens` — what the base class maps `max_completion_tokens` to in `openai/responses/llm.py` — is now optional, with each generation allowed up to 128,000 tokens, and the API reference marks `input` as the only required field. Probed today, `anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5` streams a full answer through the service with no token budget set. — *needs a call: whether to delete the sentence outright or qualify it, since the changelog ties the relaxation to the `2026-11-11.0` effort versions and a user pinning an older version (`effort=\"fast@2026-10-16.0\"`) may still need the budget*
- [piper/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/piper/tts/2026-11-23.md) — SynthesisConfig's volume and normalize_audio are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — both are in-process only; Piper's /synthesize body has no equivalent — *needs a call: exposing them would make the two Piper settings classes asymmetric — a call on whether that is acceptable*
- [piper/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/piper/tts/2026-11-23.md) — The piper extra pins requests, which piper-tts no longer uses (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — piper-tts 1.8.0 declares no requests dependency in any extra; nothing under piper/ imports it, and download_voices uses urllib — *needs a call: confirm nothing else in the Piper path needs requests before dropping the pin*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-23.md) — An unsupported language fails at construction with a FileNotFoundError (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `language_to_pocket_tts_language(Language.JA)` returns `"ja"`, which `load_model` turns into a missing `ja.yaml` — *needs a call: needs a call on whether to raise an error naming the supported languages or to fall back to English*
- [pocket_tts/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/pocket_tts/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Generation parameters other than temp and quantize are not reachable (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — `load_model` also takes `noise_clamp`, `eos_threshold` and the decode-step count, and `generate_audio_stream` takes `max_tokens` and `frames_after_eos`; `temp` is constructor-only, so it cannot change at runtime — *needs a call: these are load-time model parameters, so exposing them means deciding whether they belong in Settings at all*
- [qwen/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/qwen/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Two examples pin qwen3.6-27b (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — examples/function-calling/function-calling-qwen.py and examples/update-settings/llm/llm-qwen.py both pin qwen3.6-27b; the model still serves and is on no retirement list, but it reasons by default — 2418 ms of thinking on "What is the capital of France?", TTFAT 3628 ms. aiewf-eval still scores qwen3.8-27b 96.0% against qwen3.6-27b 92.1% on aiwf_medium_context, both thinking off — *needs a call: whether examples track the newest dense model, and it carries the same thinking-off question*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `voice_settings_preset_uuid` (pace, pitch, temperature, exaggeration, useHd, style description) is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — unchanged - the WebSocket reference still omits the field while the endpoint reads it (an unowned but well-formed preset UUID returns a 401 naming "invalid voice settings preset"); building new request knobs for a product the provider has stopped selling is hygiene rather than a capability users will ask for — *needs a call: a preset UUID in the probe account to confirm a valid one synthesizes, plus a stance on presets overriding any SSML a user already puts in the text*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `project_uuid` is documented as required but never sent (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — Resemble's OpenAPI spec marks `project_uuid` optional on both HTTP synthesis endpoints, so the WebSocket page's "required" is a doc inconsistency rather than a request Resemble will start rejecting; synthesis succeeds without it (probe below) and clips land in the account's default project, so what is missing is the ability to choose one — *needs a call: a call on whether to expose the project at all, and a project UUID to probe with - none is available in the probe environment*
- [resembleai/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/resembleai/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Non-default `precision` and `output_format` values are advertised but the frames are built as 16-bit PCM (since 2026-09-01, 11 weeks) — the constructor documents PCM_32/PCM_24/MULAW and mp3, but `_process_messages` aligns chunks to 2 bytes and emits `TTSAudioRawFrame` at `self.sample_rate`, which downstream treats as PCM16 — *needs a call: confirmation of what a MULAW or mp3 session actually sounds like, then either wire the format through or narrow the documented values*
- [rime/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Prism's push-to-talk mode (`turn_detection: null`) cannot be selected — Prism supports turning its own turn detection off and letting the client end turns with `input_audio_buffer.commit` / `prism.input_audio_buffer.start`. `Realtime.connect` always sends a `turn_detection` object and the SDK session exposes no commit, so neither the SDK nor the service can express it; the service also rejects anything but SDK server turn detection. — *needs a call: the limitation is upstream in `rimelabs-sdk`, so this needs an SDK change before Pipecat could add a turn strategy that commits*
- [rime/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — Prism's history truncation is never surfaced — Prism defaults to `truncation=auto`, which cuts history back to 80% of the window, and reports what it dropped in `response.done` (`ResponseTruncation.items_left_out`, `first_kept_item_id`). The service ignores the field, so a long call silently leaves the local `LLMContext` holding turns Prism no longer sees. — *needs a call: a call on whether dropped turns should reach the application (event or response metadata) and whether the local context should follow Prism's window*
- [rime/realtime](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/realtime/2026-11-23.md) — `rimelabs-sdk` pin is one alpha behind (`==0.1.0a31`, latest 0.1.0a32) — 0.1.0a32 (2026-11-17) adds exactly one field, `SpeechStopped.end_of_turn_min_silence_ms`, which the service does not read — though `on_rime_event` hands raw SDK events to applications, so an app wanting it needs the newer SDK. Diff of the two sdists shows no other realtime change. — *needs a call: the exact pin is shared with rime/tts and rime/stt and Rime advises pinning the alpha you tested against, so bumping it means re-probing all three units*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Dutch is missing from both Rime language maps (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Rime lists `nl` among Coda's ten languages; `language_to_rime_language` and `language_to_rime_v1_language` have no `Language.NL` entry, so Dutch resolves through the unverified base-code fallback with a warning. — *needs a call: the legacy map holds ISO 639-2 codes while Rime's canonical form is now BCP-47, so adding Dutch needs a choice of code form for that map*
- [rime/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/rime/tts/2026-11-23.md) — A RimeTTSService built without a voice fails at connect with an opaque HTTP 400 — `voice` defaults to None and `_build_ws_params` puts it in the query string regardless, so `/ws3` closes the connection with `Invalid speaker: none`; the legacy HTTP path answers `speaker is required`. Omitting the parameter entirely also 400s on `/ws3`, so only Rime's newer `coda.api.rime.ai` host has a random-voice default. — *needs a call: a call on whether an unset voice should raise at construction or pick a documented default voice*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — Three Provisioned Throughput models stay out of the allowlist (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `deepseekv4-pro`, `kimi-k3.1-pro` and `qwen38-27b` are in the /v2 chat enum but each answers 400 "This model runs on Provisioned Throughput and is not enabled for your organisation" on our key, so none can be exercised through the class. `kimi-k3`, `mimo-v2.6-flash` and `mimo-v2.6-pro` remain in the catalogue but were withdrawn on 2026-11-06 and are absent from the chat enum, so they are not candidates. — *needs a call: a Provisioned Throughput account, or a decision to admit models we cannot probe*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `_SUPPORTED_MODELS` still admits `deepseekv4.1-flash`, a name Sarvam no longer lists (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — The name is gone from `GET /v2/models` and from the /v2 chat enum, and PR — *needs a call: whether to drop an undocumented name that currently works, which would break user code that passes it, or leave it until Sarvam stops serving it*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `x-session-affinity` has no first-class home on the service (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Documented in the /v2 chat reference as an optional header on every model: one opaque ID per conversation enables routing and prompt-cache reuse on later turns. A caller can already pass it as `default_headers={"x-session-affinity": ...}` at construction, which fits Pipecat's one-service-per-session shape. — *needs a call: whether the existing `default_headers` route is enough, or the service should mint and send a session ID itself*
- [sarvam/llm](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/llm/2026-11-23.md) — `sarvamai` is pinned to ==0.1.28 while 0.1.49 is current (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The pin is on the shared sarvam extra; the LLM service itself talks to Sarvam through the openai SDK. Sarvam's 2026-11-06 Instant Voice Cloning change names 0.1.45 as its floor, which touches the voice services rather than this one. — *needs a call: coordinating with the Sarvam STT/TTS services that actually import the SDK*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — sarvamai pinned to ==0.1.28, twenty-one releases behind 0.1.49 (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the STT-facing distance is unchanged from the 0.1.45 comparison: `speech_to_text.transcribe()` gained `keyterms` and `with_timestamps`, `speech_to_text_streaming.connect()` gained `keyterms`, and `speech_to_text_realtime_streaming` does not exist at 0.1.28. `SarvamHttpSTTService` and `SarvamSTTService` send `keyterms` by hand through `request_options`, so nothing is unreachable. No new Saaras model string appears anywhere in the 0.1.49 wheel — *needs a call: whether the exact SDK pin moves now that the parameters our classes hand-roll are first-class in the current release*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — sarvamai ships a first-party realtime STT client we do not use (since 2026-09-14, 10 weeks) — `sarvamai.speech_to_text_realtime_streaming` wraps the endpoint `SarvamRealtimeSTTService` speaks over raw websockets; its 0.1.49 connect signature still matches `_query_params()` field for field (language_code, model, stream_type, mode, prompt, keyterms, endpointing, encoding, sample_rate, return_timestamps, threshold, prefix_padding_ms, silence_duration_ms, min_speech_duration_ms), so adopting it buys protocol upkeep rather than capability — *needs a call: whether the hand-rolled realtime websocket is worth replacing with the SDK client, given the pin would have to move too*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `with_timestamps` is not reachable from SarvamHttpSTTService (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — Sarvam's REST reference documents `with_timestamps`, which adds chunk-level (sentence/phrase) offsets; word-level offsets are explicitly unsupported. `SarvamHttpSTTSettings` is empty and `_transcribe()` never sends it, while the realtime class exposes the equivalent `return_timestamps`. The offsets would ride along in the frame's `result`, since `run_stt()` already attaches the raw response — *needs a call: whether chunk offsets mean anything for a segment longer than 30 seconds, which `_split_wav()` cuts into pieces whose offsets each restart at zero*
- [sarvam/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Examples and a code comment still present saaras:v3 as a live choice — `examples/function-calling/function-calling-sarvam.py` and `examples/update-settings/llm/llm-sarvam.py` both override the class default with `model="saaras:v3"`, and the comment above `_SUPPORTED_REALTIME_MODELS` calls `saaras:v3-realtime` "beta-gated". Sarvam now says "Always set `saaras:v4`; Saaras v3 is legacy" and calls `saaras:v3-realtime` "a legacy model that is still accepted for existing integrations". Both strings still work: `saaras:v3-realtime` probed green this run — *needs a call: whether the update-settings example pins a non-default model deliberately, to show a setting taking effect, before the examples are moved to saaras:v4*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `SarvamTTSSpeakerV3` is out of step with Sarvam's v3 voice list (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — it names `amelia` and `sophia`, which the API rejects, and omits the 14 voices anand, tanya, tarun, sunny, mani, gokul, vijay, shruti, suhani, mohit, kavitha, rehan, soham, rupali that the Bulbul page's 37-name list carries; `voice` is a free string so nothing is gated, but the enum also feeds the public `get_speakers_for_model()` — *needs a call: removing two members from a public StrEnum — decide whether that needs a deprecation cycle*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `bulbul:v3-beta` is still a SarvamTTSModel member and a TTS_MODEL_CONFIGS key (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Sarvam promoted the tag to `bulbul:v3` in February 2026 and its docs no longer list it; the endpoint still synthesized for it on 2026-10-28, so nothing breaks today — *needs a call: whether to deprecate the enum member and table entry or leave the pre-GA tag working*
- [sarvam/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/sarvam/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `SarvamHttpTTSService`'s `sample_rate` docstring attributes 32000/44100/48000 to bulbul:v3 — the docstring reads "8000, 16000, 22050, 24000, and, for bulbul:v3, 32000, 44100, 48000", while Sarvam's Bulbul page says the three high rates are accepted on REST for every Bulbul model and refused on streaming for all of them — the split is by transport, not by model, so a v4-flash REST user is told not to ask for 48 kHz — *needs a call: an unverified doc claim — a single REST call at 48000 on v4-flash would settle it before rewording*

- [simli/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/simli/video/2026-11-23.md) — Transport and retry options are fixed (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `enableSFU=True` is hard-coded and `transport_mode` (P2P or LIVEKIT) and `retry_count` are not exposed; only `simli_url` can be varied (`retry_timeout` is moot — simli-ai 2.1 deprecated and ignores it) — *needs a call: whether anyone wants the LiveKit transport from Pipecat, given Pipecat already owns the WebRTC leg*

- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `numerals=auto` is sent on every connection but is not in Pulse's parameter reference (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — The AsyncAPI bindings for GET /waves/v1/stt/live still list no `numerals` parameter, and pulse-2's own parameter table does not have one either; `itn_normalize`, already in Settings, is the documented way to get written-form numbers. - [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `punctuate` and `capitalize` are not reachable from Settings (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Both default to true in the AsyncAPI bindings and split what the single `format` field controls today; the feature pages and the pulse-2 card still document `format` as the only formatting switch. — *needs a call: decide whether the finer-grained pair is worth two fields next to `format`, which overlaps them and which the docs still present as the one formatting switch*
- [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — `external_session_id` is not reachable from Settings (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — Documented on the pulse-2 card as a client-supplied correlation id, not echoed back in responses. - [smallest/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Keepalive sends billed silent audio where Smallest documents a free `ping` message (since 2026-10-28, 3 weeks) — The service enables the base keepalive (`keepalive_timeout=10`) and inherits `WebsocketSTTService._send_keepalive`, which sends 100 ms of PCM silence and records it as STT audio usage. The keep-alive page and the AsyncAPI `sendKeepAlive` operation document `{"type":"ping"}` (aliases `keepalive`, `keep_alive`, `keep-alive`) answered with `pong`, explicitly not billed, resetting the 20-minute inactivity timer. The same page documents a `timeout` URL parameter raising that window to 3 hours, which the service does not expose either. — *needs a call: overriding `_send_keepalive` changes what the service bills and sends on an idle socket; decide whether `ping` should replace the silence, given silence also resets the separate 20-minute model-session idle timer that `ping` does not*

- [smallest/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/smallest/tts/2026-11-23.md) — `auto` language routing is not reachable through the Language enum (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — the language enum still lists `auto` first, for per-request detection and cross-language routing on any English or Hindi voice; users can only get it by passing the raw string, since Language has no AUTO member — *needs a call: whether to add a Language.AUTO member or document the raw-string escape hatch is a framework-wide call, not a Smallest one*

- [soniox/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/soniox/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Soniox caps output at 2 minutes per stream, and the rest of a longer turn is dropped silently (since 2026-10-12, 6 weeks) — one turn context maps to one Soniox stream_id, and the limits page still states the 2-minute stream duration as fixed and not raisable; a turn that passes it gets a 413 `max_audio_duration_reached` and a `terminated`, after which the receive loop removes the audio context and later sentences of the same turn re-open the stream but have their audio discarded — *needs a call: whether a turn should roll onto a fresh stream_id at the cap (Soniox's own advice is to split the text across requests) or whether the limit should just be documented; not probed, since confirming it means synthesizing two minutes of audio*

- [speechmatics/stt](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/stt/2026-11-23.md) — Language and output-locale maps are still behind the provider's list (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — `BASE_LANGUAGES` omits `ia` and the bilingual packs `ar_en`, `en_ms`, `cmn_en`, `cmn_en_ms_ta`, `en_ta`, all re-confirmed on the provider's language page this run and one of which (`ar_en`) Agent STT accepted on the wire in an earlier run. `LOCALES` covers only en-GB/en-US/en-AU, so Mandarin's Traditional/Simplified output locales are unreachable too. `resolve_language(use_base_code=True)` lets an unmapped code through with a warning, so these are reachable as raw strings rather than blocked. — *needs a call: none of these codes has a `Language` enum member, so closing the gap means adding enum members, not table rows*

Re-confirmed this run — the stock harness returned 13 interim transcripts and "no TranscriptionFrame within 25s", while the identical probe with the frames paced at real time returned the full final. Every passing probe in this report used a paced copy. `_run_pipeline` already sleeps on a float in the frame list, so the change is a couple of lines. — *needs a call: whether to pace STT probe audio in the harness, or leave it and have every Speechmatics run re-diagnose the false failure*

- [speechmatics/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/tts/2026-11-23.md) — TTS is still served from the preview host `preview.tts.speechmatics.com` (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — Speechmatics now lists TTS on its public pricing page at $0.011 per 1k characters with the first 1M free, but the quickstart still calls the service a free preview with stale 2025 pricing dates, the provider's own 0.2.0 TTS client still defaults to the preview host, no GA hostname is named anywhere, and the changelog has never carried a TTS entry — *needs a call: nothing to do until Speechmatics names a stable hostname — re-check each run*
- [speechmatics/tts](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/speechmatics/tts/2026-11-23.md) — Speechmatics' own Pipecat TTS page documents the deprecated `voice_id=` argument (since 2026-08-31, 12 weeks) — docs.speechmatics.com/integrations-and-sdks/pipecat/tts still shows `voice_id="sarah"` and never mentions `Settings` — *needs a call: the page lives in Speechmatics' docs repo; someone has to raise it with them*
- [tavus/video](https://github.com/pipecat-ai/provider-watch-reports/blob/main/reports/tavus/video/2026-11-23.md) — The emotion path is silent about the faces it needs — Tavus scopes expression to Phoenix-4/4.5, Pro faces preferred (since 2026-11-09, 2 weeks) — `TavusTransportClient.update_emotion` sends `conversation.set_emotion` for any face, and `tavus_policy_warning` only warns about deliveries the mapping has no emotion for.
